Notably, we do not need any additional constraints on the coefficients
\{f_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}}\}
, such as the repulsive interactions.

Remark.
The assumption is automatically satisfied for the
\phi 4
Hamiltonian (
S.28
).
Hence, only the gap condition excludes the possibility of the boson concentration on a single site.
By imposing a similar assumption to Assumption
1
for
\bm{\phi}
operators, we will be able to extend the theory to systems with parity violation.
Throughout the paper, we only consider the case of
k\geq 2
in Eq. (
S.31
), which gives non-trivial behaviours.

We stress that Assumption 2 (parity symmetry) is essential for our area-law proof; without it, the area law can fail even in gapped, local bosonic systems.
If odd powers of
\phi
are allowed, the potential need not be centered at the origin and may instead develop a minimum at large field values.
In such a situation, bosonic degrees of freedom can macroscopically condense around this minimum, leading to a large expectation value of the local boson number.
This condensation strongly induces anisotropic fluctuations among collective modes and can generate arbitrarily large bipartite entanglement, thereby obstructing any uniform entanglement bound.

To illustrate the role of parity symmetry explicitly, we consider the following two-site bosonic model whose Hamiltonian contains odd powers of
\phi
:

\displaystyle H=\pi_{1}^{2}+\pi_{2}^{2}+\left(\epsilon\phi_{1}^{4}-\phi_{1}^{3}\right)+\left(\epsilon\phi_{2}^{4}-\phi_{2}^{3}\right)+(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})^{2}(\phi_{1}^{2}+\phi_{2}^{2}),\qquad\epsilon>0.

(S.32)

As before, the canonical variables are defined as

\displaystyle\phi_{i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(b_{i}+b_{i}^{\dagger}\right),\qquad\pi_{i}=\frac{-i}{\sqrt{2}}\left(b_{i}-b_{i}^{\dagger}\right),\qquad[\phi_{i},\pi_{j}]=i\delta_{ij},\qquad(i,j\in\{1,2\}).

(S.33)

The onsite potential

\displaystyle V(\phi)=\epsilon\phi^{4}-\phi^{3}

(S.34)

explicitly breaks the parity symmetry
\phi\to-\phi
and admits a nontrivial classical minimum determined by

\displaystyle V^{\prime}(\phi)=\phi^{2}(4\epsilon\phi-3)=0,

(S.35)

namely

\displaystyle\phi_{0}=\frac{3}{4\epsilon}.

(S.36)

As
\epsilon
decreases, the classical minimum
\phi_{0}
shifts to larger field values, and bosonic degrees of freedom concentrate around this configuration.

Writing
\phi_{i}=\phi_{0}+x_{i}
and expanding the Hamiltonian to quadratic order in the fluctuations
x_{i}
, one finds

\displaystyle H=E_{\mathrm{cl}}(\epsilon)+\pi_{1}^{2}+\pi_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}V^{\prime\prime}(\phi_{0})(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})+2\phi_{0}^{2}(x_{1}-x_{2})^{2}+\mathcal{O}(x^{3}),

(S.37)

with

\displaystyle V^{\prime\prime}(\phi_{0})=\frac{9}{4\epsilon},\qquad E_{\mathrm{cl}}(\epsilon)=-\frac{27}{128\epsilon^{3}}.

(S.38)

By defining symmetric and antisymmetric normal modes,
x_{\pm}=\frac{x_{1}\pm x_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}
and
p_{\pm}=\frac{\pi_{1}\pm\pi_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}
, we can rewrite the quadratic Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle H=E_{\mathrm{cl}}(\epsilon)+\left(p_{+}^{2}+\Omega_{+}^{2}x_{+}^{2}\right)+\left(p_{-}^{2}+\Omega_{-}^{2}x_{-}^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}(x^{3}),

(S.39)

where the normal-mode frequencies are

\displaystyle\Omega_{+}^{2}=\frac{9}{8\epsilon},\qquad\Omega_{-}^{2}=\frac{9(\epsilon+2)}{8\epsilon^{2}}.

(S.40)

As
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
, these frequencies scale as

\displaystyle\Omega_{+}\sim\epsilon^{-1/2},\qquad\Omega_{-}\sim\epsilon^{-1},

(S.41)

showing a parametrically large separation of energy scales.

This separation controls the low-energy properties of the system.
At quadratic order, the ground-state energy is

\displaystyle E_{0}(\epsilon)\simeq E_{\mathrm{cl}}(\epsilon)+\Omega_{+}(\epsilon)+\Omega_{-}(\epsilon)=-\frac{27}{128\epsilon^{3}}+\sqrt{\frac{9}{8\epsilon}}+\sqrt{\frac{9(\epsilon+2)}{8\epsilon^{2}}},

(S.42)

while the lowest excitation gap is set by the softer symmetric mode,

\displaystyle\Delta(\epsilon)\simeq 2\Omega_{+}(\epsilon)=\frac{3}{\sqrt{2\epsilon}},

(S.43)

so the system remains gapped even for small
\epsilon
.
Finally, the same mode anisotropy determines the bipartite entanglement structure.
Within the leading quadratic theory, tracing out one site yields a symplectic eigenvalue

\displaystyle\nu=\frac{1}{4}\sqrt{2+\frac{\Omega_{-}}{\Omega_{+}}+\frac{\Omega_{+}}{\Omega_{-}}}\sim\frac{1}{4}\sqrt{\frac{\Omega_{-}}{\Omega_{+}}}\sim\epsilon^{-1/4},

(S.44)

which implies a logarithmically growing bipartite entanglement entropy between the two sites
[
61
]
:

\displaystyle S(\rho_{1})=\left(\nu+\frac{1}{2}\right)\log\left(\nu+\frac{1}{2}\right)-\left(\nu-\frac{1}{2}\right)\log\left(\nu-\frac{1}{2}\right)\sim\frac{1}{4}\log\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)+\mathcal{O}(1).

(S.45)

The analytical predictions above are supported by direct numerical simulations of the Hamiltonian (
S.32
); see Supplementary Figure
1
.

This demonstrates explicitly that allowing odd-power terms enables boson concentration at large-field minima, leading to parametrically large entanglement even in a local and gapped system.
Parity symmetry excludes this mechanism by forbidding such terms and is therefore essential for establishing an entanglement area law.

Supplementary Figure 1:
Numerical results for the two-site bosonic Hamiltonian in Eq. (
S.32
). The Hamiltonian is diagonalized in a displaced harmonic-oscillator basis defined around the classical minimum of the onsite potential, with the local Hilbert space at each site truncated to dimension
N_{\mathrm{trunc}}=200
.
Panel
\mathbf{a}
shows the ground-state energy, and panel
\mathbf{b}
shows the spectral gap between the first excited state and the ground state; in both cases, solid black lines indicate the leading-order predictions from the quadratic expansion around the classical minimum.
Panel
\mathbf{c}
shows the bipartite entanglement entropy between site 1 and site 2; the solid line represents a linear fit to the numerical data, exhibiting a logarithmic scaling with a slope close to
1/4
, as predicted by Eq. (
S.45
).

For the convenience of our analyses, we define the parameter
\bar{f}
as

\displaystyle\bar{f}=\max_{i\in\Lambda}\left(\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k/2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}\in\Lambda\\
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}\}\ni i\end{subarray}}\left|f_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}}\right|\right).

(S.46)

Supplementary Note 3
Maximum moment bounds for Bose-Hubbard class

In this section, we mainly treat the Bose-Hubbard class and aim to derive an upper bound for the
(k/2)
th order moment.
The result in this section plays a key role in proving the concentration bound on the boson number distribution in the subsequent section (
Supplementary Note 4
).
A schematic overview of the proof is presented in Supplementary Figure
2
.
In the following, we often separately consider the cases of i) the total boson number is not conserved and ii) the total boson number is conserved.
In case (ii), we fix the total boson number to be
N
and make the ground state dependent on the boson number.
The ground state and the ground energy are described by
|\Omega_{N}\rangle
and
E_{0}(N)
, respectively.

Supplementary Figure 2:
This figure outlines the proof of Theorem 1, which establishes the exponential decay of the boson-number distribution for Bose–Hubbard class Hamiltonians.

Supplementary Note 3.1
Preliminary

In this section, we show several essential ingredients for the proof.

We first prove that the ground energy of the subset Hamiltonian
H_{X}
is no smaller than that of
H_{\Lambda}
.
In general, we prove the following lemma:

Lemma 3
.

For arbitrary two subsets
X
and
\bar{X}
such that
X\subseteq\bar{X}
, we have

\displaystyle E_{0,X}\geq E_{0,\bar{X}},

(S.47)

where
E_{0,X}
is the ground energy of
H_{X}
.

In the case where the Hamiltonian conserves the total boson number, we consider the
N
dependence of the ground energy
E_{0,X}(N)
for the subset Hamiltonian
H_{X}
(
X\subseteq\Lambda
) with the total boson number equal to
N
.
We obtain

\displaystyle E_{0,X}(N)\geq E_{0,\bar{X}}(N).

(S.48)

Proof of Lemma
3
.

We consider the case where the boson number is conserved, but the same proof applies to the case where it is not.
The proof is immediately followed by considering the quantum state as
|\Phi\rangle_{\bar{X}}=|\Omega_{N}\rangle_{X}\otimes|0\rangle_{\bar{X}\setminus X}
, where
|\Omega_{N}\rangle_{X}
is the ground state of
H_{X}
.
We then obtain

\displaystyle\langle\Phi|H_{\bar{X}}|\Phi\rangle_{\bar{X}}=\langle\Omega_{N}|H_{X}|\Omega_{N}\rangle_{X}=E_{0,X}(N).

(S.49)

On the other hand, we always obtain

\displaystyle\langle\Phi|H_{\bar{X}}|\Phi\rangle_{\bar{X}}\geq\langle\Omega_{N}|H_{\bar{X}}|\Omega_{N}\rangle_{\bar{X}}=E_{0,\bar{X}}(N).

(S.50)

By combining the above two inequalities, we obtain the main inequality (
S.48
).
This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
3
]

The second proposition plays an essential role in analyzing the Hamiltonian
H_{0}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})
.

Proposition 4
.

Let
|\psi\rangle
be an arbitrary quantum state.
We also consider the operators of

\displaystyle\overline{H_{0,i}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\in\Lambda\\
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\}\ni i\end{subarray}}\sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}+1}\left|J^{(s)}_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}}\right|\cdot\left|b_{i_{k_{1}}}^{\dagger}\cdots b_{i_{s+1}}^{\dagger}b_{i_{s}}^{\dagger}b_{i_{s-1}}\cdots b_{i_{2}}b_{i_{1}}\right|,

(S.51)

and

\displaystyle\widehat{V_{+,i}}(\mathbf{n})=\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k/2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\in\Lambda\\
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\}\ni i\end{subarray}}V_{+,i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}}\hat{n}_{i_{1}}\hat{n}_{i_{2}}\cdots\hat{n}_{i_{k_{1}}},

(S.52)

where
\left|O\right|
is defined by
\sqrt{O^{\dagger}O}
for an arbitrary operator.
Then, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\psi|\overline{H_{0,i}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\psi\rangle\leq\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\left[\bar{J}_{i,k}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\psi\rangle+\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\left(\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{(k-1)/2}|\psi\rangle+1\right)\right],

(S.53)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\psi|\widehat{V_{+,i}}(\mathbf{n})|\psi\rangle\leq\bar{v}\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\psi\rangle,

(S.54)

where we have defined
\bar{v}
in Eq. (
S.23
).
Also, we define the parameter
\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}:=\max_{i\in\Lambda}\left\{\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k}\bar{J}_{i,k_{1}}\left[1+(2k_{1})^{k_{1}}\right]\right\}.

(S.55)

Remark.
The operator
\overline{H_{0,i}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})
satisfies the operator inequality as

\displaystyle\widehat{H_{0,i}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})\preceq\overline{H_{0,i}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger}),

(S.56)

where we remind that the notation
\widehat{H_{0,i}}
has been defined in Eq. (
S.11
).
Also, the inequality (
S.53
) implies the operator inequality as

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\overline{H_{0,i}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})\preceq\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\left[\bar{J}_{i,k}\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}+\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\left(\hat{n}_{i}^{(k-1)/2}+1\right)\right].

(S.57)

This is helpful to connect the Hamiltonian
H_{0}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})
with the moment functions with respect to the boson number operators.

Supplementary Note 3.1.1
Proof of Proposition
4

We first upper-bound the expectation
\langle\psi|\widehat{V_{+,i}}(\mathbf{n})|\psi\rangle
.
Using the Hölder inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i_{1}}\hat{n}_{i_{2}}\cdots\hat{n}_{i_{k_{1}}}|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{j=1}^{k_{1}}\left(\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i_{j}}^{k_{1}}|\psi\rangle\right)^{1/k_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i_{j}}^{k_{1}}|\psi\rangle.

(S.58)

Using the above inequality, we can derive

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\psi|\widehat{V_{+,i}}(\mathbf{n})|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k/2}\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\in\Lambda\\
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\}\ni i\end{subarray}}\frac{V_{+,i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}}}{k_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i_{j}}^{k_{1}}|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k/2}\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\in\Lambda\\
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\}\ni i\end{subarray}}V_{+,i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{k_{1}}|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k/2}\bar{v}_{k_{1}}\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\psi\rangle,

(S.59)

where we use the definition (
S.23
) for
\bar{v}_{k_{1}}
.
Note that
\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{p}|\psi\rangle\leq\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{p^{\prime}}|\psi\rangle
for
p\leq p^{\prime}
.
We thus prove one of the main inequalities, i.e., (
S.54
).

For the proof of the inequality (
S.53
), we utilize the following basic lemma (the proof is given in
Supplementary Note 3.1.2
.):

Lemma 5
.

For an arbitrary multiset of
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k}\}
, we obtain the following operator inequality:

\displaystyle\left|b_{i_{k}}^{\dagger}\cdots b_{i_{s+1}}^{\dagger}b_{i_{s}}^{\dagger}b_{i_{s-1}}\cdots b_{i_{2}}b_{i_{1}}\right|\preceq\prod_{j=1}^{k}(\hat{n}_{i_{j}}+k)^{1/2}.

(S.60)

Also, for an arbitrary quantum state
|\psi\rangle
, we derive

\displaystyle\left|\langle\psi|b_{i_{k}}^{\dagger}\cdots b_{i_{s+1}}^{\dagger}b_{i_{s}}^{\dagger}b_{i_{s-1}}\cdots b_{i_{2}}b_{i_{1}}|\psi\rangle\right|\leq\frac{1}{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i_{j}}^{k/2}|\psi\rangle+\frac{(2k)^{k}}{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i_{j}}^{k/2-1}|\psi\rangle+k^{k/2},

(S.61)

By applying the above lemma to the definition of Eq. (
S.51
) for
\overline{H_{0,i}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\psi|\overline{H_{0,i}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\in\Lambda\\
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\}\ni i\end{subarray}}\sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}+1}\left|J^{(s)}_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}}\right|\left[\frac{1}{k_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i_{j}}^{k_{1}/2}|\psi\rangle+\frac{(2k_{1})^{k_{1}}}{k_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i_{j}}^{k_{1}/2-1}|\psi\rangle+k_{1}^{k_{1}/2}\right].

(S.62)

To further upper-bound the RHS of the above inequality, we first use a similar inequality to (
S.59
) and derive the following three inequalities:

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\in\Lambda\\
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\}\ni i\end{subarray}}\sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}+1}\left|J^{(s)}_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}}\right|\frac{1}{k_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i_{j}}^{k_{1}/2}|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\in\Lambda\\
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\}\ni i\end{subarray}}\sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}+1}\left|J^{(s)}_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}}\right|\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{k_{1}/2}|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\bar{J}_{i,k_{1}}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{k_{1}/2}|\psi\rangle,

(S.63)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\in\Lambda\\
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\}\ni i\end{subarray}}\sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}+1}\left|J^{(s)}_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}}\right|\frac{(2k_{1})^{k_{1}}}{k_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i_{j}}^{k_{1}/2-1}|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\Lambda}(2k_{1})^{k_{1}}\bar{J}_{i,k_{1}}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{k_{1}/2-1}|\psi\rangle,

(S.64)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\in\Lambda\\
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\}\ni i\end{subarray}}\sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}+1}\left|J^{(s)}_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}}\right|k_{1}^{k_{1}/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\bar{J}_{i,k_{1}}k_{1}^{k_{1}/2},

(S.65)

where the parameter
\bar{J}_{i,k_{1}}
was defined in Eq. (
S.20
).

By combining all the above inequalities, we reach the desired upper bound (
S.53
) as follows:

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\psi|\overline{H_{0,i}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k}\bar{J}_{i,k_{1}}\left(\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{k_{1}/2}|\psi\rangle+(2k_{1})^{k_{1}}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{k_{1}/2-1}|\psi\rangle+k_{1}^{k_{1}/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\left\{\bar{J}_{i,k}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\psi\rangle+\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k}\bar{J}_{i,k_{1}}\left[1+(2k_{1})^{k_{1}}\right]\left(\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{(k-1)/2}|\psi\rangle+1\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\left[\bar{J}_{i,k}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\psi\rangle+\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\left(\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{(k-1)/2}|\psi\rangle+1\right)\right],

(S.66)

where we use the definition (
S.55
) of
\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}
, and the second inequality is derived from
\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{p}|\psi\rangle\leq\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i}^{p^{\prime}}|\psi\rangle
for
p\leq p^{\prime}
.
This completes the proof of Proposition
4
.
\square

Supplementary Note 3.1.2
Proof of Lemma
5
.

We denote
\hat{B}_{k}
by

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{k}=b_{i_{k}}^{\dagger}\cdots b_{i_{s+1}}^{\dagger}b_{i_{s}}^{\dagger}b_{i_{s-1}}\cdots b_{i_{2}}b_{i_{1}}.

(S.67)

Because of

\displaystyle\left[\hat{n}_{i},\hat{B}^{\dagger}_{k}\hat{B}_{k}\right]=0\quad\textrm{for}\quad\forall i\in\Lambda,

(S.68)

we can describe

\displaystyle|\hat{B}_{k}|^{2}=\hat{B}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{B}_{k}=f_{k}(\mathbf{n}),

(S.69)

where
f_{k}(\mathbf{n})
is an appropriate function with respect to
\mathbf{n}=\{\hat{n}_{i}\}_{i\in\Lambda}
.
Our purpose is to prove

\displaystyle f_{k}(\mathbf{n})\preceq\prod_{j=1}^{k}(\hat{n}_{i_{j}}+k),

(S.70)

which also implies the inequality (
S.60
) by taking the square root of the both sides.

For
k=1
, the inequality (
S.70
) trivially holds from
b_{i}b_{i}^{\dagger}=n_{i}+1
and
b_{i}^{\dagger}b_{i}=\hat{n}_{i}
.
We assume the target inequality up to a certain
k
and prove the case of
k+1
.
We then generally consider the operators of

\displaystyle b_{i}^{\dagger}\hat{B}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{B}_{k}b_{i},\quad b_{i}\hat{B}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{B}_{k}b_{i}^{\dagger}

(S.71)

for
\forall i\in\Lambda
.
By using the assumption, we obtain

\displaystyle b_{i}^{\dagger}\hat{B}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{B}_{k}b_{i}\preceq b_{i}^{\dagger}\prod_{s=1}^{k}(\hat{n}_{i_{s}}+k)b_{i},\quad b_{i}\hat{B}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{B}_{k}b_{i}^{\dagger}\preceq b_{i}\prod_{s=1}^{k}(\hat{n}_{i_{s}}+k)b_{i}^{\dagger}.

(S.72)

For an arbitrary integer
m
, we have

\displaystyle b_{i}^{\dagger}(\hat{n}_{i}+k)^{m}b_{i}=\hat{n}_{i}(\hat{n}_{i}+k-1)^{m}\preceq(\hat{n}_{i}+k+1)^{m+1},

\displaystyle b_{i}(\hat{n}_{i}+k)^{m}b_{i}^{\dagger}=(\hat{n}_{i}+1)(\hat{n}_{i}+k+1)^{m}\preceq(\hat{n}_{i}+k+1)^{m+1},

(S.73)

where we use the fact that each of the above two operators can be diagonalized by the Fock states on the site
i
.
By combining the inequalities (
S.72
) and (
S.73
), we prove the operator inequalities of

\displaystyle b_{i}^{\dagger}\hat{B}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{B}_{k}b_{i}\preceq\prod_{j=1}^{k+1}(\hat{n}_{i_{j}}+k+1),\quad b_{i}\hat{B}_{k}^{\dagger}\hat{B}_{k}b_{i}^{\dagger}\preceq\prod_{j=1}^{k+1}(\hat{n}_{i_{j}}+k+1),

(S.74)

which proves (
S.70
), where we let
i_{k+1}=i
.

For the proof of the second inequality (
S.61
), we begin with the Hölder inequality since the operators
\{\hat{n}_{i_{j}}+k\}_{j=1}^{k}
commute with each other:

\displaystyle\left|\langle\psi|b_{i_{k}}^{\dagger}\cdots b_{i_{s+1}}^{\dagger}b_{i_{s}}^{\dagger}b_{i_{s-1}}\cdots b_{i_{2}}b_{i_{1}}|\psi\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\langle\psi|\prod_{j=1}^{k}(\hat{n}_{i_{j}}+k)^{1/2}|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{j=1}^{k}\left(\langle\psi|(\hat{n}_{i_{j}}+k)^{k/2}|\psi\rangle\right)^{1/k}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\langle\psi|(\hat{n}_{i_{j}}+k)^{k/2}|\psi\rangle.

(S.75)

Finally, for an arbitrary site
i\in\Lambda
, we can prove the operator inequality of

\displaystyle(\hat{n}_{i}+m)^{k/2}

\displaystyle\preceq\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}+\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2-1}\max\left[(1+m)^{k/2}-1,\frac{mk}{2}\right]+m^{k/2}

\displaystyle\preceq\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}+(2m)^{k}\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2-1}+m^{k/2},

(S.76)

where
k
and
m
are arbitrary non-negative integers.
The above inequality is proved from the following inequality (see below for the proof):

\displaystyle(x+y)^{z}\leq x^{z}+x^{z-1}\max\left[(1+y)^{z}-1,yz\right]

(S.77)

for
x\geq 1
,
y>0
and
z>0
.
We applied the inequality (
S.77
) by letting
x\to\hat{n}_{i}
,
y\to m
and
z\to k/2
.
By combining the inequalities (
S.75
) and (
S.76
) with
m=k
, we prove the second main inequality (
S.61
) as follows:

\displaystyle\left|\langle\psi|b_{i_{k}}^{\dagger}\cdots b_{i_{s+1}}^{\dagger}b_{i_{s}}^{\dagger}b_{i_{s-1}}\cdots b_{i_{2}}b_{i_{1}}|\psi\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i_{j}}^{k/2}|\psi\rangle+\frac{(2k)^{k}}{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\langle\psi|\hat{n}_{i_{j}}^{k/2-1}|\psi\rangle+k^{k/2}.

(S.78)

This completes the proof.
\square

[Proof of the inequality (
S.77
)]

For the proof, we consider an upper bound of

\displaystyle f_{y,z}(x):=x(1+y/x)^{z}-x,

(S.79)

which yields

\displaystyle(x+y)^{z}-x^{z}\leq x^{z-1}\sup_{x:x\geq 1}\left[f_{y,z}(x)\right].

(S.80)

We first consider the derivative of

\displaystyle f^{\prime}_{y,z}(x):=-1+\left(\frac{x+y}{x}\right)^{z}\left(1-\frac{yz}{x+y}\right).

(S.81)

For
z\leq 1
, we have
[1-y/(x+y)]^{z}\leq 1-yz/(x+y)
, and hence

\displaystyle f^{\prime}_{y,z}(x)\geq-1+\left(\frac{x+y}{x}\right)^{z}\left(1-\frac{y}{x+y}\right)^{z}=0,

(S.82)

which means that
f_{y,z}(x)
is maximized at
x=\infty
. We obtain
\lim_{x\to\infty}f_{y,z}(x)=yz
.
On the other hand, for
z>1
, we have
[1-y/(x+y)]^{z}\geq 1-yz/(x+y)

\displaystyle f^{\prime}_{y,z}(x)\leq-1+\left(\frac{x+y}{x}\right)^{z}\left(1-\frac{y}{x+y}\right)^{z}=0,

(S.83)

which means that
f_{y,z}(x)
is maximized at
x=1
as
x\geq 1
. We then obtain
f_{y,z}(1)=(1+y)^{z}-1
.

Supplementary Note 3.2
Maximum moment bounds: the total boson number not conserved

We here aim to derive the boson number distribution on a single site in the ground state
|\Omega\rangle
for the Bose-Hubbard class under Assumption
1
.
We treat separately the cases in which the total boson number is not conserved and those in which it is.
In the latter case, the ground state
|\Omega_{N}\rangle
as well as the ground energy
E_{0}(N)
depend on the total boson number, and the analyses become more complicated.
When there is no total-boson-number dependence, we simply denote the ground state and the ground energy by
|\Omega\rangle
and
E_{0}
, respectively.

In this section, we begin by treating the case where the total boson number is not conserved.
Let us consider an upper bound of the maximum moment in the set
\Lambda
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\Omega|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega\rangle={\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k/2}.

(S.84)

Regarding the quantity
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}
, we aim to prove the following proposition:

Proposition 6
.

Under Assumption
1
, the moment
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}
is bounded from above by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}\leq\max\left[1,\left(\frac{2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}}\right)^{2}\right],

(S.85)

where we define
\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}
as in Eq. (
S.55
).

Proof of Proposition
6
.

It is enough to prove the case of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}\geq 1
.
Without loss of generality, let us label the site
1
such that
\left\langle\hat{n}_{1}^{k/2}\right\rangle_{\Omega}={\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k/2}
.
For arbitrary
k_{1}\leq k
and
i\in\Lambda
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle\hat{n}_{i}^{k_{1}/2}\right\rangle_{\Omega}\leq\left(\left\langle\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}\right\rangle_{\Omega}\right)^{k_{1}/k}\leq{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k_{1}/2}.

(S.86)

Then, we consider the Schmidt decomposition of

\displaystyle|\Omega\rangle=\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{m}|S_{m}\rangle_{1}|\chi_{m}\rangle_{\Lambda_{1}},\quad\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda\setminus\{1\}.

(S.87)

Using it, we adopt the reference quantum state
\tilde{\rho}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle=|0\rangle\langle 0|_{1}\otimes\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\lambda^{2}_{m}|\chi_{m}\rangle\langle\chi_{m}|_{\Lambda_{1}}

\displaystyle=:\rho_{1}\otimes\rho_{\Lambda_{1}}.

(S.88)

Note that
\rho_{\Lambda_{1}}
are equivalent to the reduced density matrix of
|\Omega\rangle
on the subset
\Lambda_{1}
.

By decomposing the Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle H=\widehat{H_{1}}+H_{\Lambda_{1}},

(S.89)

we have

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left(H\tilde{\rho}\right)-\langle\Omega|H|\Omega\rangle={\rm tr}\left(\widehat{H_{1}}\tilde{\rho}\right)-\langle\Omega|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Omega\rangle=-\langle\Omega|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Omega\rangle\geq 0,

(S.90)

where we use
{\rm tr}\left(H_{\Lambda_{1}}\tilde{\rho}\right)={\rm tr}\left(H_{\Lambda_{1}}\rho_{\Lambda_{1}}\right)=\langle\Omega|H_{\Lambda_{1}}|\Omega\rangle
.

In the following, we aim to upper-bound
-\langle\Omega|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Omega\rangle
(or lower-bound
\langle\Omega|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Omega\rangle
).
Because we have

\displaystyle\widehat{H_{1}}=\widehat{H_{0,1}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})+\widehat{V_{+,1}}(\mathbf{n})+U_{1}\hat{n}_{1}^{k/2},

(S.91)

from the definition (
S.17
) and the notation (
S.11
),
we calculate

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle\geq U_{1}\langle\Omega|\hat{n}_{1}^{k/2}|\Omega\rangle-\langle\Omega|\overline{H_{0,1}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\Omega\rangle,

(S.92)

where we have defined
\overline{H_{0,i}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})
for
\forall i\in\Lambda
in Eq. (
S.51
) which satisfies

\widehat{H_{0,1}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})\preceq\overline{H_{0,i}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})
.
Note that
\langle\Omega|\widehat{V_{+,1}}(\mathbf{n})|\Omega\rangle\geq 0
from
\widehat{V_{+,1}}(\mathbf{n})\succeq 0
.
By applying the inequality (
S.62
) in Lemma
5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\overline{H_{0,1}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\in\Lambda\\
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\}\ni 1\end{subarray}}\sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}+1}\left|J^{(s)}_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}}\right|\left(\frac{1}{k_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}}\langle\Omega|\hat{n}_{i_{j}}^{k_{1}/2}|\Omega\rangle+\frac{(2k_{1})^{k_{1}}}{k_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}}\langle\Omega|\hat{n}_{i_{j}}^{k_{1}/2-1}|\Omega\rangle+k_{1}^{k_{1}/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\in\Lambda\\
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}\}\ni 1\end{subarray}}\sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}+1}\left|J^{(s)}_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k_{1}}}\right|\left(\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k_{1}/2}+(2k_{1})^{k_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k_{1}/2-1}+k_{1}^{k_{1}/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k}\bar{J}_{1,k_{1}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k_{1}/2}+(2k_{1})^{k_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k_{1}/2-1}+k_{1}^{k_{1}/2}\right),

(S.93)

where in the second inequality, we use the upper bound (
S.86
), and in the last inequality, we use the definition of
\bar{J}_{i,k_{1}}
in Eq. (
S.20
).

By using the parameter
\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}
in Eq. (
S.55
) and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}\geq 1
, we derive

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k}\bar{J}_{1,k_{1}}\left[{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k_{1}/2}+(2k_{1})^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k_{1}/2-1}+k_{1}^{k_{1}/2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\bar{J}_{1,k}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k/2}+2\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k}\bar{J}_{1,k_{1}}\left[1+(2k_{1})^{k_{1}}\right]{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{(k-1)/2}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{J}_{1,k}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{(k-1)/2},

(S.94)

where we use
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}\geq 1
to get
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k_{1}/2}\leq{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{(k-1)/2}
for
k_{1}\leq k-1
.
The above inequality reduces the inequality (
S.93
) to

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\overline{H_{0,1}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\Omega\rangle\leq\bar{J}_{1,k}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{(k-1)/2}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow-\langle\Omega|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Omega\rangle\leq-U_{1}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k/2}+\left(\bar{J}_{1,k}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{(k-1)/2}\right),

(S.95)

where we use
\langle\Omega|\hat{n}_{1}^{k/2}|\Omega\rangle={\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k/2}
from the assumption, and in the second line, we use the inequality (
S.92
).
Therefore, from the condition (
S.90
), we obtain the main inequality as follows:

\displaystyle-U_{1}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k/2}+\left(\bar{J}_{1,k}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{(k-1)/2}\right)\geq 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}\leq\left(\frac{2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}}\right)^{2}.

(S.96)

This completes the proof.
\square

We have proved that the
(k/2)
th order moment of the local boson number is upper-bounded by
\mathcal{O}(1)
constant in the ground state as long as
N=\mathcal{O}(|\Lambda|)
.
Conversely, we can prove that any quantum state with a large
(k/2)
th order moment has a large energy:

Corollary 7
.

Let us define
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Q}}
as the minimum energy such that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Q}}:=\inf_{|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle}\left(\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|H|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle\right)\quad\textrm{for}\quad{\mathcal{Q}}\in\mathbb{R}_{+},

(S.97)

where
\inf_{|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle}
is taken for the class of quantum state
|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle
satisfying

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\hat{n}^{k/2}_{i}|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle={\mathcal{Q}}^{k/2}.

(S.98)

We then obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Q}}-E_{0}\geq(U_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}){\mathcal{Q}}^{k/2}-2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k-1)/2}.

(S.99)

Proof of Corollary
7
.

Let us assume
\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\hat{n}^{k/2}_{1}|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle={\mathcal{Q}}^{k/2}
without loss of generality.
We then obtain

\displaystyle\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|H|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle+{\rm tr}_{\Lambda_{1}}\left[H_{\Lambda_{1}}{\rm tr}_{1}\left(|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle+E_{0,\Lambda_{1}}\geq\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle+E_{0},

(S.100)

where
E_{0,\Lambda_{1}}
has been defined as the ground energy of
H_{\Lambda_{1}}
, which was proven to be larger than
E_{0,\Lambda}
in Lemma
3
.
We can derive the same inequality as (
S.95
) for
\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle
, which yields the lower bound of

\displaystyle\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle\geq U_{1}{\mathcal{Q}}^{k/2}-\left(\bar{J}_{1,k}{\mathcal{Q}}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k-1)/2}\right).

(S.101)

By applying the above inequality to (
S.100
), we prove the desired inequality (
S.99
).
This completes the proof.
\square

Supplementary Note 3.3
Maximum moment bounds: the total boson number conserved

Supplementary Note 3.3.1
Preliminary lemmas

We here consider the lower and upper bounds of the ground energy on a subsystem
X\subseteq\Lambda
, which is proven by the following lemma:

Lemma 8
.

For the ground energy
E_{0,X}(N)
, the following inequality holds in general:

\displaystyle-\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}|X|\left[1+\left(\frac{2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}}{\tilde{U}}\right)^{k-1}\right]+\frac{\tilde{U}}{2}\sum_{i\in X}\langle\Omega_{X,N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{X,N}\rangle\leq E_{0,X}(N)\leq gN_{\ast}^{k/2}|X|,

(S.102)

where
N_{\ast}:=\left\lceil N/|X|\right\rceil\leq 1+N/|X|
,
|\Omega_{X,N}\rangle
is the ground state of the subset Hamiltonian
H_{X}
*4
*4

*4

When the ground state is degenerate, we can pick up an arbitrary state from the degenerate space.
, and

\displaystyle\tilde{U}:=\min_{i\in\Lambda}(U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}).

(S.103)

We remind that the parameters
g
and
\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}
have been defined in Eqs. (
S.13
) and (
S.55
), respectively.

Proof of Lemma
8
.

It is enough to consider the case of
X=\Lambda
, and the generalization is straightforward.
For obtaining the upper bound, let us choose the Mott state
|M\rangle
, such that
\hat{n}_{i}|M\rangle=n_{i}|M\rangle
with
n_{i}=N_{\ast}
or
N_{\ast}-1
, where
we adopt the definition of
N_{\ast}:=\left\lceil N/|\Lambda|\right\rceil\leq 1+N/|\Lambda|
.
Then, using the inequality (
S.13
), we obtain

\displaystyle\langle M|H|M\rangle\leq\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\sum_{Z:Z\ni i}\left\|\Pi_{\Lambda,\leq N_{\ast}}h_{Z}\Pi_{\Lambda,\leq N_{\ast}}\right\|\leq gN_{\ast}^{k/2}|\Lambda|,

(S.104)

which yields the upper bound in (
S.102
):

\displaystyle E_{0,\Lambda}(N)\leq\langle M|H|M\rangle\leq gN_{\ast}^{k/2}|\Lambda|.

(S.105)

We next consider the lower bound in (
S.102
).
For this purpose, we use
V_{+}(\mathbf{n})\succeq 0
to derive

\displaystyle\langle\Omega_{N}|H|\Omega_{N}\rangle\geq\langle\Omega_{N}|H_{0}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\Omega_{N}\rangle+\sum_{i\in\Lambda}U_{i}\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle,

(S.106)

By using the inequality (
S.53
) in Proposition
4
, we lower-bound

\langle\Omega_{N}|H_{0}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\Omega_{N}\rangle
by

\displaystyle\langle\Omega_{N}|H_{0}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\Omega_{N}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq-\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\Omega_{N}|\overline{H_{0,i}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\Omega_{N}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq-\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\left[\bar{J}_{i,k}\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle+\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\left(\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{(k-1)/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle+1\right)\right].

(S.107)

By combining the inequalities (
S.106
) and (
S.107
), we can derive the desired upper bound as follows:

\displaystyle\langle\Omega_{N}|H|\Omega_{N}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\left[(U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k})\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle-\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\left(\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{(k-1)/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle+1\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq-\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}|\Lambda|+\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\frac{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}}{2}\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle+\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\left(\frac{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}}{2}\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle-\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{(k-1)/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\geq-\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}|\Lambda|\left[1+\left(\frac{2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}}{\tilde{U}}\right)^{k-1}\right]+\frac{\tilde{U}}{2}\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle

(S.108)

with the definition of
\tilde{U}:=\min_{i\in\Lambda}(U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k})
,
where we use the following inequality by letting
\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle=x^{k/2}

\displaystyle\frac{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}}{2}\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle-\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{(k-1)/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}}{2}x^{k/2}-\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}x^{(k-1)/2}=\frac{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}}{2}x^{(k-1)/2}\left(x^{1/2}-\frac{2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\frac{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}}{2}\left(\frac{2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}}\right)^{k}=-\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\left(\frac{2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}}\right)^{k-1}.

(S.109)

Note that
\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{(k-1)/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle\leq\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle^{(k-1)/k}=x^{(k-1)/2}
.

We thus prove the main inequality (
S.102
).

\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
8
]

Using Lemma
8
, we further consider the energy difference between
E_{0,X}(N+1)
and
E_{0,X}(N)
.
From the previous lemma, the ground energy is proportional to the total number of bosons
N
, and hence, it is natural to expect that the energy cannot change drastically by adding one boson.
The following proposition ensures the point:

Proposition 9
.

For the ground energy
E_{0,X}(N)
, the following inequality holds in general:

\displaystyle E_{0,X}(N+1)-E_{0,X}(N)\leq\delta_{N_{\ast}},\quad N_{\ast}:=\left\lceil\frac{N}{|X|}\right\rceil,

(S.110)

where the parameter
\delta_{z}
depends on
z
as

\displaystyle\delta_{z}=2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}_{1}+\frac{4\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}_{2}}{\tilde{U}}\left[gz^{k/2}+\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}+\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\left(\frac{2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}}{\tilde{U}}\right)^{k-1}\right]

(S.111)

with
\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}_{1}
and
\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}_{2}
defined as

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}_{1}:=2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}+\bar{v}+\max_{i\in\Lambda}\left(U_{i}+\bar{J}_{i,k}\right),\quad\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}_{2}:=2^{k/2}\left[\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}+\bar{v}+\max_{i\in\Lambda}\left(U_{i}+\bar{J}_{i,k}\right)\right],

(S.112)

respectively.

Remark.

When we consider the subsystem ground energy from
E_{0,X}(0)
to
E_{0,X}(N)
, we can derive the upper bound of

\displaystyle E_{0,X}(N)-E_{0,X}(N-m)\leq\sum_{s=1}^{m}E_{0,X}(N-s+1)-E_{0,X}(N-s)\leq\sum_{s=1}^{m}\delta_{q_{\ast,s}},

(S.113)

where
q_{\ast,s}=\left\lceil(N-s)/|X|\right\rceil\leq\left\lceil N/|X|\right\rceil=:\bar{N}_{\ast}
.
Therefore, as long as
\bar{N}_{\ast}
is
\mathcal{O}(1)
, the energy difference between
E_{0,X}(m-1)
to
E_{0,X}(m)

(m\leq N)
is always upper-bounded by an
\mathcal{O}(1)
constant.
This proves the following inequality:

\displaystyle E_{0,X}(N)-E_{0,X}(N-m)\leq m\delta_{\bar{N}_{\ast}}.

(S.114)

Proof of Proposition
9
.

As in the proof of Lemma
8
, we consider the case of
X=\Lambda
for simplicity.
For the proof, we expand the ground state
|\Omega_{N}\rangle
with respect to the boson number on the site
i
as follows:

\displaystyle|\Omega_{N}\rangle=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}a_{m}|m\rangle_{i}|\chi_{m}\rangle_{\Lambda_{i}},

(S.115)

where
\hat{n}_{i}|m\rangle_{i}=m|m\rangle_{i}
, and we denote
\Lambda_{i}=\Lambda\setminus\{i\}
.
We then define the quantum state
|\Phi_{i}\rangle
with the total boson number
N+1
as

\displaystyle|\Phi_{i}\rangle=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}a_{m}|m+1\rangle_{i}|\chi_{m}\rangle_{\Lambda_{i}},

(S.116)

where we have
\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\Phi_{i}|\hat{n}_{i}|\Phi_{i}\rangle=N+1
.
Using
\{|\Phi_{i}\rangle\}_{i\in\Lambda}
, we set the reference state
\rho_{N+1}
as

\displaystyle\rho_{N+1}=\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{i\in\Lambda}|\Phi_{i}\rangle\langle\Phi_{i}|,

(S.117)

and analyze the upper bound of

\displaystyle E_{0,X}(N+1)-E_{0,X}(N)

\displaystyle\leq{\rm tr}\left(H\rho_{N+1}\right)-\langle\Omega_{N}|H|\Omega_{N}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\left(\langle\Phi_{i}|\widehat{H_{i}}|\Phi_{i}\rangle-\langle\Omega_{N}|\widehat{H_{i}}|\Omega_{N}\rangle\right),

(S.118)

where, in the last equation, we use the fact that the reduced density matrix of
|\Phi_{i}\rangle
on
\Lambda_{i}
is identical to that of
|\Omega_{N}\rangle
.

By applying Proposition
4
to
\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\Phi_{i}|\widehat{H_{i}}|\Phi_{i}\rangle
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\Phi_{i}|\widehat{H_{i}}|\Phi_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\left\{U_{i}\langle\Phi_{i}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Phi_{i}\rangle+\bar{v}\langle\Phi_{i}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Phi_{i}\rangle+\left[\bar{J}_{i,k}\langle\Phi_{i}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Phi_{i}\rangle+\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\left(\langle\Phi_{i}|\hat{n}_{i}^{(k-1)/2}|\Phi_{i}\rangle+1\right)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}|\Lambda|+\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\left(U_{i}+\bar{v}+\bar{J}_{i,k}+\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\right)\langle\Omega_{N}|(\hat{n}_{i}+1)^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}|\Lambda|+\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\left(U_{i}+\bar{v}+\bar{J}_{i,k}+\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\right)\left(2^{k/2}\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle+1\right)

\displaystyle\leq\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}_{1}|\Lambda|+\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}_{2}\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle,

(S.119)

where we use
(\hat{n}_{i}+1)^{k/2}\preceq(2\hat{n}_{i})^{k/2}+1
and the definitions for
\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}_{1}
and
\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}_{2}
.
On the other hand, for
-\langle\Omega_{N}|\widehat{H_{i}}|\Omega_{N}\rangle
, we obtain the same upper bound as

\displaystyle-\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\Omega_{N}|\widehat{H_{i}}|\Omega_{N}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\left[\bar{J}_{i,k}\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle+\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\left(\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{(k-1)/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle+1\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}_{1}|\Lambda|+\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}_{2}\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle,

(S.120)

where we use a similar inequality to (
S.119
).

Therefore, we derive

\displaystyle E_{0,X}(N+1)-E_{0,X}(N)

\displaystyle\leq 2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}_{1}+\frac{2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}_{2}}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle.

(S.121)

From the relation (
S.102
) in Lemma
8
, we obtain the upper bound of

\displaystyle-\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}|\Lambda|\left[1+\left(\frac{2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}}{\tilde{U}}\right)^{k-1}\right]+\frac{\tilde{U}}{2}\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle\leq gN_{\ast}^{k/2}|\Lambda|

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega_{N}\rangle\leq\frac{2|\Lambda|}{\tilde{U}}\left[gN_{\ast}^{k/2}+\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}+\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\left(\frac{2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}}{\tilde{U}}\right)^{k-1}\right].

(S.122)

By combining the upper bounds (
S.121
) and (
S.122
), we prove the main inequality (
S.110
).
This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Proposition
9
]

Supplementary Note 3.3.2
Main statement

Proposition 10
.

We adopt the same setup as Proposition
6
, and consider

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\Omega|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega\rangle={\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k/2}.

(S.123)

Then, under Assumption
1
, the moment
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}
is bounded from above by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}\leq\max\left[1,\left(\frac{\delta_{2N/|\Lambda|}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}}\right)^{2}\right],

(S.124)

where we define
\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}
and
\delta_{z}
for
z\geq 0
as in Eqs. (
S.55
) and (
S.111
), respectively.

Proof of Proposition
10
.

The proof is similar to the one for Proposition
6
.
We consider the case of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}\geq 1
and label the site
1
such that
\left\langle\hat{n}_{1}^{k/2}\right\rangle_{\Omega}={\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k/2}
.
From the inequality (
S.86
), for arbitrary
k_{1}\leq k
and
i\in\Lambda
, we obtain

\left\langle\hat{n}_{i}^{k_{1}/2}\right\rangle_{\Omega}\leq{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k_{1}/2}
.

For the decomposition of the ground state of

\displaystyle|\Omega_{N}\rangle=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}a_{m}|m\rangle_{1}|\chi_{m}\rangle_{\Lambda_{1}},\quad\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda\setminus\{1\}.

(S.125)

We define the reference quantum state
|\Phi_{N}\rangle
as

\displaystyle|\Phi_{N}\rangle=|0\rangle_{1}|\Omega_{N,\Lambda_{1}}\rangle_{\Lambda_{1}},

(S.126)

where
\Omega_{N,\Lambda_{1}}
is the ground state of the subset Hamiltonian
H_{\Lambda_{1}}
.

By decomposing the Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle H=\widehat{H_{1}}+H_{\Lambda_{1}},

(S.127)

we have

\displaystyle\langle\Phi_{N}|H|\Phi_{N}\rangle-\langle\Omega_{N}|H|\Omega_{N}\rangle

\displaystyle=E_{0,\Lambda_{1}}(N)-\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}|a_{m}|^{2}\langle\chi_{m}|H_{\Lambda_{1}}|\chi_{m}\rangle_{\Lambda_{1}}-\langle\Omega_{N}|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Omega_{N}\rangle\geq 0,

(S.128)

where we use
\langle\Phi_{N}|H_{\Lambda_{1}}|\Phi_{N}\rangle=\langle\Omega_{N,\Lambda_{1}}|H_{\Lambda_{1}}|\Omega_{N,\Lambda_{1}}\rangle=E_{0,\Lambda_{1}}(N)

and
\langle\Phi_{N}|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Phi_{N}\rangle=0
.

From the inequality (
S.114
), we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\chi_{m}|H_{\Lambda_{1}}|\chi_{m}\rangle_{\Lambda_{1}}\geq E_{0,\Lambda_{1}}(N-m)\geq E_{0,\Lambda_{1}}(N)-m\delta_{\bar{N}^{\ast}},

(S.129)

where we let

\displaystyle\bar{N}^{\ast}=\frac{N}{|\Lambda|-1}=\frac{|\Lambda|}{|\Lambda|-1}\cdot\frac{N}{|\Lambda|}\leq\frac{2N}{|\Lambda|}\quad\textrm{for}\quad|\Lambda|\geq 2.

(S.130)

Using the inequality (
S.129
), we immediately obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}|a_{m}|^{2}\langle\chi_{m}|H_{\Lambda_{1}}|\chi_{m}\rangle_{\Lambda_{1}}

\displaystyle\geq E_{0,\Lambda_{1}}(N)-\delta_{\bar{N}^{\ast}}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}|a_{m}|^{2}m

\displaystyle=E_{0,\Lambda_{1}}(N)-\delta_{\bar{N}^{\ast}}\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{1}|\Omega_{N}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq E_{0,\Lambda_{1}}(N)-\delta_{\bar{N}^{\ast}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{(k-1)/2},

(S.131)

where we use the condition
\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}_{1}|\Omega_{N}\rangle\leq\langle\Omega_{N}|\hat{n}^{(k-1)/2}_{1}|\Omega_{N}\rangle\leq{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{(k-1)/2}
from the assumption of
k\geq 3
.
By applying the above inequality to (
S.128
), we reach the inequality of

\displaystyle\delta_{\bar{N}^{\ast}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{(k-1)/2}-\langle\Omega_{N}|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Omega_{N}\rangle\geq\langle\Phi_{N}|H|\Phi_{N}\rangle-\langle\Omega_{N}|H|\Omega_{N}\rangle\geq 0.

(S.132)

Moreover, by employing the same analyses to derive the inequality (
S.95
), we can derive

\displaystyle-\langle\Omega_{N}|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Omega_{N}\rangle\leq-U_{1}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k/2}+\left(\bar{J}_{1,k}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{(k-1)/2}\right),

(S.133)

which reduces the inequality (
S.132
) to

\displaystyle-(U_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}){\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{k/2}+\left(\delta_{\bar{N}^{\ast}}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\right){\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}^{(k-1)/2}\geq 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Omega}\leq\left(\frac{\delta_{\bar{N}^{\ast}}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}}\right)^{2}\leq\left(\frac{\delta_{2N/|\Lambda|}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}}\right)^{2}.

(S.134)

This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Proposition
10
]

In the case where the total boson number is conserved, we can also prove a similar statement to Corollary
7
:

Corollary 11
.

We adopt the same setup as in Corollary
7
.
When the total boson number is conserved, the energy
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Q}}
in Eq. (
S.97
), i.e.,
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Q}}=\inf_{|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle}\left(\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|H|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle\right)
, satisfies

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Q}}-E_{0,\Lambda}(N)\geq(U_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}){\mathcal{Q}}^{k/2}-\left(2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}+\delta_{2N/|\Lambda|}\right){\mathcal{Q}}^{(k-1)/2}.

(S.135)

Proof of Corollary
11
.

Let us assume
\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\hat{n}^{k/2}_{1}|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle={\mathcal{Q}}^{k/2}
without loss of generality.
Then, by decomposing

\displaystyle|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle=\sum_{m}a^{\prime}_{m}|m\rangle_{1}|\chi^{\prime}_{m}\rangle_{\Lambda_{1}},

(S.136)

we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|H|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle=\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\widehat{H_{1}}+H_{\Lambda_{1}}|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle+\sum_{m}|a^{\prime}_{m}|^{2}\langle\chi^{\prime}_{m}|H_{\Lambda_{1}}|\chi^{\prime}_{m}\rangle_{\Lambda_{1}}

\displaystyle\geq\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle+\sum_{m}|a^{\prime}_{m}|^{2}E_{0,\Lambda_{1}}(N-m)\geq\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle+E_{0,\Lambda_{1}}(N)-\delta_{2N/|\Lambda|}\sum_{m}|a^{\prime}_{m}|^{2}m,

(S.137)

where
E_{0,\Lambda_{1}}(N-m)
has been defined as the ground energy of
H_{\Lambda_{1}}
with
N-m
bosons, which satisfies the inequality (
S.129
).

We can derive the same inequality as (
S.133
) for
\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle
, which yields the lower bound of

\displaystyle\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\widehat{H_{1}}|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle\geq U_{1}{\mathcal{Q}}^{k/2}-\left(\bar{J}_{1,k}{\mathcal{Q}}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k-1)/2}\right),

(S.138)

which reduces the inequality (
S.137
) to the desired inequality:

\displaystyle\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|H|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq(U_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}){\mathcal{Q}}^{k/2}-2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k-1)/2}+E_{0,\Lambda_{1}}(N)-\delta_{2N/|\Lambda|}\sum_{m}|a^{\prime}_{m}|^{2}m

\displaystyle\geq(U_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}){\mathcal{Q}}^{k/2}-\left(2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}+\delta_{2N/|\Lambda|}\right){\mathcal{Q}}^{(k-1)/2}+E_{0,\Lambda}(N),

(S.139)

where, in the last inequality, we use Lemma
3
to get
E_{0,\Lambda_{1}}(N)\geq E_{0,\Lambda}(N)
and the inequality of

\displaystyle\sum_{m}|a^{\prime}_{m}|^{2}m=\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\hat{n}_{1}|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle\leq\langle\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}|\hat{n}_{1}^{(k-1)/2}|\Psi_{\mathcal{Q}}\rangle.

(S.140)

Note that we have assumed
k\geq 3
[
(k-1)/2\geq 1
].
This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Corollary
11
]

Supplementary Note 4
Boson number distribution for Bose-Hubbard class

Supplementary Note 4.1
Main theorem

Based on the results in the previous section, we derive concentration bounds for the boson number distribution on an arbitrary site.
In this section, for the case where the boson number is conserved, we simply denote the ground state and the ground energy by
|\Omega\rangle
and
E_{0}
by omitting
N
dependence.
Similarly, the overall flow of the proof is illustrated in Supplementary Figure
2
.

We prove the following theorem (see
Supplementary Note 4.2
and
Supplementary Note 4.3
for the proof):

Theorem 1
.

Let us denote the quantity
\check{{\mathcal{J}}}
which gives the lower bound of

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Q}}-E_{0}\geq(U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}){\mathcal{Q}}^{k/2}-\check{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k-1)/2}\quad\textrm{for}\quad\forall i\in\Lambda,

(S.141)

where
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Q}}
has been defined in Eq. (
S.97
).
Then, for an arbitrary site
i\in\Lambda
, the boson number distribution decays exponentially as follows:

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\Pi_{i,\geq x}|\Omega\rangle\leq\left(\frac{1-\sqrt{1-16\zeta_{i,0}^{2}}}{4\zeta_{i,0}}\right)^{2(x-M_{i,0})/k}

(S.142)

with

\displaystyle\zeta_{i,0}:=\frac{\bar{J}_{i,k}}{U_{i}-\mathfrak{u}_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}},

(S.143)

and

\displaystyle M_{i,0}:=\max\left[2^{2/k}\left(\frac{\check{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}}\right)^{2},\left(\frac{\check{{\mathcal{J}}}\bar{J}_{i,k}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}(U_{i}-\mathfrak{u}_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k})}{\mathfrak{u}_{i}\bar{J}_{i,k}}\right)^{2}\right],

(S.144)

where
\mathfrak{u}_{i}
is an arbitrary positive constant.
We remind that the projection
\Pi_{i,\geq x}
was defined in Eq. (
S.7
).

Remark.

We note that the decay rate satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{1-\sqrt{1-16\zeta_{i,0}^{2}}}{4\zeta_{i,0}}<1\quad\textrm{for}\quad\zeta_{i,0}<\frac{1}{4}.

(S.145)

From Assumption
1
of
U_{i}>5\bar{J}_{i,k}
, we can find
\mathfrak{u}_{i}>0
such that
\zeta_{i,0}<1/4
*5
*5

*5

For example, by defining
\Delta U_{i}
by
U_{i}=5\bar{J}_{i,k}+\Delta U_{i}
, we can choose
\mathfrak{u}_{i}=\Delta U_{i}/2>0
and obtain

\displaystyle\zeta_{i,0}=\frac{\bar{J}_{i,k}}{4\bar{J}_{i,k}+\Delta U_{i}/2}<\frac{1}{4}.

(S.146)

.

Regarding the parameter
\check{{\mathcal{J}}}
, from Corollaries
7
and
11
,
we have already derived

\displaystyle\check{{\mathcal{J}}}=\begin{cases}2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}&\quad\textrm{for}\quad\textrm{Boson number is not conserved},\\
2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}+\delta_{2N/|\Lambda|}&\quad\textrm{for}\quad\textrm{Boson number is conserved}.\end{cases}

(S.147)

The condition (
S.141
) also implies an upper bound for the
(k/2)
th order moment.
By choosing
|\Psi_{Q}\rangle=|\Omega\rangle
in Eq. (
S.97
), which gives
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Q}}=E_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle 0\geq(U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k})\langle\Omega|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega\rangle-\check{{\mathcal{J}}}\langle\Omega|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega\rangle^{(k-1)/k}\longrightarrow\langle\Omega|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega\rangle^{1/k}\leq\frac{\check{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}},

(S.148)

and hence

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega\rangle\leq\left(\frac{\check{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}}\right)^{k}.

(S.149)

The parameter
M_{i,0}
cannot be well-defined in the case where
\bar{J}_{i,k}=0
.
In this case, we can prove the following alternative corollary:

Corollary 12
.

Let us adopt the same setup as in Theorem
1
and assume
\bar{J}_{i,k}=0
.
Then, for an arbitrary site
i\in\Lambda
, the boson number distribution decays exponentially as follows:

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\Pi_{i,\geq x}|\Omega\rangle\leq e^{-2(x-M_{i,0})/k}

(S.150)

with

\displaystyle M_{i,0}:=\max\left[2^{2/k}\left(\frac{\check{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{i}}\right)^{2},\left(\frac{16\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}+\check{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{i}}\right)^{2}\right].

(S.151)

We prove the statement after the proof of Theorem
1
.

Supplementary Note 4.2
Preliminaries

Without loss of generality, we consider the boson number distribution at the site
1
.
We first decompose the ground state
|\Omega\rangle
as

\displaystyle|\Omega\rangle=\sum_{m=0}^{\bar{m}}\Pi_{1,I_{m}}|\Omega\rangle=\sum_{m=0}^{\bar{m}}p_{m}^{1/2}|\omega_{m}\rangle,

\displaystyle I_{0}=[0,M),\quad I_{m}=[M+(m-1)k,M+mk)\ (1\leq m\leq\bar{m}),\quad I_{\bar{m}}=[M+(\bar{m}-1)k,\infty)

(S.152)

with

\displaystyle p_{m}:=\langle\Omega|\Pi_{1,I_{m}}|\Omega\rangle,\quad|\omega_{m}\rangle:=p_{m}^{-1/2}\Pi_{1,I_{m}}|\Omega\rangle,

(S.153)

where we defined
\Pi_{1,I_{m}}
using Eq. (
S.6
) as

\displaystyle\Pi_{1,I_{m}}=\sum_{N\in I_{m}}\Pi_{1,N}.

(S.154)

Now, the integer
M
is a control parameter which will be chosen afterward (see Lemma
13
).

The Hamiltonian (
S.17
) can change the local boson number up to
k
, and hence we have

\displaystyle\Pi_{1,I_{m}}H\Pi_{1,I_{m^{\prime}}}=0\quad\textrm{for}\quad|m-m^{\prime}|>1.

(S.155)

Under the above notation, the target probability of
\langle\Omega|\Pi_{1,\geq x}|\Omega\rangle
is upper-bounded by

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\Pi_{1,\geq x}|\Omega\rangle\leq p_{\bar{m}},

(S.156)

where we choose
\bar{m}
as

\displaystyle\bar{m}=\left\lfloor 1+\frac{x-M}{k}\right\rfloor.

(S.157)

We here define
{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}
as follows:

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\Lambda}\langle\omega_{m}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\omega_{m}\rangle={\mathcal{Q}}^{k/2}_{m},

(S.158)

which also implies

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}^{k/2}_{m}\geq(M+mk-k)^{k/2}\quad\textrm{for}\quad m\geq 1

(S.159)

from
\langle\omega_{m}|\hat{n}_{1}^{k/2}|\omega_{m}\rangle\geq[M+(m-1)k]^{k/2}
.
Moreover, from the condition (
S.141
), we immediately obtain

\displaystyle\langle\omega_{m}|H|\omega_{m}\rangle-E_{0}\geq(U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}){\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{k/2}-\check{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}.

(S.160)

For the choice of the integer
M
, we prove the following lemma, which will be used for the proof of Theorem
1
:

Lemma 13
.

By choosing the integer
M
such that

\displaystyle M=\left\lceil M_{0}\right\rceil,\quad M_{0}=\max\left[2^{2/k}\left(\frac{\check{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}}\right)^{2},\left(\frac{\check{{\mathcal{J}}}\bar{J}_{1,k}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}(U_{1}-\mathfrak{u}_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k})}{\mathfrak{u}_{1}\bar{J}_{1,k}}\right)^{2}\right],

(S.161)

we have

\displaystyle p_{0}\geq\frac{1}{2},\quad{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}^{k/2}\leq 2\left(\frac{\check{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}}\right)^{k},

(S.162)

and

\displaystyle(U_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}){\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{k/2}-\check{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}\geq\frac{U_{1}-\mathfrak{u}_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}}{\bar{J}_{1,k}}\left(\bar{J}_{1,k}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}\right)

(S.163)

for
m\geq 1
,
where
\mathfrak{u}_{1}
is an arbitrary positive parameter.

Proof of Lemma
13
.

For the first inequality of
p_{0}
, we use the Markov inequality as follows:

\displaystyle p_{0}=\langle\Omega|\Pi_{1,I_{0}}|\Omega\rangle=1-\sum_{N\geq M}\left\|\Pi_{1,N}|\Omega\rangle\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{\langle\Omega|\hat{n}_{1}^{k/2}|\Omega\rangle}{M^{k/2}}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{1}{M^{k/2}}\left(\frac{\check{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}}\right)^{k},

(S.164)

where we use the inequality (
S.149
).
Therefore,
p_{0}\geq 1/2
is derived from the choice of
M_{0}
in (
S.161
).

For the second inequality in (
S.162
) for
{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}
, we first obtain

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\Omega\rangle\geq p_{0}\langle\omega_{0}|\hat{n}_{i}^{k/2}|\omega_{0}\rangle\geq\frac{{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}^{k/2}}{2},

(S.165)

where we use
p_{0}\geq 1/2
and the definition (
S.158
).
By applying the above inequality to (
S.149
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}^{k/2}}{2}\leq\left(\frac{\check{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}}\right)^{k},

(S.166)

which reduces to the second inequality (
S.162
).

The third inequality (
S.163
) is proven as follows:

\displaystyle(U_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}){\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{k/2}-\check{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}

\displaystyle=\frac{U_{1}-\mathfrak{u}_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}}{\bar{J}_{1,k}}\left(\bar{J}_{1,k}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}\right)+\mathfrak{u}_{1}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{k/2}-\check{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}-\frac{2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}(U_{1}-\mathfrak{u}_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k})}{\bar{J}_{1,k}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}

\displaystyle=\frac{U_{1}-\mathfrak{u}_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}}{\bar{J}_{1,k}}\left(\bar{J}_{1,k}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}\right)+\mathfrak{u}_{1}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}\left[{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{1/2}-\frac{\check{{\mathcal{J}}}\bar{J}_{1,k}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}(U_{1}-\mathfrak{u}_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k})}{\mathfrak{u}_{1}\bar{J}_{1,k}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{U_{1}-\mathfrak{u}_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}}{\bar{J}_{1,k}}\left(\bar{J}_{1,k}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}\right),

(S.167)

where we use
{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{1/2}\geq M^{1/2}\geq M_{0}^{1/2}\geq\left[\check{{\mathcal{J}}}\bar{J}_{1,k}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}(U_{1}-\mathfrak{u}_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k})\right]/(\mathfrak{u}_{1}\bar{J}_{1,k})
from the definition in (
S.159
).

This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
13
]

In particular, for the case of
\bar{J}_{1,k}=0
, we prove the following corollary:

Corollary 14
.

By choosing the integer
M
such that

\displaystyle M=\left\lceil M_{0}\right\rceil,\quad M_{0}=\max\left[2^{2/k}\left(\frac{\check{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{1}}\right)^{2},\left(\frac{16\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}+\check{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{1}}\right)^{2}\right],

(S.168)

We obtain the same inequalities as in (
S.162
) and the upper bound of

\displaystyle U_{1}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{k/2}-\check{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}\geq 16\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}.

(S.169)

Proof of Corollary
14
.

The inequalities in (
S.162
) holds by simply setting
\bar{J}_{1,k}=0
. Hence, we only have to reconsider the inequality (
S.163
).
We calculate

\displaystyle\frac{(U_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}){\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{k/2}-\check{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}}{\bar{J}_{1,k}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}}=\frac{U_{1}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{k/2}-\check{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}}{2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{U_{1}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{1/2}-\check{{\mathcal{J}}}}{2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}}\geq\frac{U_{1}M^{1/2}-\check{{\mathcal{J}}}}{2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}}\geq 8,

(S.170)

which is ensured for
M>(16\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}+\check{{\mathcal{J}}})^{2}/U_{1}^{2}
.
This completes the proof.
\square

Supplementary Note 4.3
Proof of Theorem
1

Based on the decomposition (
S.152
), we consider the energy contribution of the state
|\omega_{m}\rangle
to the average
\langle\Omega|H|\Omega\rangle
, which is characterized by the parameter
p_{m}
.
In detail, we let

\displaystyle|\Omega^{\neq m}\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-p_{m}}}\sum_{s\neq m}^{\infty}p_{s}^{1/2}|\omega_{s}\rangle,

(S.171)

where the state
|\Omega^{\neq m}\rangle
is normalized since
\sum_{s\neq m}^{\infty}p_{s}=1-p_{m}
.
We aim to separate the contribution by
s=m
as follows:

\displaystyle E_{0}=(1-p_{m})\langle\Omega^{\neq m}|H|\Omega^{\neq m}\rangle+p_{m}\Delta E_{m}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\langle\Omega^{\neq m}|H|\Omega^{\neq m}\rangle=\frac{E_{0}-p_{m}\Delta E_{m}}{1-p_{m}},

(S.172)

where
\Delta E_{m}
depends on
\{p_{s}\}_{s=0}^{m}
and will be calculated below.

Because
|\Omega^{\neq m}\rangle
should satisfy the condition of

\displaystyle\langle\Omega^{\neq m}|H|\Omega^{\neq m}\rangle\geq E_{0},

(S.173)

the coefficient
|a_{m}|
need to satisfy

\displaystyle\langle\Omega^{\neq m}|H|\Omega^{\neq m}\rangle=\frac{E_{0}-p_{m}\Delta E_{m}}{1-p_{m}}\geq E_{0}.

(S.174)

The inequality implies

\displaystyle\Delta E_{m}\leq E_{0}.

(S.175)

as long as
p_{m}\neq 0,1

Let us first consider the case of
p_{m}=0
. In this case, the ground energy is simply given by

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|H|\Omega\rangle=\langle\Omega^{<m}|H|\Omega^{<m}\rangle+\langle\Omega^{>m}|H|\Omega^{>m}\rangle.

(S.176)

For
m>0
, because of
p_{0}\geq 1/2
as in (
S.162
), we can ensure

|\Omega^{>m}\rangle\neq|\Omega\rangle
and
\langle\Omega^{>m}|H|\Omega^{>m}\rangle>E_{0}
.
Therefore, we obtain
p_{m+1}=p_{m+2}=\cdots=p_{\bar{m}}=0
, which trivially yields the main inequality (
S.142
) from (
S.156
).
On the other hand, the case of
p_{m}=1
is prohibited because of
p_{0}\geq 1/2
.
We thus need to consider the inequality (
S.175
) for the non-trivial cases.

In the following, we consider the parameter
\Delta E_{m}
, which is calculated as

\displaystyle p_{m}\Delta E_{m}

\displaystyle=p_{m}\langle\omega_{m}|H|\omega_{m}\rangle-\sqrt{p_{m}p_{m+1}}\left(\langle\omega_{m+1}|\widehat{H_{0,1}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\omega_{m}\rangle+{\rm c.c.}\right)-\sqrt{p_{m}p_{m-1}}\left(\langle\omega_{m-1}|\widehat{H_{0,1}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\omega_{m}\rangle+{\rm c.c.}\right)

\displaystyle\geq p_{m}\langle\omega_{m}|H|\omega_{m}\rangle-2\sqrt{p_{m}p_{m+1}}\left(\langle\omega_{m+1}|\overline{H_{0,1}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\omega_{m+1}\rangle\langle\omega_{m}|\overline{H_{0,1}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\omega_{m}\rangle\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-2\sqrt{p_{m}p_{m-1}}\left(\langle\omega_{m-1}|\overline{H_{0,1}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\omega_{m-1}\rangle\langle\omega_{m}|\overline{H_{0,1}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\omega_{m}\rangle\right)^{1/2},

(S.177)

where we use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,
\Pi_{1,I_{m}}H_{0}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})\Pi_{1,I_{m^{\prime}}}=\Pi_{1,I_{m}}\widehat{H_{0,1}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})\Pi_{1,I_{m^{\prime}}}
for
m\neq m^{\prime}
, and the definition (
S.51
) for
\overline{H_{0,1}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})
.
We obtain a similar inequality to (
S.95
) as

\displaystyle\langle\omega_{m}|\overline{H_{0,1}}(\mathbf{b},\mathbf{b}^{\dagger})|\omega_{m}\rangle\leq\bar{J}_{1,k}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}=:T^{2}_{m}\quad\textrm{for}\quad\forall m,

(S.178)

where
{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}
has been defined by Eq. (
S.158
).
Also, by using the condition (
S.141
), we have

\displaystyle\langle\omega_{m}|H|\omega_{m}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq(U_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}){\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{k/2}-\check{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}+E_{0}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\zeta_{0}}T^{2}_{m}+E_{0},\quad\zeta_{0}:=\frac{\bar{J}_{1,k}}{U_{1}-\mathfrak{u}_{1}-\bar{J}_{1,k}},

(S.179)

where the parameter
\mathfrak{u}_{1}
is defined in Lemma
13
, and we use the inequality (
S.163
) for
{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}
.

By applying the inequalities (
S.178
) and (
S.179
) to (
S.177
), we obtain

\displaystyle p_{m}\Delta E_{m}\geq

\displaystyle p_{m}\left[\frac{1}{\zeta_{0}}T^{2}_{m}+E_{0}\right]-2\sqrt{p_{m}p_{m+1}}T_{m}T_{m+1}-2\sqrt{p_{m}p_{m-1}}T_{m}T_{m-1}.

(S.180)

From the inequality (
S.175
), i.e.,
p_{m}E_{0}\geq p_{m}\Delta E_{m}
, we can derive

\displaystyle\sqrt{p_{m}}\leq 2\zeta_{0}\left(\sqrt{p_{m+1}}\frac{T_{m+1}}{T_{m}}+\sqrt{p_{m-1}}\frac{T_{m-1}}{T_{m}}\right).

(S.181)

Here, the quantity
T_{m}
depends on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}
, which cannot be controlled in general.
Hence, the coefficients
2\zeta_{0}T_{m+1}/T_{m}
and
2\zeta_{0}T_{m-1}/T_{m}
can be arbitrarily larger than
1
.
At first glance, this prohibits us from deriving a meaningful inequality for
\sqrt{p_{m}}
.
With a careful estimation, we can prove the following lemma:

Lemma 15
.

Let
\{x_{m}\}_{m=0}^{\bar{m}}
be an arbitrary set of positive numbers.
We then consider a number sequence
\{a_{m}\}_{m=0}^{\bar{m}}
such that

\displaystyle a_{m}\leq\zeta\frac{x_{m-1}}{x_{m}}a_{m-1}+\zeta\frac{x_{m+1}}{x_{m}}a_{m+1},\quad\zeta\leq\frac{1}{2},

(S.182)

which holds for
m\geq 1
.
We then obtain the upper bound of

\displaystyle a_{m}\leq\left(\frac{1-\sqrt{1-4\zeta^{2}}}{2\zeta}\right)^{m}\frac{x_{0}a_{0}}{x_{m}}

(S.183)

for
\forall m\in[0,\bar{m}]
.

Proof of Lemma
15
.

In the proof, we let
a_{\bar{m}+1}:=0
.
We first prove

\displaystyle a_{m}\leq z_{\zeta}\zeta\frac{x_{m-1}}{x_{m}}a_{m-1},\quad z_{\zeta}=\frac{1-\sqrt{1-4\zeta^{2}}}{2\zeta^{2}}.

(S.184)

Note that under the assumption of
\zeta\leq 1/2
, we have
1\leq z_{\zeta}\leq 2
.
For the proof, we use induction. For
m=\bar{m}
, we trivially obtain

\displaystyle a_{m}\leq\zeta\frac{x_{m-1}}{x_{m}}a_{m-1}\leq z_{\zeta}\zeta\frac{x_{m-1}}{x_{m}}a_{m-1}

(S.185)

because of
z_{\zeta}\geq 1
and
a_{\bar{m}+1}=0
.
We assume the inequality (
S.184
) for
m\in[m_{0},\bar{m}]
and prove the case of
m=m_{0}-1
.
We have

\displaystyle a_{m_{0}-1}\leq\zeta\frac{x_{m_{0}-2}}{x_{m_{0}-1}}a_{m_{0}-2}+\zeta\frac{x_{m_{0}}}{x_{m_{0}-1}}a_{m_{0}}.

(S.186)

From the inequality (
S.184
) for
m=m_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\zeta\frac{x_{m_{0}}}{x_{m_{0}-1}}a_{m_{0}}\leq\zeta\frac{x_{m_{0}}}{x_{m_{0}-1}}\cdot z_{\zeta}\zeta\frac{x_{m_{0}-1}}{x_{m_{0}}}a_{m_{0}-1}=z_{\zeta}\zeta^{2}a_{m_{0}-1},

(S.187)

which reduces the inequality (
S.186
) to

\displaystyle a_{m_{0}-1}\leq\frac{\zeta}{1-z_{\zeta}\zeta^{2}}\cdot\frac{x_{m_{0}-2}}{x_{m_{0}-1}}a_{m_{0}-2}=z_{\zeta}\zeta\frac{x_{m_{0}-2}}{x_{m_{0}-1}}a_{m_{0}-2},

(S.188)

where we use the form of
z_{\zeta}=(1-\sqrt{1-4\zeta^{2}})/(2\zeta^{2})
.

Using the inequality (
S.184
), we obtain for
\forall m

\displaystyle a_{m}\leq(z_{\zeta}\zeta)^{m}\frac{x_{0}}{x_{m}}a_{0}.

(S.189)

This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
15
]

We apply Lemma
15
to the inequality (
S.181
) with

\displaystyle a_{m}\to\sqrt{p_{m}},\quad x_{m}\to T_{m},\quad\zeta\to 2\zeta_{0},

(S.190)

and obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{p_{m}}\leq\left(\frac{1-\sqrt{1-16\zeta_{0}^{2}}}{4\zeta_{0}}\right)^{m}\frac{T_{0}}{T_{m}}\sqrt{p_{0}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow p_{m}\leq\left(\frac{1-\sqrt{1-16\zeta_{0}^{2}}}{4\zeta_{0}}\right)^{2m}\frac{T^{2}_{0}}{T^{2}_{m}}.

(S.191)

We finally estimate the parameters
T_{0}
and
T_{m}
. For
T_{0}
, we obtain the upper bound of

\displaystyle T_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle=\bar{J}_{1,k}{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}^{(k-1)/2}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{J}_{1,k}\bar{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{0}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\bar{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{0}^{(k-1)/2},\quad\bar{Q}_{0}^{k/2}:=2\left(\frac{\check{{\mathcal{J}}}}{U_{i}-\bar{J}_{i,k}}\right)^{k},

(S.192)

where we use the upper bound (
S.162
).
Also, we have
{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}\geq M+mk-k
from (
S.159
), and hence

\displaystyle T_{m}^{2}=\bar{J}_{1,k}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{(k-1)/2}

\displaystyle\geq\bar{J}_{1,k}(M+mk-k)^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}(M+mk-k)^{(k-1)/2}

\displaystyle\geq\bar{J}_{1,k}\bar{Q}_{0}^{k/2}+2\bar{{\mathcal{J}}}\bar{Q}_{0}^{(k-1)/2},

(S.193)

where we use
(M+mk-k)^{k/2}\geq M_{0}^{k/2}\geq\bar{Q}_{0}^{k/2}
from the definition (
S.161
).
We thus obtain
T_{m}^{2}\geq T_{0}^{2}
, which reduces the inequality (
S.191
) to

\displaystyle p_{\bar{m}}\leq\left(\frac{1-\sqrt{1-16\zeta_{0}^{2}}}{4\zeta_{0}}\right)^{2\bar{m}}

(S.194)

by choosing
m=\bar{m}
, where
\bar{m}
was defined in Eq. (
S.157
).
By using

\displaystyle\left\lfloor 1+\frac{x-\left\lceil x_{0}\right\rceil}{k}\right\rfloor\geq\frac{x-x_{0}}{k}

(S.195)

for any
x\in\mathbb{Z}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\bar{m}=\left\lfloor 1+\frac{x-\left\lceil M_{0}\right\rceil}{k}\right\rfloor\geq\frac{x-M_{0}}{k},

(S.196)

which reduces inequality (
S.194
) to the desired form (
S.142
).
This completes the proof of Theorem
1
.
\square

The proof of Corollary
12
is exactly the same since the proof does not rely on whether or not
\bar{J}_{1,k}=0
.
As the only difference, we adopt the
M_{0}
in Corollary
14
instead of Lemma
13
.
Under the choice of Eq. (
S.168
), we have
\zeta_{0}=1/8
because of the inequality (
S.169
).
Then, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1-\sqrt{1-16\zeta_{0}^{2}}}{4\zeta_{0}}=2-\sqrt{3}=0.267949\cdots\leq e^{-1},

(S.197)

which yields the inequality (
S.150
).
This completes the proof of Corollary
12
.
\square

Supplementary Note 5
Boson number distribution in
\phi 4
-type models

Supplementary Note 5.1
Main theorem

We here consider the
\phi 4
-type which is given by from Eqs. (
S.26
) and (
S.31
):

\displaystyle H

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\mu_{i}\pi_{i}^{2}+{\mathcal{F}}(\bm{\phi})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\mu_{i}\pi_{i}^{2}+\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k/2}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}\in\Lambda}f_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}}\phi_{i_{1}}\phi_{i_{2}}\cdots\phi_{i_{2k_{1}}}.

(S.198)

In this case, Assumption
1
no longer holds, and we need a qualitatively different approach.
For the convenience of readers, we show the basic parameters
\bar{\mu}
in Eq. (
S.27
) and
\bar{f}
in Eq. (
S.46
) again:

\displaystyle\bar{\mu}:=\max_{i\in\Lambda}(|\mu_{i}|),

(S.199)

and

\displaystyle\bar{f}=\max_{i\in\Lambda}\left(\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k/2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}\in\Lambda\\
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}\}\ni i\end{subarray}}\left|f_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}}\right|\right),

(S.200)

respectively.

In the subsequent subsections, we aim to prove the following theorem:

Theorem 2
.

For an arbitrary site
i
, the boson number distribution satisfies the following sub-exponential concentration bound:

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\Pi_{i,>x}|\Omega\rangle\leq 4e^{k}\exp\left(-\frac{kx^{1/k}}{8e\tilde{C}}\right),

(S.201)

with
\tilde{C}
defined in Eq. (
S.224
) below,
where the projection
\Pi_{i,>x}
has been defined in Eq. (
S.7
).

The overall proof structure of Theorem 2 is summarized in Supplementary Figure
3
.

Supplementary Figure 3:
This figure outlines the proof of Theorem 2, which establishes the subexponential decay of the boson-number distribution for
\phi 4
class Hamiltonians.

Remark.

The primary difference between Theorem
1
for the Bose-Hubbard classes and this theorem is the dependence on the spectral gap
\Delta
.
In the former case, we need no assumption on the spectral gap; instead, we need a stronger condition of repulsive interactions (Assumption
1
).

In the
\phi 4
cases, we only need the existence of the spectral gap. We utilize Assumption
2
of the parity symmetry to ensure
\left|\langle\Omega|\phi_{i}|\Omega\rangle\right|=0
, which leads to the explicit definition of
\tilde{C}
as follows:

\displaystyle\tilde{C}

\displaystyle=k^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}}{\bar{\mu}}\right)^{1/k}\left[2\max_{i\in\Lambda}\left|\langle\Omega|\phi_{i}|\Omega\rangle\right|+4\left(\frac{\bar{\mu}}{\Delta}\right)^{1/2}+1\right]

\displaystyle=k^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}}{\bar{\mu}}\right)^{1/k}\left[4\left(\frac{\bar{\mu}}{\Delta}\right)^{1/2}+1\right],\quad\bar{f}^{\prime}=\max(\bar{f},\bar{\mu}/2).

(S.202)

From
\tilde{C}\propto\Delta^{-1/2}
, we can see that the decay of the probability depends on the spectral gap as follows:

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\Pi_{i,>x}|\Omega\rangle\leq e^{-\Omega(x^{1/k}\Delta^{1/2})}=\exp\left\{-\Omega\left[\left(\frac{x}{\Delta^{-k/2}}\right)^{1/k}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\langle\Omega|\Pi_{i,>x}|\Omega\rangle\leq\exp\left\{-\Omega\left[\left(\frac{x}{\Delta^{-2}}\right)^{1/4}\right]\right\}\quad\textrm{for}\quad k=4.

(S.203)

The optimality of the gap dependence is still an open question.
On the other hand, the numerical calculations suggest that sub-exponential decay will not improve to exponential decay, while the optimal
k
dependence of the sub-exponential form is unclear (see Supplementary Figure
4
).

Supplementary Figure 4:
We consider the single-site
\phi^{4}
model with Hamiltonian
H=\pi^{2}+\phi^{2}+\phi^{4}
. The probability
\langle\Omega|\Pi_{=m}|\Omega\rangle

(0\leq m\leq N)
is calculated, where the total boson number cutoff is set to
N=25000
and numerical precision is ensured up to 5000 digits.
The orange line shows a subexponential fit given by
-6.89237-1.62833\times m^{0.747718}
, which indicates a subexponential decay of the form
e^{-3m/4}
.

Supplementary Note 5.2
Useful relations

Supplementary Note 5.2.1
Generalized commutator relation

If two operators
{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}
satisfy
[{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}]=1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left[{\mathcal{A}}^{m},{\mathcal{B}}^{n}\right]=\sum_{k=1}^{\min(m,n)}C_{k,m,n}{\mathcal{B}}^{n-k}{\mathcal{A}}^{m-k}=-\sum_{k=1}^{\min(m,n)}(-1)^{k}C_{k,m,n}{\mathcal{A}}^{m-k}{\mathcal{B}}^{n-k}

(S.204)

with

\displaystyle C_{k,m,n}:=k!\binom{m}{k}\binom{n}{k}.

(S.205)

This expression is immediately derived by parametrizing
{\mathcal{A}}\to x_{A}{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}\to x_{B}{\mathcal{B}}
with the expression of

\displaystyle e^{x_{A}{\mathcal{A}}}e^{x_{B}{\mathcal{B}}}=e^{x_{B}{\mathcal{B}}}e^{x_{A}{\mathcal{A}}}e^{x_{A}x_{B}[{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}]},

(S.206)

where we use the Zassenhaus formula as
e^{x_{A}{\mathcal{A}}+x_{B}{\mathcal{B}}}=e^{x_{A}{\mathcal{A}}}e^{x_{B}{\mathcal{B}}}e^{-x_{A}x_{B}[{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}}]/2}=e^{x_{B}{\mathcal{B}}}e^{x_{A}{\mathcal{A}}}e^{-x_{A}x_{B}[{\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{A}}]/2}
.
By comparing the terms with
x_{A}^{m}x_{B}^{n}
, we derive Eq. (
S.204
).

By using these relations, for
\phi
and
\pi
with
[\phi,\pi]=i
(or
[-i\phi,\pi]=1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left[\phi^{m},\pi^{n}\right]=\sum_{k=1}^{\min(m,n)}i^{k}C_{k,m,n}\pi^{n-k}\phi^{m-k}=-\sum_{k=1}^{\min(m,n)}(-i)^{k}C_{k,m,n}\phi^{m-k}\pi^{n-k}.

(S.207)

Supplementary Note 5.2.2
Tradeoff relation between the variance and the spectral gap

As a key analytical tool, we use the following trade-off inequality, which connects the variance and the spectral gap
[
62
,
63
,
59
]
:

\displaystyle{\rm Var}(O)\cdot\Delta\leq\frac{1}{2}\left|\langle\Omega|\left[\left[H,O\right],O\right]|\Omega\rangle\right|,

(S.208)

where the proof is elementary (for example, see Appendix A in Ref.
[
63
]
,
and we use the above upper bound without proof.

Supplementary Note 5.3
Moment of operator
\phi

We here consider the moment of the
\phi
operator as
\left|\langle\Omega|\phi^{s}|\Omega\rangle\right|
, where we omit the lattice index
i
for simplicity.
The following proposition holds for an arbitrary
\phi_{i}
(
i\in\Lambda
):

Proposition 16
.

Under the existence of the spectral gap
\Delta
, the moment of
\phi
is bounded from above by

\displaystyle\left|\langle\Omega|\phi^{s}|\Omega\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left[\left(\left|\langle\Omega|\phi|\Omega\rangle\right|+2\left(\frac{\bar{\mu}}{\Delta}\right)^{1/2}\right)s\right]^{s},

(S.209)

for arbitrary
s\in\mathbb{N}
, where the parameter
\bar{\mu}
has been defined in Eq. (
S.199
), i.e.,
\bar{\mu}:=\max_{i\in\Lambda}(|\mu_{i}|)
.

Remark.
Under the assumption of the parity symmetry (
S.31
), we can always let

\displaystyle\left|\langle\Omega|\phi|\Omega\rangle\right|=0,

(S.210)

while under the breakdown of the parity symmetry, we need to estimate the upper bound of
\left|\langle\Omega|\phi|\Omega\rangle\right|
.
It is possible by employing a similar analysis to the proof of Proposition
6
.
However, for this purpose, we need an additional condition similar to Assumption
1
for the Bose-Hubbard cases, and the unconditional proof
*6
*6

*6

We mean by the unconditional proof that any non-critical ground states with the spectral gap satisfy a boson-number concentration bound as well as the entanglement area law without any additional conditions.
of the entanglement area law is, in general, impossible.

Supplementary Note 5.3.1
Proof of Proposition
16

We aim to upper bound the variance of
\phi^{m}
for general
m
.
By applying the trade-off relation (
S.208
), we need to consider
\langle\Omega|\left[\left[H,\phi^{m}\right],\phi^{m}\right]|\Omega\rangle
. Here, only the term of
\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\mu_{i}\pi_{i}^{2}
in the Hamiltonian contributes to the commutator.
Therefore, from
\max_{i\in\Lambda}(|\mu_{i}|)=\bar{\mu}
in Eq. (
S.199
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta\left(\langle\Omega|\phi^{2m}|\Omega\rangle-\langle\Omega|\phi^{m}|\Omega\rangle^{2}\right)\leq\frac{\bar{\mu}}{2}\left|\langle\Omega|\left[\left[\pi^{2},\phi^{m}\right],\phi^{m}\right]|\Omega\rangle\right|.

(S.211)

To estimate the RHS of the above inequality, we consider the following lemma:

Lemma 17
.

For an arbitrary power of
\phi
, the double commutator
\left[\left[\pi^{2},\phi^{m}\right],\phi^{m}\right]
is given as follows:

\displaystyle\left[\left[\pi^{2},\phi^{m}\right],\phi^{m}\right]=-2m^{2}\phi^{2m-2}.

(S.212)

Proof of Lemma
17
.

We begin with the commutator
\left[\pi^{2},\phi^{m}\right]
.
Because of
[\pi,\phi]=-i
, we have

\displaystyle\left[\pi^{2},\phi^{m}\right]=\pi[\pi,\phi^{m}]+[\pi,\phi^{m}]\pi=-im\left(\pi\phi^{m-1}+\phi^{m-1}\pi\right).

(S.213)

In the same way, we can also derive

\displaystyle\left[\left(\pi\phi^{m-1}+\phi^{m-1}\pi\right),\phi^{m}\right]=-2im\phi^{2m-2}.

(S.214)

By combining the above two equations, we prove Eq. (
S.212
).
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
17
]

By applying Lemma
17
to the inequality (
S.211
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta\cdot\left(\langle\Omega|\phi^{2m}|\Omega\rangle-\langle\Omega|\phi^{m}|\Omega\rangle^{2}\right)\leq\frac{\bar{\mu}}{2}\left|\langle\Omega|\left[\left[\pi^{2},\phi^{m}\right],\phi^{m}\right]|\Omega\rangle\right|\leq\bar{\mu}m^{2}\langle\Omega|\phi^{2m-2}|\Omega\rangle,

(S.215)

which gives

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\phi^{2m}|\Omega\rangle\leq\langle\Omega|\phi^{m}|\Omega\rangle^{2}+\frac{\bar{\mu}m^{2}}{\Delta}\langle\Omega|\phi^{2m-2}|\Omega\rangle.

(S.216)

We here rewrite the target inequality (
S.209
) as follows:

\displaystyle\left|\langle\Omega|\phi^{s}|\Omega\rangle\right|\leq\left(Cs\right)^{s},\quad C=\left|\langle\Omega|\phi|\Omega\rangle\right|+2\left(\frac{\bar{\mu}}{\Delta}\right)^{1/2}.

(S.217)

For the proof, we use induction.
For
s=1
, the inequality trivially holds because of
C\geq\left|\langle\Omega|\phi|\Omega\rangle\right|
.
Also, for
s=2
, the inequality (
S.216
) gives

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\phi^{2}|\Omega\rangle\leq\langle\Omega|\phi|\Omega\rangle^{2}+\frac{\bar{\mu}}{\Delta}\leq C^{2}+\frac{C^{2}}{4}=\frac{5C^{2}}{4}\leq(2C)^{2},

(S.218)

where we use
\left|\langle\Omega|\phi|\Omega\rangle\right|\leq C
and
(\bar{\mu}/\Delta)^{1/2}\leq C/2
.

We assume the inequality (
S.217
) up to
s=2m-2
and consider the cases of
s=2m-1
and
2m
, where
m\geq 2

We first consider
s=2m
and obtain

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\phi^{2m}|\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\langle\Omega|\phi^{m}|\Omega\rangle^{2}+\frac{\bar{\mu}m^{2}}{\Delta}\langle\Omega|\phi^{2m-2}|\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\left(Cm\right)^{2m}+\frac{\bar{\mu}m^{2}}{\Delta}\left(2Cm\right)^{2m-2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(2Cm\right)^{2m}\left(2^{-2m}+\frac{\bar{\mu}}{4C^{2}\Delta}\right)\leq\left(2Cm\right)^{2m},

(S.219)

where we use the inequality of
