\displaystyle 2^{-2m}+\frac{1}{4C^{2}}\cdot\frac{\bar{\mu}}{\Delta}\leq\frac{1}{16}+\frac{1}{16}=\frac{1}{8}<1.

(S.220)

Note that
2^{-2m}\leq 1/8
because of
m\geq 2
.
For the case of
s=2m-1
, we utilize the inequality (
S.219
) to derive

\displaystyle\left|\langle\Omega|\phi^{2m-1}|\Omega\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\langle\Omega|\phi^{2m}|\Omega\rangle^{1-1/(2m)}\leq\left[\frac{1}{8}\left(2Cm\right)^{2m}\right]^{1-1/(2m)}\leq\left(\frac{1}{8}\right)^{3/4}\left(2Cm\right)^{2m-1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{8}\right)^{3/4}\left[C(2m-1)\right]^{2m-1}\left(1+\frac{1}{2m-1}\right)^{2m-1}\leq e\left(\frac{1}{8}\right)^{3/4}\left[C(2m-1)\right]^{2m-1}\leq\left[C(2m-1)\right]^{2m-1}.

(S.221)

We thus prove the inequality (
S.217
) for all
s\in\mathbb{N}
.

This completes the proof of Proposition
16
.
\square

Supplementary Note 5.4
Moment of operator
\pi

Using the upper bound on the moment function
|\langle\Omega|\phi^{s}|\Omega\rangle|
, we derive an upper bound for
|\langle\Omega|\pi^{s}|\Omega\rangle|
.
The major difficulty for the
\phi 4
-type Hamiltonian lies in this point.
We aim to prove the following subtheorem (see
Supplementary Note 5.6
for the proof):

Subtheorem 1
.

Under the existence of the spectral gap
\Delta
, the moment of
\pi_{i}
is bounded from above by

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\pi_{i}^{s}|\Omega\rangle\leq\left[\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}}{\bar{\mu}}\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}s\right)^{k}\right]^{s/2},

(S.222)

for arbitrary
s\in\mathbb{N}
and
i\in\Lambda
,
where
\bar{f}^{\prime}=\max(\bar{f},\bar{\mu}/2)
and
\check{c}_{1}
is defined as

\displaystyle\check{c}_{1}=2\max_{i\in\Lambda}\left|\langle\Omega|\phi_{i}|\Omega\rangle\right|+4\left(\frac{\bar{\mu}}{\Delta}\right)^{1/2}+1.

(S.223)

We recall that the parameters
\bar{\mu}
and
\bar{f}
were defined in Eqs. (
S.199
) and (
S.200
), respectively.

Remark.
As in Proposition
16
, we can set
\left|\langle\Omega|\phi|\Omega\rangle\right|=0
under the parity symmetry (
S.31
).
We rewrite the inequality (
S.222
) as

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\pi_{i}^{s}|\Omega\rangle\leq\left(\tilde{C}s\right)^{ks/2},\quad\tilde{C}=\check{c}_{1}k^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}}{\bar{\mu}}\right)^{1/k}.

(S.224)

Using the Markov inequality, we obtain the probability distribution of
\pi_{i}
as

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\Pi_{|\pi_{i}|>x}|\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\min_{s\in\mathbb{N}}\left[\left(\frac{\tilde{C}s}{x^{2/k}}\right)^{ks/2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{k}{2}\left\lfloor\frac{x^{2/k}}{e\tilde{C}}\right\rfloor\right)\leq e^{k/2}e^{-x^{2/k}/(2e\tilde{C}/k)},

(S.225)

where we choose
s
as
s=\left\lfloor x^{k/2}/(e\tilde{C})\right\rfloor
.
This inequality gives subexponential decay instead of exponential decay,
where
\Pi_{|\pi_{i}|>x}
denotes the projection onto the eigenspace of
\pi_{i}
whose absolute eigenvalues are larger than
x
.

By comparing the moment bound (
S.224
) with that for
\phi
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\left|\langle\Omega|\phi^{s}|\Omega\rangle\right|\leq\left[\left(\left|\langle\Omega|\phi|\Omega\rangle\right|+2\left(\frac{\bar{\mu}}{\Delta}\right)^{1/2}\right)s\right]^{s},

(S.226)

we can also ensure the following looser upper bound as

\displaystyle\left|\langle\Omega|\phi^{s}|\Omega\rangle\right|\leq\left(\tilde{C}s\right)^{ks/2}.

(S.227)

Therefore, for both of
\left|\langle\Omega|\phi^{s}|\Omega\rangle\right|
and
\left|\langle\Omega|\pi^{s}|\Omega\rangle\right|
, the upper bound
\left(\tilde{C}s\right)^{ks/2}
holds.

Supplementary Note 5.5
Moment of the number operator
\hat{n}
: concluding the proof of Theorem
2

By combining Proposition
16
and Subtheorem
1
, we finally prove the main theorem on the boson number distribution for the
\phi 4
class.
For this purpose, we first prove the following proposition on the moment upper bound (see
Supplementary Note 5.5.2
for the proof):

Proposition 18
.

For an arbitrary site
i
, the moment function of the boson number operator
\hat{n}_{i}
satisfies the following bound:

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\hat{n}^{s}|\Omega\rangle\leq 4\left(8\tilde{C}s\right)^{ks},

(S.228)

where
s
is an arbitrary positive integer, and the parameter
\tilde{C}
is defined in Eq. (
S.224
).

Based on the above proposition, we immediately prove Theorem
2
regarding the boson number distribution:

Supplementary Note 5.5.1
Proof of Theorem
2
.

As in the inequality (
S.225
), we use the Markov inequality as follows:

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\Pi_{i,>x}|\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\min_{s\in\mathbb{N}}\left[4\left(\frac{8\tilde{C}s}{x^{1/k}}\right)^{ks}\right].

(S.229)

By choosing
s
as

\displaystyle s=\left\lfloor\frac{x^{1/k}}{8e\tilde{C}}\right\rfloor\geq\frac{x^{1/k}}{8e\tilde{C}}-1,

(S.230)

we reduce the inequality (
S.229
) to the desired form:

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\Pi_{i,>x}|\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 4\exp\left(-k\left\lfloor\frac{x^{1/k}}{8e\tilde{C}}\right\rfloor\right)\leq 4e^{k}\exp\left(-\frac{kx^{1/k}}{8e\tilde{C}}\right).

(S.231)

This completes the proof of Theorem
2
.
\square

Supplementary Note 5.5.2
Proof of Proposition
18

For simplicity of the notations, we omit the site index
i
.
From the definitions of
\phi
and
\pi
in Eq. (
S.24
), we have

\displaystyle\phi^{2}+\pi^{2}=2\hat{n}+1,

(S.232)

and hence

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|(\phi^{2}+\pi^{2})^{s}|\Omega\rangle=\langle\Omega|(2\hat{n}+1)^{s}|\Omega\rangle,

(S.233)

which allows us to estimate the higher-order moments of the boson number from
the moments of
\langle\Omega|\phi^{s}|\Omega\rangle
and
\langle\Omega|\pi^{s}|\Omega\rangle
.

In the following, we generally decompose

\displaystyle(\phi^{2}+\pi^{2})^{s}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1},m_{2}\leq 2s\\
m_{1}+m_{2}\leq 2s\end{subarray}}\lambda^{(s)}_{m_{1},m_{2}}\phi^{m_{1}}\pi^{m_{2}},

(S.234)

where we can find such an expression using the commutation relation (
S.207
).
Unfortunately, it is a challenging task to find the explicit form of
\lambda^{(s)}_{m_{1},m_{2}}
, and hence, we upper-bound the absolute value.
For this purpose, we prove the following lemma:

Lemma 19
.

For arbitrary
s
,
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
such that
m_{1},m_{2}\leq 2s
and
m_{1}+m_{2}\leq 2s
, we obtain the upper bound of

\displaystyle\left|\lambda^{(s)}_{m_{1},m_{2}}\right|\leq 4^{s}s^{2s-m_{1}-m_{2}}.

(S.235)

Proof of Lemma
19
.

For the proof, we use the induction method.
For
s=1
, the inequality trivially holds since only the terms of
\lambda^{(s)}_{2,0}
and
\lambda^{(s)}_{0,2}
are non-zero and equal to
1
.
For
s=2
, we have

\displaystyle(\phi^{2}+\pi^{2})^{2}=\phi^{4}+\pi^{4}+\phi^{2}\pi^{2}+\pi^{2}\phi^{2}=\phi^{4}+\pi^{4}+2\phi^{2}\pi^{2}-4i\phi\pi-2,

(S.236)

where we use Eq. (
S.238
) below with
m_{1}=2
.
The above equation proves the inequality (
S.235
) for
\lambda^{(2)}_{m_{1},m_{2}}
.

We assume the inequality for general
s
with
s\geq 2
and consider the case of
s+1
.
Using the decomposition (
S.234
), we have

\displaystyle(\phi^{2}+\pi^{2})^{s+1}

\displaystyle=(\phi^{2}+\pi^{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1},m_{2}\leq 2s\\
m_{1}+m_{2}\leq 2s\end{subarray}}\lambda^{(s)}_{m_{1},m_{2}}\phi^{m_{1}}\pi^{m_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1},m_{2}\leq 2s\\
m_{1}+m_{2}\leq 2s\end{subarray}}\lambda^{(s)}_{m_{1},m_{2}}\left[\phi^{m_{1}+2}\pi^{m_{2}}+\phi^{m_{1}}\pi^{m_{2}+2}-2im_{1}\phi^{m_{1}-1}\pi^{m_{2}+1}-m_{1}(m_{1}-1)\phi^{m_{1}-2}\pi^{m_{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1},m_{2}\leq 2s+2\\
m_{1}+m_{2}\leq 2s+2\end{subarray}}\lambda^{(s+1)}_{m_{1},m_{2}}\phi^{m_{1}}\pi^{m_{2}},

(S.237)

where we use the commutation relation (
S.207
) with
m\to m_{1}
and
n\to 2
as follows

\displaystyle\pi^{2}\phi^{m_{1}}=\phi^{m_{1}}\pi^{2}-[\phi^{m_{1}},\pi^{2}]

\displaystyle=\phi^{m_{1}}\pi^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{2}(-i)^{k}k!\binom{2}{k}\binom{m_{1}}{k}\phi^{m_{1}-k}\pi^{2-k}

\displaystyle=\phi^{m_{1}}\pi^{2}-2im_{1}\phi^{m_{1}-1}\pi-m_{1}(m_{1}-1)\phi^{m_{1}-2}.

(S.238)

From the equation, we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda^{(s+1)}_{m_{1},m_{2}}=\lambda^{(s)}_{m_{1}-2,m_{2}}+\lambda^{(s)}_{m_{1},m_{2}-2}-2i(m_{1}+1)\lambda^{(s)}_{m_{1}+1,m_{2}-1}-(m_{1}+1)(m_{1}+2)\lambda^{(s)}_{m_{1}+2,m_{2}}.

(S.239)

where we let
\lambda^{(s)}_{m_{1},m_{2}}=0
if the condition
m_{1},m_{2}\leq 2s
or
m_{1}+m_{2}\leq 2s
is not satisfied.

Using the assumption for
\lambda^{(s)}_{m_{1},m_{2}}
and the inequality (
S.235
), we derive

\displaystyle\left|\lambda^{(s+1)}_{m_{1},m_{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 4^{s}\left(2s^{2s-m_{1}-m_{2}+2}+2(m_{1}+2)s^{2s-m_{1}-m_{2}}+(m_{1}+2)^{2}s^{2s-m_{1}-m_{2}-2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4^{s+1}(s+1)^{2s-m_{1}-m_{2}+2}\left[\frac{1}{2}+\frac{(m_{1}+1)}{2(s+1)^{2}}+\frac{(m_{1}+2)^{2}}{4(s+1)^{4}}\right]\leq 4^{s+1}(s+1)^{2s-m_{1}-m_{2}+2},

(S.240)

where, in the last inequality, we use
m_{1}+2\leq 2s+2=2(s+1)
(from
m_{1}+m_{2}\leq 2s
) and
s\geq 2
.
We thus prove the inequality (
S.235
) in the case of
s+1
.
This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
19
]

Using the decomposition (
S.234
) with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|(\phi^{2}+\pi^{2})^{s}|\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s_{0}=0}^{s}\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}=2s_{0}}\left|\lambda^{(s)}_{m_{1},m_{2}}\right|\sqrt{\langle\Omega|\phi^{2m_{1}}|\Omega\rangle\langle\Omega|\pi^{2m_{2}}|\Omega\rangle}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s_{0}=0}^{s}\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}=2s_{0}}4^{s}s^{2s-m_{1}-m_{2}}\cdot\left(2\tilde{C}m_{1}\right)^{km_{1}/2}\left(2\tilde{C}m_{2}\right)^{km_{2}/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s_{0}=0}^{s}\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}=2s_{0}}4^{s}s^{2s-2s_{0}}\cdot\left(4\tilde{C}s\right)^{ks_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq(2s+1)4^{s}\left(4\tilde{C}s\right)^{ks}\sum_{s_{0}=0}^{s}\left[\frac{s^{2}}{(4\tilde{C}s)^{k}}\right]^{s-s_{0}}\leq(2s+1)4^{s}\left(4\tilde{C}s\right)^{ks}\frac{1}{1-\frac{s^{2}}{(4\tilde{C}s)^{k}}},

(S.241)

where we use Lemma
19
and the inequalities (
S.224
) and (
S.227
).
Finally, from the upper bound of

\displaystyle\frac{s^{2}}{(4\tilde{C}s)^{k}}\leq\frac{1}{(4\tilde{C})^{k}}\leq\frac{1}{4},

(S.242)

we reduce the inequality (
S.241
) to

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|(\phi^{2}+\pi^{2})^{s}|\Omega\rangle\leq 2(2s+1)4^{s}\left(4\tilde{C}s\right)^{ks}\leq 2(2s+1)\left(8\tilde{C}s\right)^{ks},

(S.243)

where we use
k\geq 2
and
\tilde{C}\geq 1
[see Eq. (
S.224
)].

From Eq. (
S.233
), we have
\langle\Omega|(\phi^{2}+\pi^{2})^{s}|\Omega\rangle=\langle\Omega|(2\hat{n}+1)^{s}|\Omega\rangle\geq\frac{2s+1}{2}\langle\Omega|\hat{n}^{s}|\Omega\rangle
, and hence
we prove the inequality (
S.228
).
This completes the proof of Proposition
18
.
\square

Supplementary Note 5.6
Proof of Subtheorem
1

Supplementary Note 5.6.1
Key proposition

For the proof, we have to treat the expectation as

\displaystyle\left|\langle\Omega|\pi^{2m-s}\phi^{s^{\prime}}|\Omega\rangle\right|.

(S.244)

To estimate it, we need to separate the average with respect to
\pi^{2m}
using the known moment bound (
S.209
) for
\left|\langle\Omega|\phi^{s}|\Omega\rangle\right|
.
The following proposition plays a key role in our analyses (see
Supplementary Note 5.7
for the proof):

Proposition 20
.

Let
\left\langle O\right\rangle
be the expectation value with respect to a given quantum state.
We assume that the following upper bound for
\left\langle\phi^{2s}\right\rangle
holds for positive
c_{1}
:

\displaystyle\left\langle\phi^{2s}\right\rangle\leq(c_{1}s)^{2s}\quad(c_{1}\geq 1)\quad\textrm{for}\quad\forall s\in\mathbb{N}.

(S.245)

Then, for
\forall m\geq 1
,
\forall s\in[1,2m]
and
\forall s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}
such that
s^{\prime}+s^{\prime\prime}\leq\kappa s
(
\kappa\geq 1
), we obtain the upper bound of

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\pi^{2m-s}\phi^{s^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle\right|\leq 3\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa m}}\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m}\right\rangle^{1-\frac{s}{2m}}\left(4c_{1}\kappa m\right)^{s^{\prime}},\left(4c_{1}\kappa m\right)^{2m+s^{\prime}-s}\right],

(S.246)

where
\Phi_{0}
is an arbitrary Hermitian operator that commutes with
\phi
and
\pi
.

Remark.

The proposition has a similar manner to the Hölder inequality.
The difficulty here is that the Hölder inequality does not hold for non-commuting operators.
We can only utilize the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality as

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\pi^{2m-s}\phi^{s^{\prime}}\right\rangle\right|\leq\sqrt{\left\langle\phi^{2s^{\prime}}\right\rangle\left\langle\pi^{4m-2s}\right\rangle}.

(S.247)

In the above inequality, the RHS includes
\left\langle\pi^{4m-2s}\right\rangle
, which may be a higher-order moment than
\left\langle\pi^{2m}\right\rangle
if
4m-2s\geq 2m
.
This prohibits us from utilizing the inequality in upper-bounding
\langle\Omega|\pi^{s}|\Omega\rangle
.

In the proof of Subtheorem
1
, Proposition
20
is invoked at the critical bottleneck where the trade-off inequality reduces the variance of
\pi^{m}
to expectation values of double commutators involving
\pi^{m}
and interaction terms that are polynomial in
\phi
.
At this stage, a naive application of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality would necessarily introduce higher
\pi
-moments and thus break the inductive scheme.
Proposition
20
prevents this escalation by systematically converting all such mixed expectations back into expressions controlled by the top moment
\langle\pi^{2m}\rangle
, up to explicit and manageable polynomial factors in
m
.
This mechanism closes the induction on
m
and leads directly to the subexponential moment bounds, and hence the tail estimates, for the
\pi
-operator claimed in Subtheorem
1
.

Based on the above proposition, we can prove the following lemma:

Lemma 21
.

Under the setup of Proposition
20
,
for arbitrary positive
l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
, the double commutator
\left[\left[\phi^{l_{1}},\pi^{m}\right],\pi^{m}\right]\Phi_{0}^{l_{2}}
satisfies the norm inequality as

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left[\left[\phi^{l_{1}},\pi^{m}\right],\pi^{m}\right]\Phi_{0}^{l_{2}}\right\rangle\right|\leq 3\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa_{l}m}\right\rangle^{\frac{l_{2}}{4\kappa_{l}m}}(2l_{1}m)^{2}G_{1,m}^{l_{1}-2}G_{2,m}^{2m-2},

(S.248)

where
l=l_{1}+l_{2}
, and
G_{1,m},G_{2,m}
are defined as

\displaystyle G_{1,m}=4c_{1}\kappa_{l}m,\and G_{2,m}=\max\left(\left\langle\pi^{2m}\right\rangle^{1/(2m)},4c_{1}\kappa_{l}m\right)

(S.249)

with the choice of
\kappa_{l}=\max[1,(l-2)/2]
.
We recall that
[\Phi_{0},\phi]=[\Phi_{0},\pi]=0
.

Proof of Lemma
21
.

For the commutator
\left[\phi^{l_{1}},\pi^{m}\right]
for
\forall l\in\mathbb{N}
, we obtain from
[\phi,\pi]=i

\displaystyle\left[\phi^{l_{1}},\pi^{m}\right]=\sum_{s=0}^{l_{1}-1}\phi^{s}\left[\phi,\pi^{m}\right]\phi^{l_{1}-1-s}=im\sum_{s=0}^{l_{1}-1}\phi^{s}\pi^{m-1}\phi^{l_{1}-1-s},

(S.250)

By using the relation of Eq. (
S.207
), we reduce the above inequality to the form of

\displaystyle\left[\phi^{l_{1}},\pi^{m}\right]=im\sum_{s=0}^{l_{1}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\min(s,m-1)}i^{k}C_{k,s,m-1}\pi^{m-1-k}\phi^{l_{1}-1-k}.

(S.251)

In the same way, we obtain

\displaystyle\left[\left[\phi^{l_{1}},\pi^{m}\right],\pi^{m}\right]

\displaystyle=im\sum_{s=0}^{l_{1}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\min(s,m-1)}i^{k}C_{k,s,m-1}\pi^{m-1-k}\left[\phi^{l_{1}-1-k},\pi^{m}\right]

\displaystyle=-m^{2}\sum_{s=0}^{l_{1}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\min(s,m-1)}i^{k}C_{k,s,m-1}\pi^{m-1-k}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{l_{1}-2-k}\sum_{k^{\prime}=0}^{\min(s^{\prime},m-1)}i^{k^{\prime}}C_{k^{\prime},s^{\prime},m-1}\pi^{m-1-k^{\prime}}\phi^{l_{1}-2-k-k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-m^{2}\sum_{s=0}^{l_{1}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\min(s,m-1)}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{l_{1}-2-k}\sum_{k^{\prime}=0}^{\min(s^{\prime},m-1)}i^{k+k^{\prime}}C_{k,s,m-1}C_{k^{\prime},s^{\prime},m-1}\pi^{2m-2-k-k^{\prime}}\phi^{l_{1}-2-k-k^{\prime}}.

(S.252)

In particular, for
l_{1}=1
and
l_{1}=2
, we have

\displaystyle\left[\left[\phi,\pi^{m}\right],\pi^{m}\right]=0,\quad\left[\left[\phi^{2},\pi^{m}\right],\pi^{m}\right]=-m^{2}\pi^{2m-2},

(S.253)

respectively.
Also, for
m=1
, we have

\displaystyle\left[\left[\phi^{l_{1}},\pi\right],\pi\right]=-\sum_{s=0}^{l_{1}-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{l_{1}-2}\phi^{l_{1}-2}=-l_{1}(l_{1}-1)\phi^{l_{1}-2}.

(S.254)

Therefore, in the following, we consider
l_{1}\geq 3
and
m\geq 2
as the non-trivial regimes.

We then use Proposition
20
to obtain an upper bound of

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\pi^{2m-2-k-k^{\prime}}\phi^{l_{1}-2-k-k^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{l_{2}}\right\rangle\right|.

(S.255)

We here apply the inequality (
S.246
) with the choice of

\displaystyle s\to 2+k+k^{\prime},\quad s^{\prime}\to l_{1}-2-k-k^{\prime},\quad s^{\prime\prime}\to l_{2},\quad\kappa\to\kappa_{l}:=\max\left(1,\frac{l-2}{2}\right),

(S.256)

where
\kappa
was defined by a constant satisfying
s^{\prime}+s^{\prime\prime}\leq\kappa s
(
\kappa\geq 1
). Note that
l_{1}+l_{2}=l
.
We then obtain

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\pi^{2m-2-k-k^{\prime}}\phi^{l_{1}-2-k-k^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{l_{2}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq 3\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa_{l}m}\right\rangle^{\frac{l_{2}}{4\kappa_{l}m}}\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m}\right\rangle^{1-\frac{2+k+k^{\prime}}{2m}}\left(4c_{1}\kappa_{l}m\right)^{l_{1}-2-k-k^{\prime}},\left(4c_{1}\kappa_{l}m\right)^{2m+l_{1}-2(2+k+k^{\prime})}\right]

\displaystyle=3\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa_{l}m}\right\rangle^{\frac{l_{2}}{4\kappa_{l}m}}\left(4c_{1}\kappa_{l}m\right)^{l_{1}-2-k-k^{\prime}}\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m}\right\rangle^{1-\frac{2+k+k^{\prime}}{2m}},\left(4c_{1}\kappa_{l}m\right)^{2m-(2+k+k^{\prime})}\right]

\displaystyle=3\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa_{l}m}\right\rangle^{\frac{l_{2}}{4\kappa_{l}m}}G_{1,m}^{l_{1}-2-k-k^{\prime}}G_{2,m}^{2m-(2+k+k^{\prime})},

(S.257)

where we use the definitions for
G_{1,m}
and
G_{2,m}
in Eq. (
S.249
).

By applying the inequality (
S.257
) to Eq. (
S.252
), we derive

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left[\left[\phi^{l_{1}},\pi^{m}\right],\pi^{m}\right]\Phi_{0}^{l_{2}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq 3m^{2}\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m}\right\rangle^{\frac{l_{2}}{4\kappa m}}\sum_{s=0}^{l_{1}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\min(s,m-1)}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{l_{1}-2-k}\sum_{k^{\prime}=0}^{\min(s^{\prime},m-1)}C_{k,s,m-1}C_{k^{\prime},s^{\prime},m-1}G_{1,m}^{l_{1}-2-k-k^{\prime}}G_{2,m}^{2m-(2+k+k^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq 3m^{2}\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m}\right\rangle^{\frac{l_{2}}{4\kappa m}}G_{1,m}^{l_{1}-2}G_{2,m}^{2m-2}\left(\sum_{s=0}^{l_{1}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\min(s,m-1)}C_{k,s,m-1}(G_{1,m}G_{2,m})^{-k}\right)^{2},

(S.258)

where we use that
C_{k,m,n}
monotonically increases with
m
and
n
from the definition (
S.205
), i.e.,
C_{k,m,n}:=k!\binom{m}{k}\binom{n}{k}
.
We calculate the summation as

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{l_{1}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\min(s,m-1)}C_{k,s,m-1}(G_{1,m}G_{2,m})^{-k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{l_{1}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\min(s,m-1)}k!\binom{s}{k}\binom{m-1}{k}(G_{1,m}G_{2,m})^{-k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=0}^{l_{1}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\min(s,m-1)}\binom{s}{k}m^{k}\left(\frac{1}{16c_{1}^{2}\kappa_{l}^{2}m^{2}}\right)^{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=0}^{l_{1}-1}\left(1+\frac{1}{16c_{1}^{2}\kappa_{l}^{2}m}\right)^{s}

\displaystyle=16c_{1}^{2}\kappa_{l}^{2}m\left[\left(1+\frac{1}{16c_{1}^{2}\kappa_{l}^{2}m}\right)^{l_{1}}-1\right]\leq 2l_{1},

(S.259)

where, in the last inequality, we use the following upper bound from
c_{1}\geq 1
,
\kappa_{l}=\max(1,l/2-1)
, and
l\geq 3
:

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{1}{16c_{1}^{2}\kappa_{l}^{2}m}\right)^{l_{1}}-1\leq\frac{2l_{1}}{16c_{1}^{2}\kappa_{l}^{2}m}.

(S.260)

Note that
(1+1/y)^{x}-1\leq 2x/y
for
x\geq 0
as long as
y\geq x
.
By combining the inequalities (
S.258
) and (
S.259
), we reach the main inequality (
S.248
).
This completes the proof of Lemma
21
.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
21
]

Supplementary Note 5.6.2
Proof of Subtheorem
1

In the proof, we treat the moment of the
\pi_{1}
operator at the site
1
.
We then analyze the variance of
\pi_{1}^{m}
using the trade-off inequality (
S.208
) as follows:

\displaystyle{\rm Var}(\pi_{1}^{m})\cdot\Delta

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left|\langle\Omega|\left[\left[H,\pi_{1}^{m}\right],\pi_{1}^{m}\right]|\Omega\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k/2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}\in\Lambda\\
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}\}\ni 1\end{subarray}}|f_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}}|\left|\langle\Omega|\left[\left[\phi_{i_{1}}\phi_{i_{2}}\cdots\phi_{i_{2k_{1}}},\pi_{1}^{m}\right],\pi_{1}^{m}\right]|\Omega\rangle\right|.

(S.261)

For simplicity, we consider the quantity of

\displaystyle\left|\langle\Omega|\left[\left[\phi_{1}^{l_{1}}\phi_{2}^{t_{2}}\cdots\phi_{j}^{t_{j}},\pi_{1}^{m}\right],\pi_{1}^{m}\right]|\Omega\rangle\right|=\left|\langle\Omega|\left[\left[\phi_{1}^{l_{1}},\pi_{1}^{m}\right],\pi_{1}^{m}\right]\phi_{2}^{t_{2}}\phi_{3}^{t_{3}}\cdots\phi_{j}^{t_{j}}|\Omega\rangle\right|

(S.262)

for an arbitrary positive integer
j
, where
l_{1}+t_{2}+\cdots+t_{j}=2k_{1}
is assumed.
By denoting
\Phi_{0}
as

\displaystyle\Phi_{0}=\phi_{2}^{t_{2}}\phi_{3}^{t_{3}}\cdots\phi_{j}^{t_{j}},

(S.263)

we rewrite Eq. (
S.262
) as

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\left[\left[\phi_{1}^{l_{1}}\phi_{2}^{t_{2}}\cdots\phi_{j}^{t_{j}},\pi_{1}^{m}\right],\pi_{1}^{m}\right]|\Omega\rangle=\langle\Omega|\left[\left[\phi_{1}^{l_{1}},\pi_{1}^{m}\right],\pi_{1}^{m}\right]\Phi_{0}|\Omega\rangle.

(S.264)

We aim to estimate the upper bound of the above expectation value.

In order to apply Lemma
21
to the estimation of Eq. (
S.264
),
we use Proposition
16
to obtain

\displaystyle\left|\langle\Omega|\phi_{i}^{2k_{1}}|\Omega\rangle\right|\leq\left(\check{c}_{1}k_{1}\right)^{2k_{1}}\quad\textrm{for}\quad\forall i\in\Lambda

(S.265)

with

\displaystyle\check{c}_{1}=2\max_{i\in\Lambda}\left|\langle\Omega|\phi_{i}|\Omega\rangle\right|+4\left(\frac{\bar{\mu}}{\Delta}\right)^{1/2}+1,

(S.266)

where we add
+1
in Eq. (
S.266
) to ensure
\check{c}_{1}\geq 1
.
Now, we set
l_{2}=1
and
l=l_{1}+l_{2}\leq 2k_{1}
in Lemma
21
, which makes
\kappa_{l}\leq(l-2)/2\leq k_{1}
.
Then, the inequality (
S.248
) in Lemma
21
yields

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\left[\left[\phi_{1}^{l_{1}}\phi_{2}^{t_{2}}\cdots\phi_{j}^{t_{j}},\pi_{1}^{m}\right],\pi_{1}^{m}\right]|\Omega\rangle\leq 3\langle\Omega|\Phi_{0}^{4k_{1}m}|\Omega\rangle^{1/(4k_{1}m)}(4k_{1}m)^{2}\tilde{G}_{1,m}^{l_{1}-2}\tilde{G}_{2,m}^{2m-2}

(S.267)

where we set
c_{1}\to\check{c}_{1}
,
\kappa_{l}\to k_{1}
in Eq. (
S.249
) and define

\displaystyle\tilde{G}_{1,m}=4\check{c}_{1}k_{1}m,\and\tilde{G}_{2,m}=\max\left(\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle^{1/(2m)},4\check{c}_{1}k_{1}m\right).

(S.268)

Note that the RHS of (
S.248
) monotonically increases with
\kappa_{l}
.

Furthermore, using the Hölder inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\Phi_{0}^{4k_{1}m}|\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\Omega|\phi_{2}^{4t_{2}k_{1}m}\phi_{3}^{4t_{3}k_{1}m}\cdots\phi_{j}^{4t_{j}k_{1}m}|\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\langle\Omega|\phi_{2}^{4(2k_{1}-l_{1})k_{1}m}|\Omega\rangle^{\frac{t_{2}}{2k_{1}-l_{1}}}\cdot\langle\Omega|\phi_{3}^{4(2k_{1}-l_{1})k_{1}m}|\Omega\rangle^{\frac{t_{3}}{2k_{1}-l_{1}}}\cdots\langle\Omega|\phi_{j}^{4(2k_{1}-l_{1})k_{1}m}|\Omega\rangle^{\frac{t_{j}}{2k_{1}-l_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left[2\check{c}_{1}(2k_{1}-l_{1})k_{1}m\right]^{4t_{2}k_{1}m}\cdot\left[2\check{c}_{1}(2k_{1}-l_{1})k_{1}m\right]^{4t_{3}k_{1}m}\cdots\left[2\check{c}_{1}(2k_{1}-l_{1})k_{1}m\right]^{4t_{j}k_{1}m}

\displaystyle\leq\left(4\check{c}_{1}k_{1}^{2}m\right)^{4k_{1}m(2k_{1}-l_{1})},

(S.269)

where we use the inequality (
S.265
) and
t_{2}+t_{3}+\cdots+t_{j}=2k_{1}-l_{1}\leq 2k_{1}
.
By applying the inequality (
S.269
) to (
S.267
), we have

\displaystyle|\langle\Omega|\left[\left[\phi_{1}^{l_{1}}\phi_{2}^{t_{2}}\cdots\phi_{j}^{t_{j}},\pi_{1}^{m}\right],\pi_{1}^{m}\right]|\Omega\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq 3\left(4\check{c}_{1}k_{1}^{2}m\right)^{2k_{1}-l_{1}}(4k_{1}m)^{2}\tilde{G}_{1,m}^{l_{1}-2}\tilde{G}_{2,m}^{2m-2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\Delta}{16\bar{\mu}}\left(4\check{c}_{1}k_{1}^{2}m\right)^{2k_{1}}\max\left(\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle^{1-1/m},\left(4\check{c}_{1}k_{1}^{2}m\right)^{2m-2}\right),

(S.270)

where we use
\check{c}_{1}\geq 4\left(\bar{\mu}/\Delta\right)^{1/2}
from Eq. (
S.223
) to derive

\displaystyle(4k_{1}m)^{2}\tilde{G}_{1,m}^{-2}=\frac{1}{\check{c}_{1}^{2}}\leq\frac{\Delta}{16\bar{\mu}}.

(S.271)

From the upper bound (
S.270
), we upper-bound

|\langle\Omega|\phi_{i_{1}}\phi_{i_{2}}\cdots\phi_{i_{2k_{1}}}|\Omega\rangle|
as

\displaystyle|\langle\Omega|\left[\left[\phi_{i_{1}}\phi_{i_{2}}\cdots\phi_{i_{2k_{1}}},\pi_{1}^{m}\right],\pi_{1}^{m}\right]|\Omega\rangle|\leq\frac{3\Delta}{16\bar{\mu}}\left(4\check{c}_{1}k_{1}^{2}m\right)^{2k_{1}}\max\left(\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle^{1-1/m},\left(4\check{c}_{1}k_{1}^{2}m\right)^{2m-2}\right).

(S.272)

By applying it to the inequality (
S.261
), we have

\displaystyle{\rm Var}(\pi_{1}^{m})\cdot\Delta

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\Delta}{16\bar{\mu}}\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k/2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}\in\Lambda\\
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}\}\ni 1\end{subarray}}\left(4\check{c}_{1}k_{1}^{2}m\right)^{2k_{1}}\max\left(\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle^{1-1/m},\left(4\check{c}_{1}k_{1}^{2}m\right)^{2m-2}\right)|f_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\bar{f}\Delta}{16\bar{\mu}}\cdot\frac{(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m)^{2}}{(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m)^{2}-1}\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{k}\max\left(\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle^{1-1/m},\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{2m-2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\bar{f}\Delta}{\bar{\mu}}\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{k}\max\left(\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle^{1-1/m},\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{2m-2}\right),

(S.273)

where we use the definition (
S.200
) for
\bar{f}
, which we reshow as follows:

\displaystyle\bar{f}=\max_{i\in\Lambda}\left(\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k/2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}\in\Lambda\\
\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}\}\ni i\end{subarray}}\left|f_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{2k_{1}}}\right|\right).

(S.274)

From the inequality (
S.273
), we derive

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle\leq\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{m}|\Omega\rangle^{2}+\frac{\bar{f}}{\bar{\mu}}\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{k}\max\left(\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle^{1-1/m},\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{2m-2}\right).

(S.275)

As in the proof of Proposition
16
, we use the induction method to derive the target inequality (
S.222
), which we show again as follows:

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{s}|\Omega\rangle\leq\left[\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}}{\bar{\mu}}\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}s\right)^{k}\right]^{s/2}

(S.276)

with
\bar{f}^{\prime}=\max(\bar{f},\bar{\mu}/2)
.
We begin with
s=1
. The Hamiltonian is invariant under
\pi_{i}\to-\pi_{i}
for
\forall i\in\Lambda
, and hence
\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}|\Omega\rangle=0
.
For
s=2
, from the inequality (
S.275
) with
\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}|\Omega\rangle=0
and
m=1
, we obtain
the inequality (
S.222
) as follows:

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2}|\Omega\rangle\leq\frac{\bar{f}\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}\right)^{k}}{\bar{\mu}}\leq\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}(2\check{c}_{1}k^{2})^{k}}{\bar{\mu}}.

(S.277)

We assume the inequality up to
s=2m-2
and consider the cases of
s=2m-1,2m
.
We begin with the case of
s=2m
.
In the inequality (
S.275
), for the case where
\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle^{1-1/m}\leq\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{2m-2}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle^{1-1/m}\leq\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{2m-2}

\displaystyle\quad\longrightarrow\quad\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle\leq\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{2m}\leq\left[\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}}{\bar{\mu}}\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}\cdot 2m\right)^{k}\right]^{m},

(S.278)

which immediately yields the main inequality (
S.276
), where we use
2\bar{f}^{\prime}\geq\bar{\mu}
and
k\geq 2
.
We therefore consider the case of
\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle^{1-1/m}>\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{2m-2}
, and the inequality (
S.275
) reduces to

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle\leq\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{m}|\Omega\rangle^{2}+\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{k}}{\bar{\mu}}\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle^{1-1/m}.

(S.279)

We rewrite it as

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle^{1/m}\leq\frac{\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{m}|\Omega\rangle^{2}}{\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle^{1-1/m}}+\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{k}}{\bar{\mu}}\leq\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{m}|\Omega\rangle^{2/m}+\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{k}}{\bar{\mu}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle\leq\left(\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{m}|\Omega\rangle^{2/m}+\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{k}}{\bar{\mu}}\right)^{m},

(S.280)

where we use
\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{m}|\Omega\rangle\leq\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle^{1/2}
.
Using the inequality (
S.276
) for
\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{m}|\Omega\rangle
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{k}}{\bar{\mu}}+\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}\left(\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{k}}{\bar{\mu}}\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=\left[2^{-k+1}\cdot\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}}{\bar{\mu}}\left(2\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{k}\right]^{m}\leq\left[\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}}{\bar{\mu}}\left(2\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{k}\right]^{m},

(S.281)

where we use
k\geq 2
in the last inequality.

Finally, we consider the case of
s=2m-1
, which is upper-bounded in the similar way to (
S.221
) as follows:

\displaystyle\left|\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m-1}|\Omega\rangle\right|\leq\langle\Omega|\pi_{1}^{2m}|\Omega\rangle^{1-1/(2m)}

\displaystyle\leq\left[2^{-k+1}\cdot\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}}{\bar{\mu}}\left(2\check{c}_{1}k^{2}m\right)^{k}\right]^{m-1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left[2^{-k+1}\cdot\left(\frac{2m}{2m-1}\right)^{k}\right]^{m-1/2}\left\{\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}}{\bar{\mu}}\left[\check{c}_{1}k^{2}\left(2m-1\right)\right]^{k}\right\}^{m-1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\frac{\bar{f}^{\prime}}{\bar{\mu}}\left[\check{c}_{1}k^{2}\left(2m-1\right)\right]^{k}\right\}^{m-1/2},

(S.282)

where, in the second inequality, we use the upper bound (
S.281
), and in the last inequality, we use

\displaystyle 2^{-k+1}\cdot\left(\frac{2m}{2m-1}\right)^{k}\leq\frac{8}{9}\quad\textrm{for}\quad k\geq 2,\quad m\geq 2.

(S.283)

This completes the proof of Subtheorem
1
.
\square

Supplementary Note 5.7
Proof of Proposition
20

Supplementary Note 5.7.1
Strategy and roadmap.

We briefly outline the structure of the proof of Proposition
20

before presenting the detailed arguments.
The proposition provides an upper bound on mixed expectation values of the form
\langle\pi^{2m-s}\,\phi^{s^{\prime}}\,\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}}\rangle
, under the moment assumption on
\phi
stated explicitly in the proposition.
The proof proceeds by induction on the moment order
m
and is designed to control such
expressions in terms of the top moment
\langle\pi^{2m}\rangle
,
despite the fact that
\pi
and
\phi
do not commute.

The argument is based on a recursive reduction of the power of
\pi
appearing inside the
expectation value.
The starting point is a Cauchy-Schwarz estimate that extracts one factor of

\langle\pi^{2m}\rangle
, leaving an expectation in which powers of
\pi
are interlaced
with powers of
\phi
.
At this stage,
\pi
must be moved past
\phi
.
Because of non-commutativity, commuting
\pi
through
\phi
produces additional terms
arising from commutators.
Iterating this procedure naturally leads to a decomposition into a main contribution,
in which the
\pi
-power is reduced in a direct way, and a collection of error terms
generated by commutators.

To organize this structure, we introduce a recursive family of quantities.
At recursion depth
p
, the main term is written as

L_{p}=|\langle\pi^{2m-s_{p}}\,\phi^{s^{\prime}_{p}}\,\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}\rangle|
,
where the exponent
s_{p}
measures how much of the original
\pi
-power has been reduced.
All additional terms generated by commutators at the previous step are grouped into a second
quantity
K_{p}
, which consists of a finite linear combination of expectation values of the form

\langle\pi^{2m-s_{p}-k}\,\phi^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k}\,\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}\rangle
with
k\geq 1
.
Thus,
L_{p}
represents the principal contribution at scale
p
, while
K_{p}
collects all
error terms that arise purely from the non-commutativity of
\pi
and
\phi
.
The exponents
s_{p},s^{\prime}_{p},s^{\prime\prime}_{p}
are chosen to grow dyadically with
p
, reflecting the repeated
use of Cauchy-Schwarz and ensuring that the remaining power of
\pi
decreases rapidly.

With these definitions, one obtains a universal recursive inequality of the form

L_{p}\leq\langle\pi^{2m}\rangle^{1/2}\,\bigl(L_{p+1}+K_{p+1}\bigr)^{1/2}
,
valid as long as the remaining power of
\pi
is positive.
This recursion is iterated until the
\pi
-power becomes sufficiently small, at which point a direct Cauchy-Schwarz estimate closes the bound.
The induction hypothesis on
m
is used to control the expectations appearing in
K_{p}
,
which always involve strictly smaller total powers of
\pi
.

To compare the resulting bounds obtained at different recursion depths,
we introduce a reference quantity
W_{m,p}
.
The quantity
W_{m,p}
is constructed so as to capture the maximal size that an expression
of the form
L_{p}
or
K_{p}
can attain under the induction hypothesis.
It combines the dependence on the top moment
\langle\pi^{2m}\rangle
,
explicit polynomial factors in
m
originating from the assumed bounds on
\phi
,
and the contribution of
\Phi_{0}
into a single expression of fixed functional form.

Lemma
22
establishes that the reference quantities
W_{m,p}

are uniformly controlled by the base quantity
W_{m,0}
for all recursion depths
p
.
This result shows that the dyadic recursion does not amplify constants and allows all
bounds obtained at intermediate scales to be compared directly with the final target bound.

Lemma
23
provides a quantitative estimate on the commutator contributions

K_{p}
.
It shows that each
K_{p}
is suppressed relative to
W_{m,p}
by an explicit factor,
so that commutator-generated terms never dominate the recursive scheme.
This estimate is the key ingredient that prevents the accumulation of errors
arising from non-commutativity.

Together, Lemmas
22
and
23
imply that both the main terms

L_{p}
and the commutator terms
K_{p}
remain uniformly controlled throughout the recursion.
As a consequence, all branches of the recursive argument are bounded by a fixed multiple
of
W_{m,0}
, allowing the induction on
m
to close and yielding the bound stated in
Proposition
20
.

Supplementary Note 5.7.2
Proof.

Throughout the proof, we often use the inequality of

\displaystyle\left\langle O^{2m}\right\rangle\leq\left\langle O^{2m^{\prime}}\right\rangle^{m/m^{\prime}}\quad\textrm{for}\quad m^{\prime}\geq m,

(S.284)

which gives
\left\langle O^{2m}\right\rangle^{1/(2m)}\leq\left\langle O^{2m^{\prime}}\right\rangle^{1/(2m^{\prime})}
.

We prove the statement by induction.
For
m=1
, the inequality is trivially satisfied from

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\pi^{2-s}\phi^{s^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\left\langle\pi^{2}\right\rangle^{1/2}\left\langle\phi^{2s^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{2s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle^{1/2}&\quad\textrm{for}\quad s=1,\\
\left\langle\phi^{2s^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{2s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle^{1/2}&\quad\textrm{for}\quad s=2,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\left\langle\pi^{2m}\right\rangle^{1-\frac{s}{2m}}\left(2c_{1}\kappa m\right)^{s^{\prime}}\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa m}}&\quad\textrm{for}\quad s=1,\\
\left(2c_{1}\kappa m\right)^{s^{\prime}}\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa m}}&\quad\textrm{for}\quad s=2,\end{cases}

(S.285)

where, in the first inequality, we use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and in the second inequality,
we use the Hölder inequality with
\tilde{s}=s^{\prime}+s^{\prime\prime}\leq\kappa s\leq 2\kappa m
(note that
s\leq 2m
):

\displaystyle\left\langle\phi^{2s^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{2s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle\phi^{2\tilde{s}}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime}}{\tilde{s}}}\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{2\tilde{s}}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{s}}}\leq\left(2c_{1}\kappa m\right)^{2s^{\prime}}\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{2\kappa m}}.

(S.286)

Note that we have assume
\left\langle\phi^{2s}\right\rangle\leq(c_{1}s)^{2s}
and the inequality (
S.284
) was used for
\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{2\tilde{s}}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{s}}}
.

We then assume the target inequality for
m\leq m_{0}-1
:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\pi^{2m-s}\phi^{s^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle\right|\leq\zeta\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa m}}\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m}\right\rangle^{1-\frac{s}{2m}}\left(4c_{1}\kappa m\right)^{s^{\prime}},\left(4c_{1}\kappa m\right)^{2m+s^{\prime}-s}\right],

(S.287)

where
\zeta
is proven to be chosen as
\zeta=3
afterward [see the discussion below the inequality (
S.311
)].
We aim to prove the case of
m=m_{0}\geq 2
under the assumption of (
S.287
).

For this purpose, we take the following two steps. In the first step, we will prove the inequality of

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-1-s}\phi^{s^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle\right|\leq\zeta\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa m_{0}}}\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-\frac{s+1}{2m_{0}}}\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{s^{\prime}},\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{2m_{0}+s^{\prime}-(s+1)}\right]

(S.288)

for
s\in[1,2m_{0}-1]
and
s^{\prime}+s^{\prime\prime}\leq\kappa s
.
This inequality can be proved based on the assumption of (
S.287
) for
m\leq m_{0}-1
.

In order to reach the target inequality (i.e., the inequality (
S.287
) with
m=m_{0}
), we utilize the above inequality (
S.288
) and derive

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-s}\phi^{s^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle\right|\leq\zeta\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa m_{0}}}\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-\frac{s}{2m_{0}}}\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{s^{\prime}},\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{2m_{0}+s^{\prime}-s}\right]

(S.289)

for
s\in[1,2m_{0}]
and
s^{\prime}+s^{\prime\prime}\leq\kappa s
.

In the following, we aim to prove the inequality (
S.288
), but the same analyses will be applied to the proof of (
S.289
).
We first consider the case of
s+1\geq m_{0}
, which gives
2[2m_{0}-(s+1)]\leq 2m_{0}
.
In this case, we immediately obtain from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-(s+1)}\phi^{s^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle\pi^{2[2m_{0}-(s+1)]}\right\rangle^{1/2}\left\langle\phi^{2s^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{2s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{2m_{0}-(s+1)}{2m_{0}}}\left[\left(2c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{2s^{\prime}}\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{2\kappa m_{0}}}\right]^{1/2},

(S.290)

where we use the inequality (
S.286
) in the last inequality.
This reduces to the inequality (
S.288
).

We next consider the case of
s+1<m_{0}
.
We start with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality as

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-(s+1)}\phi^{s^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle\right|\leq\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1/2}\left\langle\phi^{s^{\prime}}\pi^{2m_{0}-2-2s}\phi^{s^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{2s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle^{1/2}\quad\textrm{for}\quad s+1<m_{0}.

(S.291)

We then apply the relation (
S.207
) to
\phi^{s^{\prime}}\pi^{2m_{0}-2s-2}
and obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle\phi^{s^{\prime}}\pi^{2m_{0}-2s-2}\phi^{s^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{2s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-2s-2}\phi^{2s^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{2s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle+\sum_{k=1}^{\min(2m_{0}-2s-2,s)}i^{k}C_{k,2m_{0}-2s-2,s^{\prime}}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-2s-2-k}\phi^{2s^{\prime}-k}\Phi_{0}^{2s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle.

(S.292)

We here define

\displaystyle s_{p}:=2^{p}(s+1),\quad s^{\prime}_{p}:=2^{p}s^{\prime},\and s^{\prime\prime}_{p}=2^{p}s^{\prime\prime}.

(S.293)

Then, by defining
L_{p}
and
K_{p}
as

\displaystyle L_{p}:=\left|\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-s_{p}}\phi^{s^{\prime}_{p}}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}\right\rangle\right|,

\displaystyle K_{p}:=\sum_{k=1}^{\min(2m_{0}-s_{p},s^{\prime}_{p-1})}i^{k}C_{k,2m_{0}-s_{p},s^{\prime}_{p-1}}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-s_{p}-k}\phi^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}\right\rangle,

(S.294)

we reduce the inequality (
S.291
) to

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-\tilde{s}}\phi^{s^{\prime}_{0}}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{0}}\right\rangle\right|=L_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1/2}\left(L_{1}+K_{1}\right)^{1/2}.

(S.295)

By generalizing the above inequality, we can also derive

\displaystyle L_{p}\leq\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1/2}\left(L_{p+1}+K_{p+1}\right)^{1/2}\quad\textrm{for}\quad s_{p}<m_{0}.

(S.296)

In the following, we define
W_{m,p}
as follows:

\displaystyle W_{m,p}:=\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}{4\kappa m}}\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m}\right\rangle^{1-\frac{s_{p}}{2m}}\left(4c_{1}\kappa m\right)^{s^{\prime}_{p}},\left(4c_{1}\kappa m\right)^{2m+s^{\prime}_{p}-s_{p}}\right].

(S.297)

Note that the
W_{m,0}
corresponds to the RHS of the target inequality (
S.288
) from the definition of (
S.293
).

To analyze the recursive relation (
S.296
), we define
p_{0}
as the minimum non-negative integer that satisfies

\displaystyle L_{p}>K_{p}\quad\textrm{for}\quad p\leq p_{0},\quad L_{p_{0}+1}\leq K_{p_{0}+1},

(S.298)

where, in particular for
p_{0}=0
, we do not require
L_{0}>K_{0}
and only the condition
L_{p+1}\leq K_{p+1}
is satisfied for all
p
.
We first consider the case where
p_{0}=0
. In this case, the inequality (
S.295
) gives

\displaystyle L_{0}\leq\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1/2}\left(L_{1}+K_{1}\right)^{1/2}\leq 2^{1/2}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1/2}K_{1}^{1/2}\quad\textrm{for}\quad p_{0}=0.

(S.299)

When
p_{0}\geq 1
, we prove the following inequality for arbitrary
p_{1}<p_{0}
:

\displaystyle L_{0}\leq a_{p_{1}}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p_{1}+1}}\left(L_{p_{1}+1}\right)^{1/2^{p_{1}+1}},\quad a_{p_{1}}=\prod_{s=0}^{p_{1}}\left(1+\frac{1}{2^{s+1}}\right).

(S.300)

Note that
L_{p_{1}}>K_{p_{1}}

(p_{1}<p_{0})
holds from the definition (
S.298
) for
p_{0}
.
We rely on the induction method. First, for
p_{1}=0
, the inequality (
S.300
) is derived from (
S.295
) as follows:

\displaystyle L_{0}\leq\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1/2}\left(L_{1}+K_{1}\right)^{1/2}\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\right)\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1/2}L_{1}^{1/2},

(S.301)

where we use the following general inequality for
x\geq y
with
\alpha=1/2
:

\displaystyle\left(x+y\right)^{\alpha}=x^{\alpha}\left(1+\frac{y}{x}\right)^{\alpha}\leq x^{\alpha}\left(1+\alpha\cdot\frac{y}{x}\right)\leq(1+\alpha)x^{\alpha}\quad(0<\alpha<1).

(S.302)

By assuming the inequality (
S.300
) up to
p=p_{1}-1
(
p_{1}<p_{0}
), we obtain

\displaystyle L_{0}

\displaystyle\leq a_{p_{1}-1}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p_{1}}}\left(L_{p_{1}}\right)^{1/2^{p_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq a_{p_{1}-1}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p_{1}}}\left(\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1/2}\left(L_{p_{1}+1}+K_{p_{1}+1}\right)^{1/2}\right)^{1/2^{p_{1}}}

\displaystyle=a_{p_{1}-1}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p_{1}+1}}\left(L_{p_{1}+1}+K_{p_{1}+1}\right)^{1/2^{p_{1}+1}}

\displaystyle\leq a_{p_{1}-1}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p_{1}+1}}\left(1+\frac{1}{2^{p_{1}+1}}\right)\left(L_{p_{1}+1}\right)^{1/2^{p_{1}+1}}=a_{p_{1}}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p_{1}+1}}\left(L_{p_{1}+1}\right)^{1/2^{p_{1}+1}},

(S.303)

where, in the third inequality, we use the relation (
S.302
) with the condition of
L_{p_{1}+1}>K_{p_{1}+1}
for
p_{1}<p_{0}
(or
p_{1}+1\leq p_{0}
).
This completes the proof of the inequality (
S.300
).

In the following, we define the integer
\bar{p}
(
\geq 0
) as

\displaystyle s_{\bar{p}}=2^{\bar{p}}(s+1)<m_{0}\leq 2^{\bar{p}+1}(s+1)=s_{\bar{p}+1}<2m_{0}.

(S.304)

We then separate the cases of
\bar{p}<p_{0}
and
\bar{p}\geq p_{0}
.

For
\bar{p}<p_{0}
, we use the inequality (
S.300
) with
p_{1}=\bar{p}<p_{0}
, we obtain

\displaystyle L_{0}\leq a_{\bar{p}}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{\bar{p}+1}}\left(L_{\bar{p}+1}\right)^{1/2^{\bar{p}+1}}.

(S.305)

Then, by using the inequality (
S.290
) and
m_{0}\leq s_{\bar{p}+1}<2m_{0}
, we obtain

\displaystyle L_{\bar{p}+1}=\left|\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-s_{\bar{p}+1}}\phi^{s^{\prime}_{\bar{p}+1}}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{p}+1}}\right\rangle\right|\leq W_{m_{0},\bar{p}+1},

(S.306)

which reduces the inequality (
S.305
) to

\displaystyle L_{0}\leq a_{\bar{p}}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{\bar{p}+1}}\left(W_{m_{0},\bar{p}+1}\right)^{1/2^{\bar{p}+1}}.

(S.307)

For
\bar{p}\geq p_{0}
, we use the inequality (
S.300
) with
p_{1}=p_{0}-1
, we obtain

\displaystyle L_{0}\leq a_{p_{0}-1}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p_{0}}}\left(L_{p_{0}}\right)^{1/2^{p_{0}}}.

(S.308)

Because of
s_{p_{0}+1}<2m_{0}
from
s_{p_{0}}\leq s_{\bar{p}}<m_{0}
, the inequality (
S.296
) gives

\displaystyle\left(L_{p_{0}}\right)^{1/2^{p_{0}}}\leq\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1/2^{p_{0}+1}}\left[\left(L_{p_{0}+1}+K_{p_{0}+1}\right)^{1/2}\right]^{1/2^{p_{0}}}.

(S.309)

By applying the above inequality to (
S.308
), we derive

\displaystyle L_{0}

\displaystyle\leq a_{p_{0}-1}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p_{0}}}\cdot\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1/2^{p_{0}+1}}\left[\left(L_{p_{0}+1}+K_{p_{0}+1}\right)^{1/2}\right]^{1/2^{p_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{1/2^{p_{0}+1}}a_{p_{0}-1}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p_{0}+1}}\left(K_{p_{0}+1}\right)^{1/2^{p_{0}+1}},

(S.310)

where, in the second inequality, we use
L_{p_{0}+1}\leq K_{p_{0}+1}
from the definition (
S.298
) of
p_{0}
.

Therefore, by combining the inequalities (
S.299
), (
S.307
) and (
S.310
), we obtain

\displaystyle L_{0}

\displaystyle\leq 3\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1/2}K_{1}^{1/2},\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{\bar{p}+1}}\left(W_{m_{0},\bar{p}+1}\right)^{1/2^{\bar{p}+1}},\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p_{0}+1}}\left(K_{p_{0}+1}\right)^{1/2^{p_{0}+1}}\right],

(S.311)

where we use the inequality of

\displaystyle 2^{1/2^{p+1}}a_{p-1}\leq 2.38423\cdots<3\quad\textrm{for}\quad\forall p\in\mathbb{N}.

(S.312)

To further reduce the upper bound to the desired form as in (
S.246
), we prove the following two lemmas:

Lemma 22
.

For an arbitrary
s_{p}
such that
s_{p}\leq 2m_{0}
, we prove the following upper bound:

\displaystyle\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p}}\left(W_{m_{0},p}\right)^{1/2^{p}}\leq W_{m_{0},0}.

(S.313)

We recall that
s_{p}:=2^{p}(s+1)
and
s^{\prime}_{p}:=2^{p}s^{\prime}
as in Eq. (
S.293
).

Lemma 23
.

The quantity
K_{p}
in Eq. (
S.294
) is upper-bounded as follows:

\displaystyle K_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\zeta W_{m_{0},p}\left(e^{1/(8c_{1}^{2}\kappa)}-1\right)\leq\frac{\zeta}{7c_{1}^{2}\kappa}W_{m_{0},p}.

(S.314)

We recall that
\zeta
is the control parameter that was adopted in the inequality (
S.287
).

By applying Lemmas
22
and
23
to the inequality (
S.311
), we obtain

\displaystyle L_{0}

\displaystyle\leq 3W_{m_{0},0}\max\left[\left(\frac{\zeta}{7c_{1}^{2}\kappa}\right)^{1/2},1,\left(\frac{\zeta}{7c_{1}^{2}\kappa}\right)^{1/2^{p_{0}+1}}\right].

(S.315)

Because of the assumption of
c_{1}\geq 1
and
\kappa\geq 1
, we have
\zeta/(7c_{1}^{2}\kappa)\leq\zeta/7\leq 1
for
\zeta\leq 7
.
Therefore, by choosing
\zeta=3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\max\left[\left(\frac{\zeta}{7c_{1}^{2}\kappa}\right)^{1/2},1,\left(\frac{\zeta}{7c_{1}^{2}\kappa}\right)^{1/2^{p_{0}+1}}\right]=1,

(S.316)

which reduces (
S.315
) to the target inequality (
S.288
).

To derive the second inequality (
S.289
).
We follow the same analytical processes.
For the case of
s\geq m_{0}
, using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality as in (
S.290
),
we obtain the inequality (
S.289
).

For the case of
s<m_{0}
,
the inequality (
S.291
) is replaced by

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-s}\phi^{s^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle\right|\leq\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1/2}\left\langle\phi^{s^{\prime}}\pi^{2m_{0}-2s}\phi^{s^{\prime}}\Phi_{0}^{2s^{\prime\prime}}\right\rangle^{1/2}\quad\textrm{for}\quad s<m_{0}.

(S.317)

Then, by redefining

\displaystyle s_{p}:=2^{p}s,\quad s^{\prime}_{p}:=2^{p}s^{\prime},\and s^{\prime\prime}_{p}=2^{p}s^{\prime\prime},

(S.318)

we can derive the same inequality as (
S.296
) by redefining
L_{p}
and
K_{p}
using the above
s_{p}
in Eq. (
S.294
):

\displaystyle L_{p}\leq\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1/2}\left(L_{p+1}+K_{p+1}\right)^{1/2}\quad\textrm{for}\quad s_{p}<m_{0}.

(S.319)

The remaining parts are the same in the proof for the first inequality (
S.288
).

One difference stems from the proof of Lemma
23
.
There, we analyze
K_{p}
as

\displaystyle K_{p}:=\sum_{k=1}^{\min(2m_{0}-s_{p},s^{\prime}_{p-1})}i^{k}C_{k,2m_{0}-s_{p},s^{\prime}_{p-1}}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-s_{p}-k}\phi^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}\right\rangle,

(S.320)

and treat the expectation
\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-s_{p}-k}\phi^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}\right\rangle
.
To estimate it, we rely on the proved inequality (
S.288
) instead of the inequality (
S.287
),
By writing

\displaystyle\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-s_{p}-k}\phi^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}\right\rangle=\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-1-(s_{p}+k-1)}\phi^{(s^{\prime}_{p}-k)}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}\right\rangle,

(S.321)

we have
(s^{\prime}_{p}-k)+s^{\prime\prime}_{p}\leq\kappa\left(s_{p}+k-1\right)
because of

\displaystyle(s^{\prime}_{p}-k)+s^{\prime\prime}_{p}

\displaystyle=2^{p}(s^{\prime}+s^{\prime\prime})-k\leq 2^{p}\kappa s-k

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\left[2^{p}s+k-1\right]

\displaystyle=\kappa\left(s_{p}+k-1\right)

(S.322)

for
k\geq 1
.
This allows us to use the inequality (
S.288
) with

\displaystyle s\to s_{p}+k-1,\quad s^{\prime}\to s^{\prime}_{p}-k,\quad s^{\prime\prime}\to s^{\prime\prime}_{p},

(S.323)

which yields the same inequality as (
S.334
)

\displaystyle\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-s_{p}-k}\phi^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}\right\rangle\leq\zeta\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}{4\kappa m_{0}}}\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-\frac{s_{p}+k}{2m_{0}}}\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k},\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{2m_{0}+s^{\prime}_{p}-s_{p}-2k}\right].

(S.324)

Hence, we can apply the same analyses going forward.

The reason why we cannot directly use the inequality (
S.287
) for (
S.321
) is that the condition (
S.322
) cannot be ensured. If we rewrite

\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-s_{p}-k}\phi^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}\right\rangle=\left\langle\pi^{2(m_{0}-1)-(s_{p}+k-2)}\phi^{(s^{\prime}_{p}-k)}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}\right\rangle

and apply (
S.287
) with
m\to m_{0}-1
,
s\to s_{p}+k-2
,
s^{\prime}\to s^{\prime}_{p}-k
, and
s^{\prime\prime}\to s^{\prime\prime}_{p}
, the following condition must be satisfied:

\displaystyle s^{\prime}_{p}-k+s^{\prime\prime}_{p}\leq\kappa(s_{p}+k-2).

(S.325)

However, in the case where
s^{\prime}_{p}+s^{\prime\prime}_{p}=\kappa s_{p}
,
k=1
and
\kappa=2
, the LHS is equal to
2s_{p}-1
, which is larger than the RHS, i.e.,
\kappa(s_{p}+k-2)=2s_{p}-2
.
We thus cannot ensure the condition (
S.325
).

By obtaining the inequalities (
S.288
) and (
S.289
),
we reach the main inequality (
S.287
) with
\zeta=3
.
This completes the proof of Proposition
20
.
\square

Supplementary Note 5.7.3
Proof of Lemma
22

From the definition of
W_{m,p}
in Eq. (
S.297
), we aim to upper-bound

\displaystyle\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p}}\left(W_{m_{0},p}\right)^{1/2^{p}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p}}\left\{\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}{4\kappa m_{0}}}\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-\frac{s_{p}}{2m_{0}}}(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{s^{\prime}_{p}},(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{2m_{0}+s^{\prime}_{p}-s_{p}}\right]\right\}^{1/2^{p}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p}}\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa m_{0}}}\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2^{p}}-\frac{s+1}{2m_{0}}}(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{s^{\prime}},(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{2m_{0}/2^{p}+s^{\prime}-(s+1)}\right],

(S.326)

where, in the second equality, we use
s_{p}:=2^{p}(s+1)
,
s^{\prime}_{p}:=2^{p}s^{\prime}
and
s^{\prime\prime}_{p}=2^{p}s^{\prime\prime}
from the definition (
S.293
).
In the case of
\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle\geq(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{2m_{0}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p}}\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2^{p}}-\frac{s+1}{2m_{0}}}(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{s^{\prime}},(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{2m_{0}/2^{p}+s^{\prime}-(s+1)}\right]

\displaystyle=\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p}}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2^{p}}-\frac{s+1}{2m_{0}}}(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-\frac{s+1}{2m_{0}}}(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{s^{\prime}}.

(S.327)

On the other hand, in the case of
\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle<(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{2m_{0}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p}}\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2^{p}}-\frac{s+1}{2m_{0}}}(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{s^{\prime}},(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{2m_{0}/2^{p}+s^{\prime}-(s+1)}\right]

\displaystyle<\left[(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{2m_{0}}\right]^{1-1/2^{p}}\cdot(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{2m_{0}/2^{p}+s^{\prime}-(s+1)}

\displaystyle=(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{2m_{0}+s^{\prime}-(s+1)}.

(S.328)

By combining the upper bounds (
S.327
) and (
S.328
) with the inequality (
S.326
), we reach the upper bound of

\displaystyle\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-1/2^{p}}\left(W_{m_{0},p}\right)^{1/2^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa m_{0}}}\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1-\frac{s+1}{2m_{0}}}(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{s^{\prime}},(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{2m_{0}+s^{\prime}-(s+1)}\right]

\displaystyle=W_{m_{0},0}.

(S.329)

This completes the proof.
\square

Supplementary Note 5.7.4
Proof of Lemma
23

We first show the definition (
S.294
) for
K_{p}
again:

\displaystyle K_{p}:=\sum_{k=1}^{\min(2m_{0}-s_{p},s^{\prime}_{p-1})}i^{k}C_{k,2m_{0}-s_{p},s^{\prime}_{p-1}}\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-s_{p}-k}\phi^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}\right\rangle.

(S.330)

By writing

\displaystyle\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-s_{p}-k}\phi^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}\right\rangle=\left\langle\pi^{2(m_{0}-1)-(s_{p}+k-2)}\phi^{(s^{\prime}_{p}-k)}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}\right\rangle,

(S.331)

we have

\displaystyle(s^{\prime}_{p}-k)+s^{\prime\prime}_{p}

\displaystyle=2^{p}(s^{\prime}+s^{\prime\prime})-k\leq 2^{p}\kappa s-k

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\left[2^{p}(s+1)+k-2\right]

\displaystyle=\kappa\left(s_{p}+k-2\right)

(S.332)

for
k\geq 1
and
p\geq 0
, where we use
s^{\prime}+s^{\prime\prime}\leq\kappa s
for the condition of the inequality (
S.288
). Note that
s_{p}=2^{p}(s+1)
.

Hence, we can apply the inequality (
S.287
) with

\displaystyle m\to m_{0}-1,\quad s\to s_{p}+k-2,\quad s^{\prime}\to s^{\prime}_{p}-k,\quad s^{\prime\prime}\to s^{\prime\prime}_{p},

(S.333)

we upper-bound Eq. (
S.331
) by

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-s_{p}-k}\phi^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\zeta\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa m_{0}}}\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-2}\right\rangle^{\frac{2m_{0}-s_{p}-k}{2m_{0}-2}}\left[4c_{1}\kappa(m_{0}-1)\right]^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k},\left[4c_{1}\kappa(m_{0}-1)\right]^{2m_{0}+s^{\prime}_{p}-s_{p}-2k}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\zeta\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa m_{0}}}\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{2m_{0}-s_{p}-k}{2m_{0}}}\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k},\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{2m_{0}+s^{\prime}_{p}-s_{p}-2k}\right],

(S.334)

where, in the second inequality, we use the inequality (
S.284
) to get
\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-2}\right\rangle^{1/(2m_{0}-2)}\leq\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{1/(2m_{0})}
.
Then, in the case of
\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle\leq\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{2m_{0}}
, we have

\displaystyle\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{2m_{0}-s_{p}-k}{2m_{0}}}\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k},\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{2m_{0}+s^{\prime}_{p}-s_{p}-2k}\right]=\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{2m_{0}+s^{\prime}_{p}-s_{p}-2k}.

(S.335)

On the other hand, in the case of
\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle>\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{2m_{0}}
, the following upper bound holds:

\displaystyle\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{2m_{0}-s_{p}-k}{2m_{0}}}\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k},\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{2m_{0}+s^{\prime}_{p}-s_{p}-2k}\right]

\displaystyle=\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{2m_{0}-s_{p}}{2m_{0}}}\frac{\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k}}{\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{k/(2m_{0})}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{2m_{0}-s_{p}}{2m_{0}}}\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{s^{\prime}_{p}-2k}.

(S.336)

By applying the above two inequalities to (
S.334
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}-s_{p}-k}\phi^{s^{\prime}_{p}-k}\Phi_{0}^{s^{\prime\prime}_{p}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\zeta(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{-2k}\left\langle\Phi_{0}^{4\kappa m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa m_{0}}}\max\left[\left\langle\pi^{2m_{0}}\right\rangle^{\frac{2m_{0}-s_{p}}{2m_{0}}}\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{s^{\prime}_{p}},\left(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0}\right)^{2m_{0}+s^{\prime}_{p}-s_{p}}\right]

\displaystyle=\zeta(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{-2k}W_{m_{0},p},

(S.337)

where we use the definition (
S.297
) for
W_{m_{0},p}
.

We use the inequality (
S.337
) to upper-bound the quantity
K_{p}
in Eq. (
S.330
) in the following way:

\displaystyle K_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\zeta W_{m_{0},p}\sum_{k=1}^{\min(2m_{0}-s_{p},s^{\prime}_{p-1})}C_{k,2m_{0}-s_{p},s^{\prime}_{p-1}}(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{-2k}.

(S.338)

For the summation, we calculate

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\min(2m_{0}-s_{p},s^{\prime}_{p-1})}C_{k,2m_{0}-s_{p},s^{\prime}_{p-1}}(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{-2k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\min(2m_{0}-s_{p},s^{\prime}_{p-1})}k!\binom{2m_{0}-s_{p}}{k}\binom{s^{\prime}_{p-1}}{k}(4c_{1}\kappa m_{0})^{-2k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{s^{\prime}_{p-1}}\binom{s^{\prime}_{p-1}}{k}\left(\frac{2m_{0}}{16c_{1}^{2}\kappa^{2}m_{0}^{2}}\right)^{k}

\displaystyle=\left(1+\frac{1}{8c_{1}^{2}\kappa^{2}m_{0}}\right)^{s^{\prime}_{p-1}}-1\leq e^{1/(8c_{1}^{2}\kappa)}-1,

(S.339)

where, in the last inequality, we use
s^{\prime}_{p-1}\leq s^{\prime}_{p-1}+s^{\prime\prime}_{p-1}\leq\kappa s_{p-1}\leq\kappa m_{0}
from
s_{p}\leq 2m_{0}
.
By applying the upper bound (
S.339
) to (
S.338
), we obtain

\displaystyle K_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\zeta W_{m_{0},p}\left[e^{1/(8c_{1}^{2}\kappa)}-1\right]\leq\frac{8(e^{1/8}-1)}{8c_{1}^{2}\kappa}\zeta W_{m_{0},p}\leq\frac{\zeta}{7c_{1}^{2}\kappa}W_{m_{0},p},

(S.340)

where we use
e^{1/x}-1\leq x_{0}(e^{1/{x_{0}}}-1)/x
for
x\geq x_{0}
with
8c_{1}^{2}\kappa\geq 8
from
c_{1}\geq 1
and
\kappa\geq 1
.
We thus prove the main inequality (
S.314
).
This completes the proof.
\square

Part 2
Entanglement area law in bosonic systems with long-range interactions

Supplementary Note 6
1D entanglement area law of interacting bosons

Supplementary Note 6.1
Setup and assumptions

We consider a one-dimensional chain located on
\mathbb{Z}
*7
*7

*7

Without loss of generality, we can make Hamiltonian on a finite set
\Lambda
defined on
\mathbb{Z}
by adding zero operators.
; that is, each of the site
i
is characterized by the position
x\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We separate the total system into
L=(-\infty,0]
and
R=[1,\infty)
and consider the entanglement entropy for the ground state
|\Omega\rangle
under the assumption of the spectral gap
\Delta
.
In the following, we also assume the boson number distribution in the form of

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{i,>N}|\Omega\rangle\right\|\leq\mathfrak{c}e^{-\mathfrak{b}N^{1/\mathfrak{a}}}\quad\textrm{for}\quad\forall i\in\Lambda,

(S.341)

where
\{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{c}\}
are
\mathcal{O}(1)
constants, and
\mathfrak{b}
depends on spectral gap as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}\propto\Delta^{2\upsilon/(\mathfrak{a}k)}

(S.342)

with
\upsilon
an
\mathcal{O}(1)
constant.

We consider a general boson model with
k
-body interactions, as in

\displaystyle H=\sum_{Z:|Z|\leq k}h_{Z},

(S.343)

where
h_{Z}
consists of the boson number operator and satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|h_{Z}\Pi_{\Lambda,\leq N}\right\|\leq J_{Z}N^{k/2}\quad\textrm{for}\quad N\geq 1,

(S.344)

with

\displaystyle\max_{i,i^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{Z:Z\ni\{i,i^{\prime}\}}J_{Z}\right)\leq g\bar{J}(d_{i,i^{\prime}}).

(S.345)

We assume that
\bar{J}(0)=1
and
\bar{J}(d_{i,i^{\prime}})
decays faster than
d_{i,i^{\prime}}^{-2}
, which satisfies

\displaystyle\left(r^{2}+1\right)\bar{J}(r)\leq\frac{1}{r^{\bar{\alpha}}+1}\quad(\bar{\alpha}>0).

(S.346)

Note that we recover the condition (
S.13
) by letting
i^{\prime}=i
.

For an arbitrary operator
O
, we define the Schmidt rank
{\rm SR}(O)
as the minimum integer such that

\displaystyle O=\sum_{m=1}^{{\rm SR}(O)}O_{L,m}\otimes O_{R,m},

(S.347)

where
O_{L,m}
and
O_{R,m}
are supported on the subsystems
L
and
R
, respectively.
Also, the entanglement entropy
S_{L}(\Omega)
of the ground state
|\Omega\rangle
is defined by

\displaystyle S_{L}(\Omega):=-\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\lambda^{2}_{j}\log(\lambda^{2}_{j})

(S.348)

with the Schmidt decomposition of the ground state as

\displaystyle|\Omega\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{j}|L_{j}\rangle\otimes|R_{j}\rangle,

(S.349)

where each of the states
\{|L_{j}\rangle\}_{j}
(resp.
\{|R_{j}\rangle\}_{j}
) is supported on
L
(resp.
R
).

Supplementary Note 6.2
Main statement

The main statement here is the following theorem regarding the entanglement area law:

Theorem 3
.

Under the setup in
Supplementary Note 6.1
, the entanglement entropy for an arbitrary partition is upper-bounded as follows:

\displaystyle S_{L}(\Omega)\leq C_{0}\Delta^{-(1+2/\bar{\alpha})(\upsilon+1)}\left[\log\left(1/\Delta\right)\right]^{4+3/\bar{\alpha}+\chi(1+2/\bar{\alpha})},

(S.350)

where
\chi=k\mathfrak{a}/2
,
\upsilon
and
\bar{\alpha}
are defined in Eqs. (
S.342
) and (
S.346
), respectively, and
C_{0}
is a constant that depends only on system details.
In particular, in the case of
\upsilon=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle S_{L}(\Omega)\leq C_{0}\Delta^{-1-2/\bar{\alpha}}\left[\log\left(1/\Delta\right)\right]^{3+3/\bar{\alpha}+\chi(1+2/\bar{\alpha})}\log\log(1/\Delta).

(S.351)

Moreover, there exists a matrix product state (MPS)
|{\rm M}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\rangle
that approximates the ground state in the sense of

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm tr}_{X^{\rm c}}\left(|\Omega\rangle\langle\Omega|-|{\rm M}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\rangle\langle{\rm M}_{{\mathcal{D}}}|\right)\right\|_{1}\leq\delta|X|

(S.352)

for an arbitrary subset
X\subseteq\Lambda
, where
{\mathcal{D}}
is the bond dimension and chosen as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}=\exp\left\{C_{1}\Delta^{-(1+2/\bar{\alpha})(\upsilon+1)}\left[\log\left(1/\Delta\right)\right]^{4+3/\bar{\alpha}+\chi(1+2/\bar{\alpha})}+C_{2}\frac{\log^{\chi/2+5/2}\left[1/(\delta\Delta)\right]}{\Delta^{(\upsilon+1)/2}}\right\}.

(S.353)

Remark.

For the Bose-Hubbard classes, from Theorem
1
, the exponential decay of the boson number distribution (
S.142
) gives
\mathfrak{a}=1
and
\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c}=\mathcal{O}(1)
in (
S.341
), and hence
\upsilon=0
and
\chi=k/2
.
We thus prove the entanglement area law in the form of

\displaystyle S_{L}(\Omega)\leq C_{0}\Delta^{-1-2/\bar{\alpha}}\left[\log\left(1/\Delta\right)\right]^{3+3/\bar{\alpha}+k(1+2/\bar{\alpha})/2}\log\log(1/\Delta),

(S.354)

On the other hand, for the
\phi 4
classes, the inequality (
S.201
) in Theorem
2
gives

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{i,>x}|\Omega\rangle\right\|=\sqrt{\langle\Omega|\Pi_{i,>x}|\Omega\rangle}\leq 2e^{k/2}\exp\left(-\frac{kx^{1/k}}{16e\tilde{C}}\right),

(S.355)

which yields
\mathfrak{a}=k
,
\mathfrak{b}=k/(16e\tilde{C})\propto\Delta^{1/2}
(i.e.,
2\upsilon/(\mathfrak{a}k)=1/2
) from Eq. (
S.202
), and
\mathfrak{c}=2e^{k/2}
.
We hence obtain
\chi=k^{2}/2
and
\upsilon=k^{2}/4
, which reduces the inequality (
S.350
) to

\displaystyle S_{L}(\Omega)\leq C_{0}\Delta^{-(1+2/\bar{\alpha})(k^{2}/4+1)}\left[\log\left(1/\Delta\right)\right]^{4+3/\bar{\alpha}+k^{2}(1+2/\bar{\alpha})/2}.

(S.356)

Also, regarding the MPS approximation, a particularly important case is
\Delta=\mathcal{O}(1)
and
\delta=1/{\rm poly}(n)
, where the sufficient bond dimension is given by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}\propto\exp\left[\log^{\chi/2+5/2}\left(n\right)\right],

(S.357)

which is a quasi-polynomial form with respect to the system size.

Supplementary Note 6.3
Brief Outline of the proof strategy

In this section, we provide a high-level overview of the proof of the area law for one-dimensional systems with long-range boson-boson interactions (See Supplementary Fig.
5
). The main approach we follow is based on the approximate ground state projector (AGSP) technique, initially developed in Refs.
[
11
,
12
]

for systems with short-range interactions. This method was later extended to long-range interactions in Ref.
[
29
]
.
In this work, we further extend the method to incorporate unbounded bosonic Hamiltonians.

Supplementary Figure 5:
This figure outlines the proof of Theorem 3, culminating in an area law for bosonic systems.

The AGSP operator, denoted by
K
, approximates the ground state projector
|\Omega\rangle\langle\Omega|
. Typically, the Schmidt rank of the AGSP
K
and the precision of the approximation have a trade-off relationship (see Sec .
Supplementary Note 7.1
).
The key advantage is that if a suitable AGSP with low Schmidt rank and high precision is found, a low-entangled state that closely approximates the ground state can be obtained. This quantitative result is detailed in Lemma
24
. Therefore, the goal of the area law proof is to construct such a high-quality AGSP operator.

We begin by imposing a boson-number cut-off on each site, so as to render the local Hilbert space finite-dimensional everywhere. Importantly, the cut-off at site
x
is chosen to grow (at most) poly-logarithmically with the distance from the boundary. This ensures that sites closer to the boundary have stricter cut-offs, while those farther away allow more bosons [see Eq. (
S.367
)].
Through this truncation, we can approximately preserve both the ground state and the spectral gap. Importantly, a uniform cutoff cannot be applied since the error grows with system size. After truncation, the Hamiltonian remains unbounded in regions sufficiently far from the boundary. As we will demonstrate in Theorem
4
for energy truncation, this step yields a clear difference from the bounded Hamiltonian. The resulting effective Hamiltonian by boson-number truncations,
\bar{H}
, retains the important properties of the original ground state (see Proposition
25
).

Next, we apply an interaction truncation near the boundary. If all long-range interactions across the entire region were truncated, the modified Hamiltonian
H_{\rm t}
would differ significantly from the original
\bar{H}
, with
\|\bar{H}-H_{\rm t}\|\sim\mathcal{O}(|\Lambda|)
. This would lead to substantial changes in the ground state. Therefore, we restrict the truncation of long-range interactions to the vicinity of the boundary between subsystems
L
and
R
(see Supplementary Figure
6
). Around the boundary, we divide the neighboring region to the boundary into
q
blocks
\{B_{s}\}_{s=0}^{q+1}
, each of length
l
, such that only adjacent blocks interact. The remaining two blocks
B_{0}
and
B_{q+1}
are extended to the left and right ends, respectively.
The error
\|\bar{H}-H_{\rm t}\|
can be effectively controlled by adjusting the number of blocks
q
and their length
l
(Proposition
26
).

The third truncation involves applying an energy cutoff within the blocks mentioned above (see also Fig.
7
below). In previous studies of effective Hamiltonians
[
12
,
51
]
,
the energy cutoff was applied only to the edge blocks (
B_{0}
and
B_{q+1}
). For systems with long-range interactions, it is crucial to perform the energy cutoff to all blocks to obtain a better entanglement area law
[
29
]
.
This process transforms the Hamiltonian as
H_{\rm t}\to\tilde{H}_{\rm t}
.
In total, the original Hamiltonian is transformed as

\displaystyle H\xrightarrow{\textrm{Boson number truncation}}\bar{H}\xrightarrow{\textrm{Interaction truncation}}H_{\rm t}\xrightarrow{\textrm{Multi-energy truncation}}\tilde{H}_{\rm t}.

(S.358)

While the preservation of the ground state in bounded Hamiltonians has been well studied
[
51
]
,
unbounded Hamiltonians like
H_{\rm t}
pose additional challenges, such as divergence in imaginary time evolution, which was a key technique in Ref.
[
51
]
.
To address this, we have to avoid the divergence by carefully treating the unbounded Hamiltonian.
We here employ an alternative method developed in Ref.
[
60
]
,
which was originally used to treat Hamiltonians lacking strict
k
-locality. Consequently, we establish a modified theorem for the preservation of the ground state in
\tilde{H}_{\rm t}
(Theorem
4
in
Supplementary Note 7.4
).

With the effective Hamiltonian
\tilde{H}_{\rm t}
in hand, we can now construct the AGSP operator.
Following Ref.
[
12
]
,
we utilize Chebyshev polynomials, where the accuracy of the AGSP is roughly given by
e^{-m\sqrt{\Delta/\left\|\tilde{H}_{\rm t}\right\|}}
(Lemma
27
), where
m
is the polynomial degree and
\Delta
is the spectral gap. The Schmidt rank of the polynomial of
\tilde{H}_{\rm t}
is bounded as shown in Lemma
28
. Then, by optimizing all parameters, i.e., the boson-number truncations, the number of blocks
q+2
, the block length
l
, and the degree of the Chebyshev polynomial, we construct a suitable AGSP operator to meet the desired property in Lemma
24
. This leads to Proposition
29
, where we obtain a low-Schmidt-rank state with a large overlap with the ground state.

Finally, by applying a sequence of AGSP operators, we derive an upper bound on the entanglement entropy (Lemma
33
) and estimate the required Schmidt rank to approximate the ground state with sufficient accuracy. This is summarized in Proposition
30
, completing the proof of the main theorem.

Supplementary Note 7
Proof of Theorem
3
.

We utilize basic statements in Ref.
[
29
]

without proofs, which treats the bounded Hamiltonians, such as a spin or fermion model, with power-law decaying interactions.
The primary difference here is that the Hilbert space dimension and the interaction energy are unbounded.

Supplementary Note 7.1
Approximate Ground State Projection (AGSP)

We introduce the projection operator onto the ground state. Constructing the exact ground-state projection operator is generally challenging, so we consider an approximate one:

\displaystyle K|\Omega\rangle\simeq|\Omega\rangle\quad\text{and}\quad\|K(1-|\Omega\rangle\langle\Omega|)\|\simeq 0,

(S.359)

where
(1-|\Omega\rangle\langle\Omega|)
is the projection operator onto the excited states’ space. We assume
K
is Hermitian (i.e.,
K=K^{\dagger}
).

Next, we characterize the approximate ground state projection (AGSP) operator by three parameters:
\{\delta_{K},\epsilon_{K},{\mathcal{D}}_{K}\}
. Let
|\Omega_{K}\rangle
be the quantum state invariant under
K
such that:

\displaystyle K|\Omega_{K}\rangle=|\Omega_{K}\rangle.

(S.360)

The parameters are defined by the following inequalities:

\displaystyle\||\Omega\rangle-|\Omega_{K}\rangle\|\leq\delta_{K},\quad\|K(1-|\Omega_{K}\rangle\langle\Omega_{K}|)\|\leq\epsilon_{K},\quad\text{and}\quad\text{SR}(K)\leq{\mathcal{D}}_{K},

(S.361)

where
\text{SR}(K)
is the Schmidt rank of
K
between the subsystems
L
and
R
.
The second inequality in (
S.361
) implies that for any state
|\psi_{\bot}\rangle
orthogonal to
|\Omega_{K}\rangle
(i.e.,
\langle\psi_{\bot}|\Omega_{K}\rangle=0
), we have:

\displaystyle\|K|\psi_{\bot}\rangle\|=\|K(1-|\Omega_{K}\rangle\langle\Omega_{K}|)|\psi_{\bot}\rangle\|\leq\epsilon_{K}.

(S.362)

Note that
|\Omega_{K}\rangle
is an approximate ground state when
\delta_{K}\simeq 0
. If
\delta_{K}=\epsilon_{K}=0
,
K
is the exact ground state projector, i.e.,
K=|\Omega\rangle\langle\Omega|
. In the standard AGSP definition
[
11
,
12
,
59
]
,
the parameter
\delta_{K}
is typically not considered. However, in long-range interacting systems, the error
\||\Omega\rangle-|\Omega_{K}\rangle\|
can be significant, requiring careful consideration of
\delta_{K}
.

Lemma 24

(Supplementary Proposition 2 in Ref.
[
29
]
)
.

Let
K
be an AGSP operator for
|\Omega\rangle
with the parameters (
\delta_{K},\epsilon_{K},{\mathcal{D}}_{K}
).
If the following inequality holds

\displaystyle\epsilon_{K}^{2}{\mathcal{D}}_{K}\leq\frac{1}{2},

(S.363)

there exists a quantum state
|\psi\rangle
with
{\rm SR}(|\psi\rangle)\leq{\mathcal{D}}_{K}
such that

\displaystyle\left\||\psi\rangle-|\Omega\rangle\right\|\leq\epsilon_{K}\sqrt{2{\mathcal{D}}_{K}}+\delta_{K}.

(S.364)

Supplementary Note 7.2
Effective Hamiltonian by boson-number truncations

We here adopt the boson-number truncation of
\bar{\Pi}
and consider the effective Hamiltonian
\bar{H}
in the form of

\displaystyle\bar{H}:=\bar{\Pi}H\bar{\Pi}=\sum_{Z}\bar{h}_{Z}

(S.365)

with the ground state
|\bar{\Omega}\rangle
and the spectral gap
\bar{\Delta}
.

Regarding the above effective Hamiltonian, we will prove the following proposition (see
Supplementary Note 8
for the proof):

Proposition 25
.

Let us adopt
\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}
as the projection
\bar{\Pi}
, which is given by

\displaystyle\bar{\Pi}=\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}:=\bigotimes_{x\in\Lambda}\Pi_{x,\leq N_{x}},

(S.366)

with

\displaystyle N_{x}=\mathfrak{b}^{-\mathfrak{a}}\left[\log\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)+\log\left({|x|^{3}+1}\right)\right]^{\mathfrak{a}}\leq 2^{\mathfrak{a}}\mathfrak{b}^{-\mathfrak{a}}\left[\log^{\mathfrak{a}}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)+\log^{\mathfrak{a}}\left({|x|^{3}+1}\right)\right].

(S.367)

Then, the Hamiltonian
\bar{H}=\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}H\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}
preserves the ground state and the spectral gap as follows:

\displaystyle\left\||\Omega\rangle-|\bar{\Omega}\rangle\right\|\leq\delta_{\rm B},\quad\bar{\Delta}\geq\Delta\left(1-\frac{3}{2}\delta_{\rm B}^{2}\right),

(S.368)

with
\epsilon_{\rm B}
given by

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\rm B}=w_{0}\delta_{\rm B}^{2}\Delta\log^{-\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\delta_{\rm B}^{-1}\right),

(S.369)

where
w_{0}
is an
\mathcal{O}(1)
constant and we choose
\delta_{\rm B}
such that
\delta_{\rm B}\leq 1/\sqrt{6}
.
Note that
|\bar{\Omega}\rangle
and
\bar{\Delta}
are the ground state and the spectral gap of
\bar{H}
.

Under the projection (
S.366
),
each of the sites has a Hilbert space dimension
d_{x}
that depends on
x
, which is upper-bounded by
N_{x}
as follows:

\displaystyle d_{x}\leq 2^{\mathfrak{a}}\mathfrak{b}^{-\mathfrak{a}}\left[\log^{\mathfrak{a}}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)+\log^{\mathfrak{a}}\left({x^{3}+1}\right)\right]\leq d_{0}+d_{1}\log^{\mathfrak{a}}(x+1),

\displaystyle d_{0}=2^{\mathfrak{a}}\mathfrak{b}^{-\mathfrak{a}}\log^{\mathfrak{a}}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right),\quad d_{1}=6^{\mathfrak{a}}\mathfrak{b}^{-\mathfrak{a}},

(S.370)

where we use
\log^{\mathfrak{a}}\left(x^{3}+1\right)\leq\log^{\mathfrak{a}}\left(x+1\right)^{3}=3^{\mathfrak{a}}\log^{\mathfrak{a}}\left(x+1\right)
.
Here, we can write
d_{1}=\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}^{-\mathfrak{a}})
, which only depends on the system details, while the dimension
d_{0}
also depends on the error
\epsilon_{\rm B}
for the boson-number truncations.
Hence, we need to derive the area law so that the
(d_{0},d_{1})
dependences are correctly taken into account (see
Supplementary Note 7.7.2
).

Moreover, we rewrite the condition (
S.344
) as

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{h}_{Z}\right\|\leq J_{Z}N_{x}^{k/2}\quad\textrm{for}\quad Z\subseteq[-x,x].

(S.371)

Then, using the upper bound of (
S.345
), we have

\displaystyle\max_{i,i^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{Z:Z\ni\{i,i^{\prime}\},Z\subset[-x,x]}\left\|\bar{h}_{Z}\right\|\right)\leq gN_{x}^{k/2}\bar{J}(d_{i,i^{\prime}}).

(S.372)

Because of

\displaystyle gN_{x}^{k/2}

\displaystyle\leq g\mathfrak{b}^{-\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left[\log\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)+\log\left({|x|^{3}+1}\right)\right]^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}

\displaystyle\leq g_{0}+g_{1}\log^{\chi}(x+1)=:\bar{g}_{x},

(S.373)

with

\displaystyle g_{0}=g\mathfrak{b}^{-\mathfrak{a}k/2}2^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right),\quad g_{1}=g\mathfrak{b}^{-\mathfrak{a}k/2}6^{\mathfrak{a}k/2},\quad\chi=\frac{\mathfrak{a}k}{2},

(S.374)

we can reduce the inequality (
S.372
) to

\displaystyle\sum_{Z:Z\ni\{i,i^{\prime}\},Z\subset[-x,x]}\|\bar{h}_{Z}\|\leq\bar{g}_{x}\bar{J}(d_{i,i^{\prime}}),

(S.375)

which characterizes the interaction strength of the effective Hamiltonian

\bar{H}=\sum_{Z:|Z|\leq k}\bar{h}_{Z}.

Supplementary Note 7.3
Interaction-truncated Hamiltonian

Supplementary Figure 6:
Interaction truncation in the Hamiltonian. The system is decomposed into
(q+2)
blocks (
q=6
shown above).
Blocks
\{B_{s}\}_{s=1}^{q}
each have length
l
, and edge blocks
B_{0}
and
B_{q+1}
extend to the system’s left and right ends.
We truncate all interactions between separated blocks, so the truncated Hamiltonian
H_{\rm t}
[Eq. (
S.378
)] remains close to the original Hamiltonian
H
, as shown in Lemma
26
.

We decompose the system into blocks
B_{0}
,
\{B_{s}\}_{s=1}^{q}
, and
B_{q+1}
, such that
\bigcup_{s=0}^{q+1}B_{s}=\Lambda
, where
q
is an even integer with the condition
q\geq 4
and
|B_{s}|=l
for
1\leq s\leq q
. The subsets
L
and
R
are expressed as:

\displaystyle L=\bigcup_{s=0}^{q/2}B_{s},\quad R=\bigcup_{s=q/2+1}^{q+1}B_{s}.

(S.376)

Following Ref.
[
29
]
, we also define the interaction operator
V_{X,Y}(\Lambda_{0})
between two subsystems
X\subset\Lambda
and
Y\subset\Lambda
as follows.

\displaystyle V_{X,Y}(\Lambda_{0}):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z:Z\subset\Lambda_{0}\\
Z\cap X\neq\emptyset,Z\cap Y\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\bar{h}_{Z},

(S.377)

where the subset
\Lambda_{0}\subset\Lambda
and
X\sqcup Y\subset\Lambda_{0}
are arbitrarily chosen.

After truncating all interactions between non-adjacent blocks, only interactions between adjacent blocks remain:

\displaystyle H_{\rm t}=\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}h_{s}+\sum_{s=0}^{q}h_{s,s+1},

(S.378)

where
h_{s,s+1}:=V_{B_{s},B_{s+1}}(B_{s}\sqcup B_{s+1})
(with
X=B_{s}
,
Y=B_{s+1}
, and
