\Lambda_{0}=B_{s}\sqcup B_{s+1}
in the definition of
V_{X,Y}(\Lambda_{0})
in Eq. (
S.377
)), and
h_{s}
collects all terms supported on
B_{s}
.
In particular, for
s=0
and
s=q
, we let

\displaystyle h_{0,1}:=V_{\tilde{B}_{0},B_{1}}(\tilde{B}_{0}\sqcup B_{1}),\quad h_{q,q+1}:=V_{B_{q},\tilde{B}_{q+1}}(B_{q}\sqcup\tilde{B}_{q+1}),

(S.379)

where
\tilde{B}_{0}=[-ql/2-l,-ql/2)
and
\tilde{B}_{q+1}=[ql/2,ql/2+l)
(see Supplementary Fig.
6
).

In Lemma
36
in
Supplementary Note 9
, we will prove the upper bound of

\displaystyle\overline{V}_{X,Y}:=\sum_{Z:Z\cap X\neq\emptyset,Z\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\|\bar{h}_{Z}\|\leq 2\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\bar{g}_{|x|+r}\left(r^{2}+1\right)\bar{J}(r),

(S.380)

where
X=(-\infty,x]
,
Y=[y,\infty)
(
y-x=r
), and
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{2}
are
\mathcal{O}(1)
constants.
By using the inequality (
S.380
) with
r=1
and
|x|\leq ql/2
, we have:

\displaystyle\|h_{s,s+1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z:Z\subset B_{s}\sqcup B_{s+1}\\
Z\cap B_{s}\neq\emptyset,\ Z\cap B_{s+1}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\left\|h_{Z}\right\|\leq 4\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\bar{g}_{ql}\bar{J}(1)=:c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql},

(S.381)

where we use
\bar{g}_{ql/2+1}\leq\bar{g}_{ql}
from
ql\geq 2
and the monotonic increase of
\bar{g}_{x}

As notations, we define
|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle
and
\Delta_{\rm t}
as the ground state and the spectral gap of the truncated Hamiltonian
H_{\rm t}
, respectively.
We also denote the ground energy of
H_{\rm t}
by
E_{{\rm t},0}
:

H_{\rm t}|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle=E_{{\rm t},0}|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle.

Under the inequality (
S.380
), we can prove the following proposition (see
Supplementary Note 9
for the proof):

Proposition 26
.

The norm distance between
\bar{H}
and
H_{\rm t}
is bounded from above by

\displaystyle\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|=\left\|\bar{H}-H_{\rm t}\right\|\leq 4\eta_{1}\eta_{2}q\bar{g}_{ql}\left(l^{2}+1\right)\bar{J}(l),

(S.382)

where we define
\delta H_{\rm t}:=\bar{H}-H_{\rm t}
.
Also, the spectral gap
\Delta_{\rm t}
of
H_{\rm t}
is bounded from below by

\displaystyle\Delta_{\rm t}\geq\bar{\Delta}-2\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|\geq\bar{\Delta}-8\eta_{1}\eta_{2}q\bar{g}_{ql}\left(l^{2}+1\right)\bar{J}(l).

(S.383)

Under the assumption of
4\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|<\bar{\Delta}
, the ground state
|\bar{\Omega}\rangle
have an overlap with that of the truncated Hamiltonian
|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle
as follows:

\displaystyle\||\bar{\Omega}\rangle-|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle\|\leq\frac{\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|}{\bar{\Delta}-4\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|}.

(S.384)

Also, for an arbitrary quantum state
|\phi\rangle
, the norm distance between
|\bar{\Omega}\rangle
and
|\phi\rangle
is bounded from above by

\displaystyle\||\bar{\Omega}\rangle-|\phi\rangle\|\leq\||\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\phi\rangle\|+\frac{\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|}{\bar{\Delta}-4\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|}.

(S.385)

Supplementary Note 7.4
Effective Hamiltonian with Multi-energy Cut-off

Supplementary Figure 7:
Schematic of the effective Hamiltonian
\tilde{H}_{\rm t}
. We modify the energy spectrum in each
\{h_{s}\}_{s=0}^{q+1}
so that energies above
\tau_{s}
are constant, while
\{h_{s,s+1}\}_{s=0}^{q}
remains the same as the original Hamiltonian. The low-energy spectrum is approximately preserved. The accuracy improves subexponentially with the cut-off energy
\tau
(Theorem
4
).

In constructing the AGSP operator (
S.359
), we require an effective Hamiltonian
\tilde{H}_{\rm t}
with a small norm that retains the low-energy properties of the original Hamiltonian
H_{\rm t}
. To achieve this, we apply the energy cut-off to the Hamiltonian
H_{\rm t}
in Eq. (
S.378
), which plays a crucial role in Refs.
[
12
,
16
,
51
]
.
We adopt here the multi-energy cutoff used in Ref.
[
29
]
for the long-range area law.

For each block Hamiltonian
\{h_{s}\}_{s=0}^{q+1}
, we adopt the following spectral decomposition

\displaystyle h_{s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{E_{s,j}}E_{s,j}|E_{s,j}\rangle\langle E_{s,j}|,

(S.386)

where
\{E_{s,j},|E_{s,j}\rangle\}_{j}
are the eigenvalues and eigenstates of
h_{s}
, respectively.
We define the projection operator onto the eigenspace of
h_{s}
as

\displaystyle\Pi^{(s)}_{I}=\sum_{E_{s,j}\in I}|E_{s,j}\rangle\langle E_{s,j}|

(S.387)

for
I\subset\mathbb{R}
. Especially for
\Pi^{(s)}_{(-\infty,E)}
and
\Pi^{(s)}_{(-\infty,E]}
, we denote them by
\Pi^{(s)}_{<E}
and
\Pi^{(s)}_{\leq E}
, respectively.
In the same way, we define
\Pi^{(s)}_{>E}
and
\Pi^{(s)}_{\geq E}
.

Using the projections
\{\Pi^{(s)}_{\leq\tau_{s}}\}_{s=0}^{q+1}
, we define the total projection
\tilde{\Pi}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{\Pi}=\bigotimes_{s=0}^{q+1}\Pi^{(s)}_{\leq\tau_{s}},

(S.388)

with

\displaystyle\tau_{s}=E_{s,0}+\tau\quad\textrm{for}\quad s=0,1,2\ldots,q+1.

(S.389)

By the projection
\Pi^{(s)}_{\leq\tau_{s}}
, we have

\displaystyle\Pi^{(s)}_{\leq\tau_{s}}h_{s}\Pi^{(s)}_{\leq\tau_{s}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{E_{s,j}\leq\tau_{s}}E_{s,j}|E_{s,j}\rangle\langle E_{s,j}|.

(S.390)

By using the above notations, we describe the effective Hamiltonian
\tilde{H}_{\rm t}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{H}_{\rm t}=\tilde{\Pi}H_{\rm t}\tilde{\Pi}.

(S.391)

We note that the above definition differs slightly from the original one.
In Ref.
[
29
]
, the following expression was considered:

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{\prime}_{\rm t}=\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}\tilde{h}_{s}+\sum_{s=0}^{q}h_{s,s+1},\quad\tilde{h}_{s}:=h_{s}\Pi^{(s)}_{\leq\tau_{s}}+\tau_{s}\Pi^{(s)}_{>\tau_{s}}

(S.392)

for
s=0,1,2\ldots,q+1
.

One advantage of utilizing the definition (
S.391
) is that we can utilize Lemma
34
.
Here, we do not need spectral analyses of
\tilde{H}_{\rm t}
, which is quite challenging in our setup with unbounded Hamiltonians
*8
*8

*8

As will be mentioned in Lemma
37
, the Hamiltonian
H_{\rm t}
does not converge the imaginary time evolution as
e^{-\beta H_{\rm t}}Oe^{\beta H_{\rm t}}
. Hence, we have to avoid using the imaginary time evolution as in
Supplementary Note 10.3
.
However, the spectral analyses of
\tilde{H}_{\rm t}
in Supplementary Note 4. F of Ref.
[
29
]
, which fully utilizes the imaginary time evolution, is difficult to extend to our setup.

As shown below, we can prove the preservation of the ground state and the spectral gap by choosing the truncation parameter
\tau
sufficiently large (see
Supplementary Note 10
for the proof).

Theorem 4
.

Let us define
\varepsilon_{1}
and
\varepsilon_{2}
as

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{1}=2q{\mathcal{E}}_{\tau-4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}-T_{0}},\quad\varepsilon_{2}=\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{1-\varepsilon_{1}}2q\left(\tau+2c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}\right),

(S.393)

where
{\mathcal{E}}_{y}
(
y\geq 0
) is a sub-exponentially decaying function defined in Eq. (
S.607
), and
T_{0}
is defined by
T_{m=0}
using
T_{m}
in Eq. (
S.566
).
Then, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\||\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\tilde{\Omega}_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\varepsilon_{2}}{\Delta_{\rm t}}},

(S.394)

and the spectral gap
\tilde{\Delta}
is lower-bounded by

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}_{\rm t}\geq\Delta_{\rm t}-3\varepsilon_{2}.

(S.395)

Remark.

In Ref.
[
48
]
, short-range bosonic area laws in specific models were considered.
Therein, the theorem on the effective Hamiltonian in Ref.
[
12
]
, which was derived for bounded Hamiltonians, was utilized
*9
*9

*9

More precisely, Appendix D 1 of Ref.
[
48
]
considers the Hilbert space truncation only around the boundary up to a finite distance. At this stage, interaction strength is unbounded beyond a finite distance from the boundary.
.
However, the unboundedness of the total Hamiltonian makes a troublesome problem, as will be shown in
Supplementary Note 10.2
.
The error parameters
\varepsilon_{1}
and
\varepsilon_{2}
give a subexponential decay with respect to
\tau
, which sets a main difference in bounded Hamiltonian cases that give the exponential error decay.

For the convenience of readers, we show the explicit form of
{\mathcal{E}}_{y}
in Eq. (
S.607
):

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{y}=\mu_{1}\exp\left(-\frac{y}{8e\tilde{T}_{y/T_{0}}}\right)+\mu_{2}\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{y}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right],

(S.396)

with

\displaystyle\tilde{T}_{y/T_{0}}:=(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})\bar{g}_{y/T_{0}+ql},\quad T_{0}=(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})\bar{g}_{ql},

\displaystyle\mu_{1}=1+e\int_{0}^{\infty}(z+2)\exp\left[-\frac{1}{8e}\cdot\frac{z}{1+\log^{\chi}(z+3)}\right]dz,\quad\mu_{2}:=1+e\int_{0}^{\infty}(z+2)\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1}}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}}z\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right]dz,

\displaystyle c_{0}=4\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\bar{J}(1),\quad\tilde{c}_{1}=\frac{2^{\chi+\bar{\alpha}+3}4k\eta_{1}}{1-2^{-\bar{\alpha}}},\quad\tilde{c}_{2}=\tilde{c}_{1}\left[\frac{2\chi(2+\bar{\alpha})}{\bar{\alpha}}\right]^{\chi},\quad\tilde{c}_{3}=2^{\bar{\alpha}}+\frac{2(2+\bar{\alpha})}{\bar{\alpha}},

(S.397)

where we refer to Eq. (
S.607
) for
{\mathcal{E}}_{y}
, Eq. (
S.597
) for
\tilde{T}_{y/T_{0}}
,
Eq. (
S.566
) for
T_{0}
, Eqs. (
S.608
) and (
S.609
) for
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}
,
Eq. (
S.381
) for
c_{0}
,
Eq. (
S.562
) for
\tilde{c}_{1},\tilde{c}_{2}
, and Eq. (
S.567
) for
\tilde{c}_{3}
.
We recall that the parameter
\eta_{p}
is defined by the inequality (
S.545
), and the parameter
\bar{g}_{x}
has been defined as

\bar{g}_{x}=g_{0}+g_{1}\log^{\chi}(x+1)
in Eq. (
S.373
).

In the following, we assume

\displaystyle\tau\leq\bar{g}_{ql}ql.

(S.398)

Then, by letting
y=\tau-4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}-T_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{y}{T_{0}}=\frac{\tau-4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}-T_{0}}{T_{0}}\leq\frac{1}{2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1}}ql\leq ql

(S.399)

from
\tilde{c}_{1}\geq 1
, and hence, we upper-bound
\bar{g}_{y/T_{0}+ql}
by
\bar{g}_{2ql}
, which also yields an upper bound for the parameter
{\mathcal{E}}_{y}
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{\tau-4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}-T_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{1}\exp\left[-\frac{\tau-4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}-T_{0}}{8e(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})\bar{g}_{2ql}}\right]+\mu_{2}\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\tau-4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}-T_{0}}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{1}\exp\left[-\frac{\tau}{16e(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})\bar{g}_{2ql}}\right]+\mu_{2}\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\tau}{8e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right].

(S.400)

where, in the second inequality, we impose the condition
\tau\geq 2(4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}+T_{0})
.

Using the inequality (
S.400
), we consider the condition for
\tau
to satisfy
\varepsilon_{2}\leq\varepsilon_{\ast}\Delta_{\rm t}
.
For this purpose, we first consider the condition for
\varepsilon_{1}\leq\varepsilon_{1,\ast}
, which is satisfied by choosing
\tau
as

\displaystyle\tau\geq 16e(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})\bar{g}_{2ql}\log\left(\frac{2\mu_{1}}{\varepsilon_{1,\ast}}\right)+8e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}\left[2\log\left(\frac{2\mu_{2}}{\varepsilon_{1,\ast}}\right)\right]^{1+\chi}\quad\textrm{for}\quad\varepsilon_{1}\leq\varepsilon_{1,\ast}.

(S.401)

Also, from Eq. (
S.393
), the main condition
\varepsilon_{2}\leq\varepsilon_{\ast}\Delta_{\rm t}
implies

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{1-\varepsilon_{1}}2q\left(\tau+2c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}\right)\leq\varepsilon_{\ast}\Delta_{\rm t}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\varepsilon_{1}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{\ast}\Delta_{\rm t}}{\varepsilon_{\ast}\Delta_{\rm t}+2q(\tau+2c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql})}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\varepsilon_{1}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{\ast}\Delta_{\rm t}}{\varepsilon_{\ast}\Delta_{\rm t}+2q(\bar{g}_{ql}ql+2c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql})}\quad\textrm{for}\quad\tau\leq\bar{g}_{ql}ql,

(S.402)

Therefore, by combining (
S.401
) and (
S.402
), we have

\displaystyle\tau\geq 16e(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})\bar{g}_{2ql}\log\left[2\mu_{1}\left(1+\frac{2q(\bar{g}_{ql}ql+2c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql})}{\varepsilon_{\ast}\Delta_{\rm t}}\right)\right]+2^{4+\chi}e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}\log^{1+\chi}\left[2\mu_{2}\left(1+\frac{2q(\bar{g}_{ql}ql+2c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql})}{\varepsilon_{\ast}\Delta_{\rm t}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\tau\geq\mathscr{C}_{1}\bar{g}_{2ql}\log\left(\frac{\mathscr{C}_{2}q^{2}l\bar{g}_{ql}}{\varepsilon_{\ast}\Delta_{\rm t}}\right)+\mathscr{C}_{3}g_{1}\log^{1+\chi}\left(\frac{\mathscr{C}_{4}q^{2}l\bar{g}_{ql}}{\varepsilon_{\ast}\Delta_{\rm t}}\right)\quad\textrm{for}\quad\varepsilon_{2}\leq\varepsilon_{\ast}\Delta_{\rm t},

(S.403)

where
\{\mathscr{C}_{1},\mathscr{C}_{2},\mathscr{C}_{3},\mathscr{C}_{4}\}
are constants of
\mathcal{O}(1)
.

Here, the condition (
S.403
) includes the condition (
S.401
), and hence we have to choose
\tau
so that the conditions (
S.398
) and (
S.403
) are satisfied:

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{1}\bar{g}_{2ql}\log\left(\frac{\mathscr{C}_{2}q^{2}l\bar{g}_{ql}}{\varepsilon_{\ast}\Delta_{\rm t}}\right)+\mathscr{C}_{3}g_{1}\log^{1+\chi}\left(\frac{\mathscr{C}_{4}q^{2}l\bar{g}_{ql}}{\varepsilon_{\ast}\Delta_{\rm t}}\right)\leq\tau\leq\bar{g}_{ql}ql

(S.404)

to ensure
\varepsilon_{2}\leq\varepsilon_{\ast}\Delta_{\rm t}
.

Supplementary Note 7.5
AGSP construction

We here construct the AGSP based on the Chebyshev polynomials
[
12
]
.
We use the following lemma, derived from Supplementary Note 2. H of Ref.
[
29
]
:

Lemma 27
.

Let
T_{m}(x)
be the Chebyshev polynomial as

\displaystyle T_{m}(x):=\frac{\left(x+\sqrt{x^{2}-1}\right)^{m}+\left(x-\sqrt{x^{2}-1}\right)^{m}}{2}.

(S.405)

Then, the polynomial of

\displaystyle K(m,x)=\frac{T_{m}\Bigl[\frac{2x-(\|\tilde{H}_{\rm t}-\tilde{E}_{{\rm t},0}\|+\tilde{\Delta}_{\rm t})}{\|\tilde{H}_{\rm t}-\tilde{E}_{{\rm t},0}\|-\tilde{\Delta}_{\rm t}}\Bigr]}{T_{m}\Bigl[-\frac{\|\tilde{H}_{\rm t}-\tilde{E}_{{\rm t},0}\|+\tilde{\Delta}_{\rm t}}{\|\tilde{H}_{\rm t}-\tilde{E}_{{\rm t},0}\|-\tilde{\Delta}_{\rm t}}\Bigr]}

(S.406)

satisfies the inequality of

\displaystyle\left\|K(m,\tilde{H}_{\rm t}-\tilde{E}_{{\rm t},0})\left(1-|\tilde{\Omega}_{\rm t}\rangle\langle\tilde{\Omega}_{\rm t}|\right)\right\|\leq 2e^{-2m\sqrt{\tilde{\Delta}_{\rm t}/\|\tilde{H}_{\rm t}-\tilde{E}_{{\rm t},0}\|}},

(S.407)

where
|\tilde{\Omega}_{\rm t}\rangle
and
\tilde{E}_{{\rm t},0}
are the ground state and the ground energy of
\tilde{H}_{\rm t}
, respectively.

Remark.

By applying the same analyses to derive the inequality (
S.630
), we can derive

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{H}_{\rm t}-\tilde{E}_{{\rm t},0}\right\|\leq\left\|(\tilde{H}_{\rm t}-\tilde{E}_{{\rm t},0})\tilde{\Pi}\right\|\leq 2q\left(\tau+2c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}\right).

(S.408)

Using the upper bound (
S.408
), we can reduce the inequality (
S.407
) to

\displaystyle\left\|K(m,\tilde{H}_{\rm t})\left(1-|\tilde{\Omega}_{\rm t}\rangle\langle\tilde{\Omega}_{\rm t}|\right)\right\|\leq 2e^{-2m\sqrt{\tilde{\Delta}_{\rm t}/\left[2q\left(\tau+2c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}\right)\right]}}.

(S.409)

Supplementary Note 7.6
Schmidt rank of the polynomials of the effective Hamiltonian

We here consider the Schmidt rank of the power of the truncated Hamiltonian
{\rm SR}(\tilde{H}_{\rm t}^{m})
.
For any partition
L\sqcup R
and the projections
P_{L}
and
P_{R}
, the combined projection
P_{L}\otimes P_{R}
does not change the Schmidt rank between
L
and
R
.
On the other hand, we have to distinguish the Schmidt ranks of
(P_{L}\otimes P_{R}HP_{L}\otimes P_{R})^{m}
and
P_{L}\otimes P_{R}H^{m}P_{L}\otimes P_{R}
.
They usually have a different Schmidt rank.
Nevertheless, by carefully following Supplementary Lemma 8 and Proposition 4 of Ref.
[
29
]
, we can recover the following lemma for the effective Hamiltonian
\tilde{\Pi}H_{\rm t}\tilde{\Pi}
under the choice of
\tilde{\Pi}
as in Eq. (
S.388
):

Lemma 28

(Supplementary Lemma 8 and Proposition 4 in Ref.
[
29
]
)
.

The Schmidt rank of the power of the truncated Hamiltonian
{\rm SR}(\tilde{H}_{\rm t}^{m})
is bounded from above by

\displaystyle{\rm SR}(\tilde{H}_{\rm t}^{m})\leq\min\left\{\left[2+(2d_{ql}l)^{k}\right]^{m},d_{ql}^{ql}(q+m+1)^{q+1}[e(q+1)^{2}(2d_{ql}l)^{k}]^{m/(q+1)}\right\},

(S.410)

where
d_{x}
is defined as the Hilbert space dimension on the site
x
as in Eq. (
S.370
).

Proof of Lemma
28
.

The proof is exactly the same as that in Supplementary Lemma 8 and Proposition 4 of Ref.
[
29
]
, which is based on Ref.
[
12
]
.
The only point to consider is the Schmidt rank of the operators
\{h_{s,s+1}\}_{s=0}^{q+1}
.
These operators are supported on
\tilde{B}_{0}\cup\left(\bigcup_{s=1}^{q}B_{s}\right)\cup\tilde{B}_{q+1}=[-(q/2+1)l,(q/2+1)l]
(see Supplementary Fig.
6
).
From Eq. (
S.370
), the local Hilbert space is now given by
d_{(q/2+1)l}\leq d_{ql}
.
We thus prove the inequality (
S.410
).
\square

Supplementary Note 7.7
Quantum state with a small Schmidt rank and a large overlap with the ground state

We have the ingredients to find a good AGSP operator in the sense that the condition (
S.363
) in Lemma
24
is satisfied.
Using it, we can prove the existence of a quantum state that has a small Schmidt rank and a large overlap with the ground state:

Proposition 29
.

There exists a quantum state
|\phi\rangle
such that

\displaystyle\||\Omega\rangle-|\phi\rangle\|\leq\frac{1}{2}

(S.411)

with

\displaystyle\log\left[{\rm SR}(|\phi\rangle)\right]\leq c_{\ast}\Delta^{-(1+2/\bar{\alpha})(\upsilon+1)}\left[\log\left(1/\Delta\right)\right]^{4+3/\bar{\alpha}+\chi(1+2/\bar{\alpha})},

(S.412)

where
c_{\ast}
is a constant that depends only on system details.
In particular, for
\upsilon=0
, the upper bound is improved to

\displaystyle\log\left[{\rm SR}(|\phi\rangle)\right]\leq c_{\ast}\Delta^{-1-2/\bar{\alpha}}\left[\log\left(1/\Delta\right)\right]^{3+3/\bar{\alpha}+\chi(1+2/\bar{\alpha})}\log\log(1/\Delta).

(S.413)

Supplementary Note 7.7.1
Guide to the parameter trade-offs for the proof of Proposition
29

We briefly explain the parameter choices in the proof of Proposition
29
from a scaling perspective.
The construction involves several truncations followed by an AGSP based on a polynomial of degree
m
, and the parameters are chosen so that each approximation error is reduced to a constant level, while the Schmidt rank of the resulting state grows only moderately.

The boson-number truncation is controlled by
\delta_{{\rm B}}
.
Fixing
\delta_{{\rm B}}=\mathcal{O}(1)
ensures that the ground state is preserved up to a constant error,

\left\||\Omega\rangle-|\bar{\Omega}\rangle\right\|=\mathcal{O}(1)
,
and that the spectral gap remains stable,
\bar{\Delta}=\Theta(\Delta)
.
At the same time, this choice induces effective local dimensions and interaction strengths that grow at most polylogarithmically in the distance to the boundary.
Choosing
\delta_{{\rm B}}
parametrically smaller would strictly worsen these quantities and, as a consequence, deteriorate the final Schmidt-rank scaling.

The interaction truncation is governed by the block length
l
and the number of blocks
q
.
The discarded interaction norm scales as

\left\|\delta H_{{\rm t}}\right\|=\mathcal{O}\left(q\,\bar{g}_{ql}\,l^{-\bar{\alpha}}\right)
,
where the effective interaction strength on the relevant scale satisfies

\bar{g}_{ql}=\mathcal{O}\left(\Delta^{-\upsilon}\log^{\chi}(ql)\right)
.
Thus, taking

\displaystyle l=\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{q\log^{\chi}(q/\Delta)}{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}\right)^{1/\bar{\alpha}}\right)

(S.414)

is sufficient to ensure
\left\|\delta H_{{\rm t}}\right\|=\mathcal{O}(\Delta)
and hence
\Delta_{{\rm t}}=\Theta(\Delta)
.
While increasing
l
further would indeed suppress the interaction truncation error, it would simultaneously enlarge the effective operator support and worsen the Schmidt-rank estimates.
The above choice, therefore, represents a minimal block length that achieves the required accuracy without unnecessarily inflating entanglement.

The multi-energy cut-off introduces the parameter
\tau
.
The corresponding approximation error decays subexponentially as

\varepsilon_{2}=\mathcal{E}_{\tau-\mathcal{O}(\bar{g}_{ql})}
,
so choosing

\tau=\mathcal{O}\left(\Delta^{-\upsilon}\log^{1+\chi}(q/\Delta)\right)

is sufficient to make
\varepsilon_{2}=\mathcal{O}(\Delta)
.
This guarantees that both the overlap

\left\||\Omega_{{\rm t}}\rangle-|\tilde{\Omega}_{{\rm t}}\rangle\right\|

and the effective gap
\tilde{\Delta}_{{\rm t}}
remain stable, while keeping the spectral radius

\left\|\tilde{H}_{{\rm t}}-\tilde{E}_{{\rm t},0}\right\|=\mathcal{O}\left(q(\tau+\bar{g}_{ql})\right)

within a range that can still be resolved efficiently by the Chebyshev filter.

Finally, the remaining parameters
q
and the polynomial degree
m
are fixed by balancing the AGSP suppression against the growth of the Schmidt rank.
From the Chebyshev construction, the AGSP error satisfies

\displaystyle\epsilon_{K}=\mathcal{O}\left(\exp\left[-m\sqrt{\frac{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}{q\log^{1+\chi}\left(q/\Delta\right)}}\right]\right),

(S.415)

which decays exponentially in
m
for fixed
q
.
On the other hand, Lemma
28
yields the Schmidt-rank bound

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{D}_{K}=\mathcal{O}\left(\left(ql\right)\,\log d_{ql}+\frac{m}{q}\,\log\left(d_{ql}l\right)\right).

(S.416)

Using the previously fixed choice of
l
and the corresponding bounds on the effective local dimension,
the above expression reduces to

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{D}_{K}=\mathcal{O}\left(q\left(\frac{q\log^{\chi}(q/\Delta)}{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}\right)^{1/\bar{\alpha}}\log(q/\Delta)+\frac{m}{q}\log(q/\Delta)\right).

(S.417)

Here the dependence on the local Hilbert space enters only through the worst-case block dimension
d_{ql}
,
which grows at most polylogarithmically in
q/\Delta
, as established in the boson-number truncation step.

Thus, after fixing
l
, the Schmidt-rank growth separates into a contribution that is independent of
m

and a contribution that grows linearly in
m
but is suppressed by a factor
1/q
.
The bootstrapping argument requires that the AGSP error be chosen so that

\epsilon_{K}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{K}}
and
\epsilon_{K}^{2}\mathcal{D}_{K}

are bounded by fixed constants, for instance by
1/2
and
1/8
, respectively.
To handle these constraints simultaneously, we impose the sufficient condition

\epsilon_{K}\mathcal{D}_{K}\leq\epsilon_{K}^{1/2}
,
which guarantees both bounds once
\epsilon_{K}
is sufficiently small
and does not affect the leading asymptotic scaling.

Intuitively, the parameters
q
and
m
play complementary roles in the AGSP construction.
Increasing
q
suppresses the
m
-dependent contribution to
\log\mathcal{D}_{K}
by a factor
1/q
,
but simultaneously enlarges the
m
-independent contribution.
The choice of
q
therefore balances the growth of these two terms.
Once
q
is fixed, the remaining freedom lies in the polynomial degree
m
,
which controls the strength of the exponential suppression provided by the Chebyshev filter.
This condition fixes the scale of
q
by requiring that the exponential decay of
\epsilon_{K}

dominates the
m
-independent contribution to
\log\mathcal{D}_{K}
,
leading to the choice
q=\mathcal{O}\left(\Delta^{-(\upsilon+1)}\log^{3+\chi}\left(1/\Delta\right)\right)
.
For this choice of
q
, the polynomial degree
m
is then taken sufficiently large so that
the exponential suppression in
\epsilon_{K}
compensates for the remaining

m
-dependent term
\frac{m}{q}\log(q/\Delta)
in
\log\mathcal{D}_{K}
.
This determines the scaling of
m
and yields the Schmidt-rank bound stated in
Proposition
29
.

Supplementary Note 7.7.2
Proof of Proposition
29

We begin with choosing the parameter
\delta_{\rm B}
in Proposition
25
as

\displaystyle\delta_{\rm B}=\frac{1}{8},

(S.418)

which gives

\displaystyle\left\||\Omega\rangle-|\bar{\Omega}\rangle\right\|\leq\frac{1}{8},\quad\bar{\Delta}\geq\frac{3}{4}\Delta,\quad\epsilon_{\rm B}=\frac{w_{0}\Delta}{64\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(8\right)}.

(S.419)

Under the above choice, the parameters
d_{0}
and
g_{0}
in Eqs. (
S.370
) and (
S.374
), respectively, are now given by

\displaystyle d_{0}\propto\Delta^{-2\upsilon/k}\log^{\mathfrak{a}}(1/\Delta),\quad d_{1}\propto\Delta^{-2\upsilon/k},

\displaystyle g_{0}\propto\Delta^{-\upsilon}\log^{\chi}(1/\Delta),\quad g_{1}\propto\Delta^{-\upsilon},

(S.420)

where we use the relation (
S.342
) for
\mathfrak{b}
, i.e.,
\mathfrak{b}\propto\Delta^{2\upsilon/(\mathfrak{a}k)}
.

We next choose the number of blocks
q
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|\leq\frac{\Delta}{16}\quad\longrightarrow\quad\Delta_{\rm t}\geq\bar{\Delta}-\frac{\Delta}{8}\geq\frac{5}{8}\Delta,

(S.421)

where the first inequality yields the second one because of the inequality (
S.383
), i.e.,
\Delta_{\rm t}\geq\bar{\Delta}-2\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|
.
Using Proposition
26
and the inequality (
S.346
), the above condition is satisfied

\displaystyle 4\eta_{1}\eta_{2}q\bar{g}_{ql}\left(l^{2}+1\right)\bar{J}(l)\leq\frac{4\eta_{1}\eta_{2}q\bar{g}_{ql}}{l^{\bar{\alpha}}+1}\leq\frac{\Delta}{16}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow l^{\bar{\alpha}}+1\geq(64\eta_{1}\eta_{2})\frac{q\bar{g}_{ql}}{\Delta}=(64\eta_{1}\eta_{2})\frac{q\left[g_{0}+g_{1}\log^{\chi}(ql+1)\right]}{\Delta}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow l=w_{1}\left[\frac{q\log^{\chi}(q/\Delta)}{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}\right]^{1/\bar{\alpha}},

(S.422)

where
w_{1}
is a constant of
\mathcal{O}(1)
and we use (
S.420
).
Under this choice, we also obtain

\displaystyle\bar{g}_{ql}\leq\frac{\Delta}{64\eta_{1}\eta_{2}}\frac{l^{\bar{\alpha}}+1}{q}\leq w_{2}\Delta^{-\upsilon}\log^{\chi}(q/\Delta)

(S.423)

with
w_{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
.
This choice of
q
gives the following inequality from the inequality (
S.385
):

\displaystyle\left\||\bar{\Omega}\rangle-|\phi\rangle\right\|\leq\left\||\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\phi\rangle\right\|+\frac{1}{8}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\left\||\Omega\rangle-|\phi\rangle\right\|\leq\||\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\phi\rangle\|+\left\||\Omega\rangle-|\bar{\Omega}\rangle\right\|+\frac{1}{8}\leq\||\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\phi\rangle\|+\frac{1}{4}

(S.424)

for an arbitrary quantum state
|\phi\rangle
, where we use the inequality (
S.419
).

Next, from Lemma
24
, if an operator
K
satisfies the AGSP condition such that

\displaystyle\epsilon_{K}^{2}{\mathcal{D}}_{K}\leq\frac{1}{2},

(S.425)

we can find a quantum state
|\psi\rangle
satisfying

\displaystyle\||\Omega\rangle-|\psi\rangle\|\leq\epsilon_{K}\sqrt{2{\mathcal{D}}_{K}}+\delta_{K}\quad{\rm with}\quad{\rm SR}(|\psi\rangle)\leq{\mathcal{D}}_{K},

(S.426)

where the parameters
\{\delta_{K},\epsilon_{K},{\mathcal{D}}_{K}\}
are defined in Eq. (
S.361
).
Hence, we aim to prove the existence of the AGSP operator such that

\displaystyle\epsilon_{K}\sqrt{2{\mathcal{D}}_{K}}+\delta_{K}\leq\frac{1}{2},

(S.427)

\displaystyle\log({\mathcal{D}}_{K})\leq c_{\ast}\Delta^{-(1+2/\bar{\alpha})(\upsilon+1)}\left[\log\left(1/\Delta\right)\right]^{4+3/\bar{\alpha}+\chi(1+2/\bar{\alpha})},

(S.428)

which proves the Proposition
29
by replacing
|\phi\rangle
with
|\psi\rangle
in the inequality (
S.424
).

In the construction of the AGSP operator, we utilize Eq. (
S.406
), which is based on the Chebyshev polynomials with respect to the effective Hamiltonian
\tilde{H}_{\rm t}
from the truncated Hamiltonian
H_{\rm t}
.
Here, the error is estimated by the inequality (
S.409
).
We adopt the operator
K(m,\tilde{H}_{\rm t}-\tilde{E}_{{\rm t},0})
as the AGSP operator
K
for the ground state
|\Omega\rangle
, and in the following, we aim to estimate the AGSP parameters
(\delta_{K},\epsilon_{K},{\mathcal{D}}_{K})
as defined in (
S.361
).

First, we choose such that
\delta_{K}\leq 3/8
.
Here,
\delta_{K}
is estimated as

\displaystyle\delta_{K}=\||\Omega\rangle-|\tilde{\Omega}_{\rm t}\rangle\|\leq\left\||\Omega\rangle-|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|+\left\||\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\tilde{\Omega}_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|\leq\frac{1}{4}+\left\||\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\tilde{\Omega}_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|,

(S.429)

where, in the second inequality, we use (
S.424
) with
|\phi\rangle\to|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle
.
For the norm of
\left\||\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\tilde{\Omega}_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|
, we obtain from the inequality (
S.394
) in Theorem
4
:

\displaystyle\left\||\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\tilde{\Omega}_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\varepsilon_{2}}{\Delta_{\rm t}}}.

(S.430)

Hence, the condition
\delta_{K}\leq 3/8
is ensured by

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2\varepsilon_{2}}{\Delta_{\rm t}}}\leq\frac{1}{8}\longrightarrow\varepsilon_{2}\leq\frac{\Delta_{\rm t}}{128}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\tau\geq\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{1}\bar{g}_{2ql}\log\left(\frac{\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{2}q^{2}l\bar{g}_{ql}}{\Delta}\right)+\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{3}g_{1}\log^{1+\chi}\left(\frac{\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{4}q^{2}l\bar{g}_{ql}}{\Delta}\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\tau\geq w_{3}\Delta^{-\upsilon}\log^{1+\chi}\left(q/\Delta\right)

(S.431)

for appropriate choices of
\mathcal{O}(1)
constants
\{\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{1},\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{2},\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{3},\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{4}\}
and
w_{3}
, where, in the third inequality, we use the inequalities (
S.404
) with
\epsilon_{\ast}\to 1/128
and
\Delta_{\rm t}\geq 5\Delta/8
as in (
S.421
), and in the last inequality, we use the upper bound (
S.423
).
Under the above choice, we can also ensure

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}_{\rm t}\geq\Delta_{\rm t}-3\varepsilon_{2}\geq 0.9\Delta_{\rm t}\geq\frac{1}{2}\Delta,

(S.432)

where we use the upper bound (
S.395
).

Second, from the inequality (
S.409
), we obtain

\displaystyle\epsilon_{K}\leq 2e^{-2m\sqrt{\tilde{\Delta}_{\rm t}/\left[2q\left(\tau+2c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}\right)\right]}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-2m\sqrt{\frac{\Delta}{4q\left(\tau+2c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-w_{4}m\sqrt{\frac{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}{q\log^{1+\chi}\left(q/\Delta\right)}}\right),

(S.433)

where we use the upper bounds (
S.431
) and (
S.432
), and
w_{4}
is an
\mathcal{O}(1)
constant.
Third, from Lemma
28
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{K}={\rm SR}[K(m,\tilde{H}_{\rm t}-\tilde{E}_{{\rm t},0})]

\displaystyle\leq d_{ql}^{ql}(q+m+1)^{q+1}[e(q+1)^{2}(2d_{ql}l)^{k}]^{m/(q+1)}

\displaystyle\leq d_{ql}^{2ql}[e(q+1)^{2}(2d_{ql}l)^{k}]^{m/(q+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left[w_{5}\log(q/\Delta)q\left[\frac{q\log^{\chi}(q/\Delta)}{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}\right]^{1/\bar{\alpha}}+\frac{w_{6}m}{q}\log(q/\Delta)\right]

(S.434)

by assuming
(q+m+1)^{q+1}\leq d_{ql}^{ql}
, where we use
d_{0}\propto\Delta^{-2\upsilon/k}\log^{\mathfrak{a}}(1/\Delta)
,
d_{1}\propto\Delta^{-2\upsilon/k}
in (
S.420
) and

\displaystyle\log(d_{ql}l)=\log(d_{ql})+\log(l)

\displaystyle\leq\log\left[d_{0}+d_{1}\log^{\mathfrak{a}}(ql+1)\right]+\log\left\{w_{1}\left[\frac{q\log^{\chi}(q/\Delta)}{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}\right]^{1/\bar{\alpha}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq{\rm const.}\log(q/\Delta).

(S.435)

Note that we ensure the condition
(q+m+1)^{q+1}\leq d_{ql}^{ql}
as long as
\log(m)\lesssim l\propto(q/\Delta^{\upsilon+1})^{1/\bar{\alpha}}
, which can be indeed satisfied under the choice of Eq. (
S.441
).

Then, for the inequality (
S.427
) to be satisfied, we need to choose
m
and
q
such that

\displaystyle\epsilon_{K}\sqrt{2{\mathcal{D}}_{K}}\leq\frac{1}{8}.

(S.436)

In the following, we choose
m
and
q
as such
\epsilon_{K}{\mathcal{D}}_{K}\leq\epsilon_{K}^{1/2}
, which reduces the conditions (
S.425
) and (
S.436
) to

\displaystyle\epsilon_{K}^{2}{\mathcal{D}}_{K}\leq\epsilon_{K}^{3/2}\leq\frac{1}{2},\quad\quad\epsilon_{K}\sqrt{2{\mathcal{D}}_{K}}\leq\sqrt{2}\epsilon_{K}^{3/4}\leq\frac{1}{8}.

(S.437)

From the inequalities (
S.433
) and (
S.434
),
the condition
\epsilon_{K}{\mathcal{D}}_{K}\leq\epsilon_{K}^{1/2}
is satisfied for

\displaystyle w_{5}\log(q/\Delta)q\left[\frac{q\log^{\chi}(q/\Delta)}{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}\right]^{1/\bar{\alpha}}\leq\frac{w_{4}m}{4}\sqrt{\frac{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}{q\log^{1+\chi}\left(q/\Delta\right)}},

\displaystyle\frac{w_{6}m}{q}\log(q/\Delta)\leq\frac{w_{4}m}{4}\sqrt{\frac{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}{q\log^{1+\chi}\left(q/\Delta\right)}}.

(S.438)

The first inequality in (
S.438
) gives the lower bound of
m
as follows:

\displaystyle m\geq\frac{4w_{5}}{w_{4}}\Delta^{-(1/2+1/\bar{\alpha})(\upsilon+1)}q^{3/2+1/\bar{\alpha}}\left[\log\left(q/\Delta\right)\right]^{\chi/\bar{\alpha}+(1+\chi)/2+1}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow m\geq w_{7}\Delta^{-(1/2+1/\bar{\alpha})(\upsilon+1)}q^{3/2+1/\bar{\alpha}}\left[\log\left(q/\Delta\right)\right]^{\chi/\bar{\alpha}+(1+\chi)/2+1},

(S.439)

where
w_{7}=4w_{5}/w_{4}=\mathcal{O}(1)
.
The second one in (
S.438
) implies

\displaystyle\frac{q^{1/2}}{\log^{3/2+\chi/2}(q/\Delta)}\geq\frac{4w_{6}}{w_{4}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\frac{q}{\log^{3+\chi}(q/\Delta)}\geq\left(\frac{4w_{6}}{w_{4}}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}\longrightarrow q\geq\frac{w_{8}}{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}\log^{3+\chi}(1/\Delta).

(S.440)

We thus choose

q=\left\lceil w_{8}(1/\Delta^{\upsilon+1})\log^{3+\chi}(1/\Delta)\right\rceil,

and then the parameters
\epsilon_{K}
decays exponentially with respect to
m
.
Therefore, by choosing the following constant
w_{9}
appropriately, the choice of

\displaystyle m=w_{9}\Delta^{-(2+2/\bar{\alpha})(\upsilon+1)}\left[\log\left(1/\Delta\right)\right]^{3/\bar{\alpha}+6+2\chi(\bar{\alpha}+1)/\bar{\alpha}}

(S.441)

satisfies (
S.437
) and (
S.439
).

Finally, under the above choices, we can upper-bound the Schmidt rank
{\mathcal{D}}_{K}
from the inequality (
S.434
) as

\displaystyle\log({\mathcal{D}}_{K})\leq w_{10}\Delta^{-(1+2/\bar{\alpha})(\upsilon+1)}\left[\log\left(1/\Delta\right)\right]^{4+3/\bar{\alpha}+\chi(1+2/\bar{\alpha})},

(S.442)

where
w_{10}
is a constant of
\mathcal{O}(1)
.
We thus obtain the inequality (
S.428
).

For
\upsilon=0
, we have
d_{0}\propto\log^{\mathfrak{a}}(1/\Delta)
,
d_{1}\propto 1
, and hence
\log(d_{ql})\lesssim\log\log(1/\Delta)
.
This point improves the inequality (
S.434
) to

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{K}={\rm SR}[K(m,\tilde{H}_{\rm t})]\leq\exp\left[w_{5}\log\log(1/\Delta)q\left[\frac{q\log^{\chi}(q/\Delta)}{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}\right]^{1/\bar{\alpha}}+\frac{w_{6}m}{q}\log(q/\Delta)\right].

(S.443)

We then follow the same calculation and eventually prove the upper bound (
S.413
).

This completes the proof of Proposition
29
.
\square

Supplementary Note 7.8
Completing the proof

We now have all the ingredients to prove the main theorem. Based on the quantum state constructed in Proposition
29
, we approximate the ground state with an arbitrary accuracy while controlling the Schmidt rank, which allows us to prove an upper bound for the entanglement entropy.

Proposition 30
.

Let
|\phi\rangle
be an arbitrary quantum state such that

\displaystyle\||\Omega\rangle-|\phi\rangle\|\leq\frac{1}{2}

(S.444)

with
{\mathcal{D}}_{\phi}:={\rm SR}(|\phi\rangle)
.
Then, there exists a quantum state
|\psi\rangle
approximating the ground state
|\Omega\rangle
such that

\displaystyle\||\Omega\rangle-|\psi\rangle\|\leq\delta,

(S.445)

and the Schmidt rank of
|\psi\rangle
satisfies

\displaystyle\log[{\rm SR}(|\psi\rangle)]\leq\log({\mathcal{D}}_{\phi})+\tilde{c}_{\ast}\frac{\log^{\chi/2+5/2}\left[1/(\delta\Delta)\right]}{\Delta^{(\upsilon+1)/2}}.

(S.446)

Furthermore, the entanglement entropy
S(|\Omega\rangle)
is bounded from above by

\displaystyle S(|\Omega\rangle)\leq\log({\mathcal{D}}_{\phi})+\tilde{c}^{\prime}_{\ast}\frac{\log^{\chi/2+5/2}\left(1/\Delta\right)}{\Delta^{(\upsilon+1)/2}}.

(S.447)

Here,
\tilde{c}_{\ast}
and
\tilde{c}^{\prime}_{\ast}
are
\mathcal{O}(1)
constants.

By applying Proposition
29
to Proposition
30
, we immediately prove Theorem
3
.
Here,
\log({\mathcal{D}}_{\phi})
corresponds to the RHS of the inequality (
S.412
), which is larger than
\tilde{c}^{\prime}_{\ast}\log^{\chi/2+5/2}\left(1/\Delta\right)/\sqrt{\Delta}
in the inequality (
S.447
) for
\Delta\ll 1
.
We thus prove the first main inequality (
S.350
).

To prove the second main inequality (
S.352
), we follow the same approach as in Ref.
[
29
]
.
The proof relies on the following two lemmas.
The first lemma connects the MPS approximation and the truncation error of the Schmidt rank:

Lemma 31

(Lemma 1 in Ref.
[
64
]
)
.

We here label the total system
\Lambda
as
\Lambda=\{1,2,\ldots,n\}
.
Then, let
|\psi\rangle
be an arbitrary quantum state, decomposed as follows between the subsets
\{1,2,\ldots,x\}
and
\{x+1,x+2,\ldots,n\}
:

\displaystyle|\psi\rangle=\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\mu_{m}^{(x)}|\psi_{\leq x,m}\rangle\otimes|\psi_{>x,m}\rangle,

(S.448)

where
\{\mu_{m}^{(x)}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}
are the Schmidt coefficients in descending order.
Then, there exists an MPS approximation
|{\rm M}_{\psi,{\mathcal{D}}}\rangle
with bond dimension
{\mathcal{D}}
*10
*10

*10

To distinguish the notation of the spatial dimension
D
, we adopt the cursive notation
{\mathcal{D}}
for the bond dimension.
, approximating
|\psi\rangle
such that:

\displaystyle\||\psi\rangle-|{\rm M}_{\psi,{\mathcal{D}}}\rangle\|\leq 2\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\delta_{x,{\mathcal{D}}},\quad\delta_{x,{\mathcal{D}}}:=\sum_{m>{\mathcal{D}}}|\mu_{m}^{(x)}|^{2}.

(S.449)

Moreover, the inequality is generalized as follows:

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm tr}_{X^{\rm c}}\left(|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|-|{\rm M}_{\psi,{\mathcal{D}}}\rangle\langle{\rm M}_{\psi,{\mathcal{D}}}|\right)\right\|_{1}\leq 2\sum_{x=x_{0}-1}^{x_{1}}\delta_{x,{\mathcal{D}}},

(S.450)

where the subset
X=[x_{0},x_{1}]
(
x_{0}\leq x_{1}
) can arbitrarily chosen.

From the above lemma, we aim to derive the upper bound on the error
\delta_{x,{\mathcal{D}}}
by the Schmidt rank truncation for the ground state
|\Omega\rangle
.
For this purpose, we utilize the Eckart-Young theorem:

Lemma 32

(Eckart-Young theorem
[
65
]
)
.

Given a normalized state
|\psi\rangle
as in Eq. (
S.448
), for any quantum state
|\psi^{\prime}\rangle
, the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\sum_{m>{\rm SR}(|\psi^{\prime}\rangle)}|\mu_{m}^{(i)}|^{2}\leq\||\psi\rangle-|\psi^{\prime}\rangle\|^{2},

(S.451)

where
{\rm SR}(|\psi^{\prime}\rangle)
is defined for the decomposition of
\{1,2,\ldots,i\}
and
\{i+1,i+2,\ldots,n\}
.

To apply the Eckart-Young theorem, we use the inequality (
S.446
), which ensures the existence of a quantum state
|\psi_{\mathcal{D}}\rangle
such that

\displaystyle\||\Omega\rangle-|\psi_{\mathcal{D}}\rangle\|\leq\delta,

(S.452)

and the Schmidt rank of
|\psi_{\mathcal{D}}\rangle
satisfies

\displaystyle\log({\mathcal{D}})\leq\log({\mathcal{D}}_{\phi})+\tilde{c}_{\ast}\frac{\log^{\chi/2+5/2}\left[1/(\delta\Delta)\right]}{\Delta^{(\upsilon+1)/2}}.

(S.453)

This implies that by choosing

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{0}=\exp\left\{C_{1}\Delta^{-(1+2/\bar{\alpha})(\upsilon+1)}\left[\log\left(1/\Delta\right)\right]^{4+3/\bar{\alpha}+\chi(1+2/\bar{\alpha})}+C_{2}\frac{\log^{\chi/2+5/2}\left[1/(\delta\Delta)\right]}{\Delta^{(\upsilon+1)/2}}\right\},

(S.454)

we can ensure
\delta_{x,{\mathcal{D}}_{0}}\leq\delta^{2}
for an arbitrary bi-partition of the ground state.
By applying it to the inequality (
S.450
), we prove the second main inequality (
S.352
), where we use
|X|=x_{1}-x_{0}+1
from
X=[x_{0},x_{1}]
.

This completes the proof.
\square

Supplementary Note 7.8.1
Preliminary lemma

We first introduce the following proposition that relates the AGSP operators and the entanglement entropy:

Lemma 33

(Supplementary Proposition 3 in Ref.
[
29
]
)
.

Let
\{K_{p}\}_{p=1}^{\infty}
be a set of AGSP operators.
such that the errors
\epsilon_{K_{p}}
and
\delta_{K_{p}}
decrease with the index
p
and goes to zero in the limit of
p\to\infty
, i.e.,
\epsilon_{K_{\infty}}=0
,
\delta_{K_{\infty}}=0
. That is, the operator
K_{\infty}
is the exact ground-state projector.
Also, we set
|\psi_{\mathcal{D}}\rangle
be an arbitrary quantum state with

\displaystyle\||\psi_{\mathcal{D}}\rangle-|\Omega\rangle\|=\nu_{0}\quad{\rm and}\quad{\rm SR}(|\psi_{\mathcal{D}}\rangle)={\mathcal{D}}.

(S.455)

Then, we prove for each of
\{K_{p}\}_{p=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{K_{p}e^{-i\theta_{p}}|\psi_{\mathcal{D}}\rangle}{\|K_{p}|\psi_{\mathcal{D}}\rangle\|}-|\Omega\rangle\right\|\leq\Gamma_{K_{p}}

(S.456)

with
\theta_{p}\in\mathbb{R}
appropriately chosen, where
\{\Gamma_{K_{p}}\}_{p=1}^{\infty}
are defined as

\displaystyle\Gamma_{K_{p}}:=\frac{\epsilon_{K_{p}}}{1-\nu_{0}-\delta_{K_{p}}}+\delta_{K_{p}}.

(S.457)

Moreover, under the condition
\Gamma_{K_{p}}\leq 1
for all
p
, the entanglement entropy
S(|\Omega\rangle)
is bounded from above by

\displaystyle S(|\Omega\rangle)

\displaystyle\leq\log({\mathcal{D}})-\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}\Gamma_{K_{p}}^{2}\log\frac{\Gamma_{K_{p}}^{2}}{3{\mathcal{D}}_{K_{p+1}}},

(S.458)

where we set
\Gamma_{K_{0}}:=1
.

Supplementary Note 7.8.2
Proof of Proposition
30

The proof is based on Lemma
33
.
We set
q=2
and construct the AGSP operator for
|\Omega\rangle
by using
K(m,\tilde{H}_{\rm t})
as in the proof of Proposition
29
.
Then, the AGSP parameters
\{\delta_{K},\epsilon_{K},D_{K}\}
depends on the parameters
\epsilon_{\rm B}
,
l
,
m
and
\tau
.
We adopt the state
|\phi\rangle
in Proposition
29
as the quantum state
|\psi_{\mathcal{D}}\rangle
in Eq. (
S.455
). Then, we can ensure

\displaystyle\nu_{0}\leq\frac{1}{2},

(S.459)

which reduces the inequality (
S.458
) to

\displaystyle S(|\Omega\rangle)

\displaystyle\leq\log({\mathcal{D}}_{\phi})-\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}\Gamma_{K_{p}}^{2}\log\frac{\Gamma_{K_{p}}^{2}}{3{\mathcal{D}}_{K_{p+1}}},

(S.460)

where we denote
{\mathcal{D}}_{\phi}={\rm SR}(|\phi\rangle)
.

In the following, we consider a set of AGSP
\{K_{p}\}_{p=1}^{\infty}
such that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{K_{p}}\leq\frac{1}{p},

(S.461)

and estimating the Schmidt rank
{\mathcal{D}}_{K_{p}}
to achieve it.
The condition (
S.461
) is satisfied when

\displaystyle\delta_{K_{p}}\leq\frac{1}{3p},\quad\frac{\epsilon_{K_{p}}}{1-\nu_{0}-\delta_{K_{p}}}\leq\frac{2}{3p}\quad\text{or}\quad\epsilon_{K_{p}}\leq\frac{1}{9p},

(S.462)

where the second inequality is derived from

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon_{K_{p}}}{1-\nu_{0}-\delta_{K_{p}}}\leq\frac{\epsilon_{K_{p}}}{\frac{1}{2}-\delta_{K_{p}}}\leq\frac{\epsilon_{K_{p}}}{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{3}}=6\epsilon_{K_{p}}\leq\frac{2}{3p}.

(S.463)

Here, we use
\nu_{0}\leq\frac{1}{2}
.

We begin with the parameter
\epsilon_{\rm B}
in Proposition
25
and choose it as

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\rm B}=w_{0}\delta_{\rm B}^{2}\Delta\log^{-\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\delta_{\rm B}^{-1}\right),

(S.464)

which gives

\displaystyle\left\||\Omega\rangle-|\bar{\Omega}\rangle\right\|\leq\delta_{\rm B},\quad\bar{\Delta}\geq\Delta\left(1-\frac{3\delta_{\rm B}^{2}}{2}\right)

(S.465)

from the inequality (
S.368
).
Then, for

\displaystyle\delta_{\rm B}=\frac{1}{9p},

(S.466)

we have from Eq. (
S.369
)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\rm B}=\frac{w_{0}}{81p^{2}\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(9p\right)}\Delta,\quad\bar{\Delta}\geq\Delta\left(1-\frac{1}{54p^{2}}\right)\geq\frac{3}{4}\Delta.

(S.467)

The above choice provides the parameters
d_{0},d_{1}
and
g_{0},g_{1}
in a similar way to (
S.420
) as follows:

\displaystyle d_{0}\propto\Delta^{-2\upsilon/k}\log^{\mathfrak{a}}(p/\Delta),\quad d_{1}\propto\Delta^{-2\upsilon/k},

\displaystyle g_{0}\propto\Delta^{-\upsilon}\log^{\chi}(p/\Delta),\quad g_{1}\propto\Delta^{-\upsilon},

(S.468)

where we use Eqs. (
S.370
) and (
S.374
).

Second, from the inequality (
S.382
) with
q=2
, we get the upper bound of
\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|
as:

\displaystyle\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|\leq 8\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\bar{g}_{2l}(l^{2}+1)\bar{J}(l)\leq\frac{8\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\bar{g}_{2l}}{l^{\bar{\alpha}}+1},

(S.469)

where we use the condition (
S.346
).
From the inequalities (
S.383
) and (
S.465
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta_{\rm t}\geq\bar{\Delta}-2\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|\geq\frac{3}{4}\Delta-2\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|,

(S.470)

We then choose the parameter
l
so that the following condition is satisfied [see also (
S.384
)]:

\displaystyle\||\bar{\Omega}\rangle-|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle\|\leq\frac{\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|}{\bar{\Delta}-4\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|}\leq\frac{\Delta/\bar{\Delta}}{12p}\longrightarrow\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|\leq\frac{\Delta}{12p+4(\Delta/\bar{\Delta})}\leq\frac{\Delta}{12p}.

(S.471)

By applying the above to (
S.470
), we get the lower bound of

\displaystyle\Delta_{\rm t}\geq\frac{3}{4}\Delta-\frac{\Delta}{6p}\geq\frac{7}{12}\Delta\quad\textrm{for}\quad p\geq 1.

(S.472)

To satisfy the above condition (
S.471
), we have to satisfy the lower bound of
l
from the inequality (
S.469
) as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{8\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\bar{g}_{2l}}{l^{\bar{\alpha}}+1}\leq\frac{\Delta}{12p}\longrightarrow\frac{l^{\bar{\alpha}}}{\bar{g}_{2l}}\geq(8\eta_{1}\eta_{2})\frac{12p}{\Delta}\longrightarrow l\geq\tilde{w}_{1}\left[\frac{p}{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}\log^{\chi}(p/\Delta)\right]^{1/\bar{\alpha}},

(S.473)

where we use
\bar{g}_{2l}=g_{0}+g_{1}\log^{\chi}(2l+1)\lesssim\Delta^{-\upsilon}\log^{\chi}(p/\Delta)+\Delta^{-\upsilon}\log^{\chi}(2l+1)
from (
S.468
).
Under the inequality (
S.473
) for
l
, we also obtain

\displaystyle\bar{g}_{2l}\leq\tilde{w}_{2}\Delta^{-\upsilon}\log^{\chi}(p/\Delta),\quad\tilde{w}_{2}=\mathcal{O}(1).

(S.474)

Note that a similar inequality holds for
\bar{g}_{2ql}=\bar{g}_{4l}
, which will be utilized in (
S.478
).

Furthermore, from the inequality (
S.385
), for an arbitrary quantum state
|\phi\rangle
, we have

\displaystyle\left\||\bar{\Omega}\rangle-|\phi\rangle\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\||\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\phi\rangle\|+\frac{\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|}{\bar{\Delta}-4\left\|\delta H_{\rm t}\right\|}\leq\||\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\phi\rangle\|+\frac{1}{9p},

(S.475)

where we use the inequality (
S.471
) and
(\Delta/\bar{\Delta})/(12p)\leq 1/(9p)
in the second inequality.
By combining the above inequality with (
S.465
) [
\delta_{\rm B}=1/(9p)
], we have

\displaystyle\left\||\Omega\rangle-|\phi\rangle\right\|\leq\left\||\Omega\rangle-|\bar{\Omega}\rangle\right\|+\||\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\phi\rangle\|+\frac{1}{9p}\leq\||\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\phi\rangle\|+\frac{2}{9p}.

(S.476)

Third, for the construction of the AGSP operator, we use the effective Hamiltonian from the truncated Hamiltonian
H_{\rm t}
with
q=2
.
Then, from the inequality (
S.394
) in Theorem
4
, the condition

\displaystyle\||\tilde{\Omega}_{\rm t}\rangle-|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle\|\leq\frac{1}{9p}

(S.477)

is satisfied by

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2\varepsilon_{2}}{\Delta_{\rm t}}}\leq\frac{1}{9p}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\varepsilon_{2}\leq\frac{\Delta_{\rm t}}{162p^{2}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\tau\geq\mathscr{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\bar{g}_{4l}\log\left(\frac{\mathscr{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}l\bar{g}_{2l}p^{2}}{\Delta}\right)+\mathscr{C}^{\prime\prime}_{3}g_{1}\log^{1+\chi}\left(\frac{\mathscr{C}^{\prime\prime}_{4}l\bar{g}_{2l}p^{2}}{\Delta}\right)\quad\textrm{for}\quad\varepsilon_{2}\leq\frac{\Delta_{\rm t}}{162p^{2}}

(S.478)

with
\{\mathscr{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1},\mathscr{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2},\mathscr{C}^{\prime\prime}_{3},\mathscr{C}^{\prime\prime}_{4}\}
constants of
\mathcal{O}(1)
,
where we use the inequality (
S.404
) to derive the upper bound for
\tau
.
Using the obtained upper bounds (
S.473
) and (
S.474
), we choose
\tau
in the form of

\displaystyle\tau=\tilde{w}_{3}\Delta^{-\upsilon}\log^{1+\chi}(p/\Delta),\quad\tilde{w}_{3}=\mathcal{O}(1)

(S.479)

in order to satisfy the condition (
S.477
).
Then, by combining the inequalities (
S.476
) and (
S.477
) with
|\phi\rangle=|\tilde{\Omega}_{\rm t}\rangle
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\||\Omega\rangle-|\tilde{\Omega}_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|\leq\frac{1}{3p},

(S.480)

which yields the first desired condition for
\delta_{K_{p}}
, i.e.,
\delta_{K_{p}}\leq 1/(3p)
.

We next consider the second condition for
\epsilon_{K_{p}}
of
\epsilon_{K_{p}}\leq 1/(9p)
.
The remaining control parameter is
m
, which is the degree of the Chebyshev polynomial.
First, the condition (
S.478
) implies

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}_{\rm t}\geq\Delta_{\rm t}-3\varepsilon_{2}\geq\frac{53}{54}\Delta_{\rm t}\geq\frac{1}{2}\Delta.

(S.481)

from the inequality (
S.395
), where we use
\Delta_{\rm t}\geq 7\Delta/12
as from inequality (
S.472
).
Second, we obtain a similar inequality to (
S.433
) as

\displaystyle\epsilon_{K_{p}}\leq 2e^{-2m\sqrt{\tilde{\Delta}_{\rm t}/\left[4\left(\tau+2c_{0}\bar{g}_{2l}\right)\right]}}\leq 2\exp\left(-m\sqrt{\frac{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}{2\left[\tilde{w}_{3}\log^{\chi+1}(p/\Delta)+2c_{0}\tilde{w}_{2}\log^{\chi}(p/\Delta)\right]}}\right),

(S.482)

where we use the inequality (
S.474
) and Eq. (
S.479
) for
\bar{g}_{2l}
and
\tau
, respectively.
Therefore, the condition
\epsilon_{K_{p}}\leq 1/(9p)
is satisfied by choosing

\displaystyle m=\left\lceil\sqrt{\frac{2\left[\tilde{w}_{3}\log^{\chi+1}(p/\Delta)+2c_{0}\tilde{w}_{2}\log^{\chi}(p/\Delta)\right]}{\Delta^{\upsilon+1}}}\log(18p)\right\rceil\leq\tilde{w}_{4}\Delta^{-(\upsilon+1)/2}\log^{\chi/2+3/2}(p/\Delta),

(S.483)

where
\tilde{w}_{4}=\mathcal{O}(1)
.

We finally estimate the Schmidt rank
{\mathcal{D}}_{K_{p}}
and calculate
\log(3{\mathcal{D}}_{K_{p}})
that appears in the upper bound (
S.458
).
From Lemma
28
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{K_{p}}\leq\left[2+(2d_{2l}l)^{k}\right]^{m}\leq\exp\left[\tilde{w}_{5}m\log(p/\Delta)\right]\leq\exp\left[\tilde{w}_{4}\tilde{w}_{5}\Delta^{-(\upsilon+1)/2}\log^{\chi/2+5/2}(p/\Delta)\right],

(S.484)

where
d_{2l}\lesssim\Delta^{-2\upsilon/k}\log^{\mathfrak{a}}(p/\Delta)
is derived from the inequality (
S.468
) and the condition (
S.473
) for
l
.
We thus upper-bound
\log({\mathcal{D}}_{K_{p}})
by

\displaystyle\log({\mathcal{D}}_{K_{p}})\leq\tilde{w}_{6}\frac{\log^{\chi/2+5/2}(3p/\Delta)}{\Delta^{(\upsilon+1)/2}},\quad\tilde{w}_{6}=\mathcal{O}(1).

(S.485)

Using the upper bound (
S.485
), we immediately derive the first main inequality (
S.446
) as follows.
From the inequality (
S.456
) with (
S.461
) and (
S.462
), the quantum state

|\psi_{p}\rangle:=\frac{K_{p}e^{-i\theta_{p}}|\phi\rangle}{\|K_{p}|\phi\rangle\|}

satisfies

\displaystyle\left\||\psi_{p}\rangle-|\Omega\rangle\right\|\leq\Gamma_{K_{p}}\leq\frac{1}{p}

(S.486)

under an appropriate choice of the phase factor
\theta_{p}
. Also, the Schmidt rank of
|\psi_{p}\rangle
is upper-bounded by

\displaystyle\log\left[{\rm SR}(|\psi_{p}\rangle)\right]\leq\log({\mathcal{D}}_{\phi})+\log({\mathcal{D}}_{K_{p}})\leq\log({\mathcal{D}}_{\phi})+\tilde{w}_{6}\frac{\log^{\chi/2+5/2}(3p/\Delta)}{\Delta^{(\upsilon+1)/2}},

(S.487)

which yields the inequality (
S.446
) by choosing
p=\left\lceil 1/\delta\right\rceil
.

Lastly, by combining the upper bounds (
S.485
) and
\Gamma_{K_{p}}\leq 1/p
with the inequality (
S.460
), we obtain

\displaystyle S(|\Omega\rangle)

\displaystyle\leq\log({\mathcal{D}}_{\phi})+\log(3{\mathcal{D}}_{K_{1}})+\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{p^{2}}\left\{\log(p^{2})+\tilde{w}_{6}\frac{\log^{\chi/2+5/2}\left[3(p+1)/\Delta\right]}{\Delta^{(\upsilon+1)/2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\log({\mathcal{D}}_{\phi})+\tilde{w}_{7}\frac{\log^{\chi/2+5/2}\left(1/\Delta\right)}{\Delta^{(\upsilon+1)/2}},

(S.488)

where we let
\Gamma_{0}=1
in the case of
p=0
and
\tilde{w}_{7}
is an
\mathcal{O}(1)
constant.
This completes the second main inequality (
S.447
), completing the proof of Proposition
30
.
\square

Supplementary Note 8
Proof of Proposition
25
: effective Hamiltonian by boson-number truncation

Supplementary Note 8.1
Effective Hamiltonian by arbitrary projection

For an arbitrary projection
\Pi
, we prove the following proposition
[
66
]
, which is similar to Supplemental Lemma 4 of Ref.
[
29
]
:

Lemma 34
.

Let us set the ground energy to be equal to zero, i.e.,
H|\Omega\rangle=0
.
We consider the Hilbert space spanned by
\Pi
and consider
the effective Hamiltonian
\tilde{H}
in this restricted Hilbert space:

\displaystyle\tilde{H}:=\Pi H\Pi,

(S.489)

Then, the ground state
|\tilde{\Omega}\rangle
of
\tilde{H}
, which satisfies
\Pi|\tilde{\Omega}\rangle=|\tilde{\Omega}\rangle
, is close to the original ground state
|\Omega\rangle
in the sense that

\displaystyle\left\||\Omega\rangle-|\tilde{\Omega}\rangle\right\|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\epsilon_{H}}{\Delta}},\quad\epsilon_{H}:=\frac{\langle\Omega|\Pi H\Pi|\Omega\rangle}{\left\|\Pi|\Omega\rangle\right\|^{2}},

(S.490)

and the spectral gap
\tilde{\Delta}
is lower-bounded by

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}\geq\Delta-3\epsilon_{H}.

(S.491)

Remark.
A straightforward estimation of
\epsilon_{H}
reads

\displaystyle\epsilon_{H}=\frac{\langle\Omega|(1-\Pi)H(1-\Pi)|\Omega\rangle}{\left\|\Pi|\Omega\rangle\right\|^{2}}\leq\frac{\left\|H\right\|\cdot\left\|(1-\Pi)|\Omega\rangle\right\|^{2}}{\left\|\Pi|\Omega\rangle\right\|^{2}}.

(S.492)

However, in the thermodynamic limit (
|\Lambda|\to\infty
), the RHS of the inequality diverges to infinity, and hence we have to estimate
\epsilon_{H}
more carefully (see Proposition
35
).

Supplementary Note 8.1.1
Proof of Lemma
34

We first expand
|\tilde{\Omega}\rangle
as

\displaystyle|\tilde{\Omega}\rangle=e^{i\theta}\sqrt{1-\epsilon_{\tilde{\Omega}}}|\Omega\rangle+\sqrt{\epsilon_{\tilde{\Omega}}}|\psi_{\bot}\rangle,\quad\langle\Omega|\psi_{\bot}\rangle=0,

(S.493)

where
e^{i\theta}
characterizes a phase factor and can be chosen to be equal to
1
(i.e.,
\theta=0
).
We then derive

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{\Omega}|H|\tilde{\Omega}\rangle=\epsilon_{\tilde{\Omega}}\langle\psi_{\bot}|H|\psi_{\bot}\rangle\geq\epsilon_{\tilde{\Omega}}\Delta,

(S.494)

where we use
\langle\Omega|H|\Omega\rangle=0
.
Because of
\Pi|\tilde{\Omega}\rangle=|\tilde{\Omega}\rangle
and

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{\Omega}|H|\tilde{\Omega}\rangle=\langle\tilde{\Omega}|\Pi H\Pi|\tilde{\Omega}\rangle=\langle\tilde{\Omega}|\tilde{H}|\tilde{\Omega}\rangle\leq\frac{\langle\Omega|\Pi\tilde{H}\Pi|\Omega\rangle}{\left\|\Pi|\Omega\rangle\right\|^{2}}=\frac{\langle\Omega|\Pi H\Pi|\Omega\rangle}{\left\|\Pi|\Omega\rangle\right\|^{2}}=\epsilon_{H},

(S.495)

we can reduce the inequality (
S.494
) to

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\tilde{\Omega}}\leq\frac{\epsilon_{H}}{\Delta}.

(S.496)

Then, we consider

\displaystyle\left\||\Omega\rangle-|\tilde{\Omega}\rangle\right\|^{2}=2-2{\rm Re}(\langle\tilde{\Omega}|\Omega\rangle)=2-2\sqrt{1-\epsilon_{\tilde{\Omega}}}\leq 2\epsilon_{\tilde{\Omega}}\leq\frac{2\epsilon_{H}}{\Delta},

(S.497)

where we use
2-2\sqrt{1-x}\leq 2x
for
0\leq x\leq 1
.
Note that for
\epsilon_{H}/\Delta\geq 1
the above upper bound is included in the trivial bound as
\left\||\Omega\rangle-|\tilde{\Omega}\rangle\right\|^{2}\leq 2
.

We next consider the second main inequality (
S.491
).
We let
|\tilde{E}_{1}\rangle
be the first excited state for
\tilde{H}
such that
\tilde{H}|\tilde{E}_{1}\rangle=\tilde{E}_{1}|\tilde{E}_{1}\rangle
.
Then, using
\langle\tilde{E}_{1}|\tilde{\Omega}\rangle=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle|\langle\tilde{E}_{1}|\Omega\rangle|=\left|\langle\tilde{E}_{1}|(|\Omega\rangle-|\tilde{\Omega}\rangle)\right|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\epsilon_{H}}{\Delta}},

(S.498)

where we use the upper bound (
S.497
) in the last inequality.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{E}_{1}=\langle\tilde{E}_{1}|\tilde{H}|\tilde{E}_{1}\rangle=\langle\tilde{E}_{1}|\Pi H\Pi|\tilde{E}_{1}\rangle=\langle\tilde{E}_{1}|H|\tilde{E}_{1}\rangle\geq(1-|\langle\tilde{E}_{1}|\Omega\rangle|^{2})\Delta\geq\left(1-\frac{2\epsilon_{H}}{\Delta}\right)\Delta,

(S.499)

where we use
\Pi|\tilde{E}_{1}\rangle=|\tilde{E}_{1}\rangle
.
On the other hand, the ground energy
\tilde{E}_{0}
is smaller than

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\Omega|\Pi\tilde{H}\Pi|\Omega\rangle}{\left\|\Pi|\Omega\rangle\right\|^{2}}=\frac{\langle\Omega|\Pi H\Pi|\Omega\rangle}{\left\|\Pi|\Omega\rangle\right\|^{2}}=\epsilon_{H}.

(S.500)

By combining the above inequalities, we obtain the lower bound of the spectral gap:

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}=\tilde{E}_{1}-\tilde{E}_{0}\geq\Delta-3\epsilon_{H}.

(S.501)

This completes the proof.
\square

[ End of Proof of Lemma
34
]

Supplementary Note 8.2
Projection of boson-number truncation

Here, in Lemma
34
, we choose the projection
\Pi
as the boson-number truncation operator.
We briefly show the setup of Proposition
25
again.
Under the assumption of

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{i,>N}|\Omega\rangle\right\|\leq\mathfrak{c}e^{-\mathfrak{b}N^{1/\mathfrak{a}}}\quad\textrm{for}\quad\forall i\in\Lambda,

(S.502)

we will determine an appropriate truncation boson number.

Supplementary Figure 8:
Schematic picture of boson-number truncation.
To apply the method to high-dimensional systems in the future, we consider the two-dimensional lattice here.
The truncation of the boson numbers poly-logarithmically increases as the site separates from the boundary between
L
and
R
.

To make the discussion more general, we consider the high-dimensional systems.
We slice the total system to
\{S_{x}\}_{x=-\infty}^{\infty}
such that

\displaystyle\Lambda=\bigotimes_{x=-\infty}^{\infty}S_{x},

(S.503)

where
x=0
is the boundary between
L
and
R
(see Supplementary Fig.
8
):

\displaystyle L=\bigotimes_{x=-\infty}^{0}S_{x},\quad R=\bigotimes_{x=1}^{\infty}S_{x}.

(S.504)

Let us define the truncation number as
N_{i}
(
i\in S_{x}
) as

\displaystyle N_{i}=\mathfrak{b}^{-\mathfrak{a}}\left[\log\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)+\log\left({|x|^{3D}+1}\right)\right]^{\mathfrak{a}}\leq 2^{\mathfrak{a}}\mathfrak{b}^{-\mathfrak{a}}\left[\log^{\mathfrak{a}}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)+\log^{\mathfrak{a}}\left({|x|^{3D}+1}\right)\right].

(S.505)

We then obtain from (
S.502
)

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{i,>N_{i}}|\Omega\rangle\right\|\leq\frac{\mathfrak{c}\epsilon_{\rm B}}{x^{3D}+1}\quad\textrm{for}\quad\forall i\in S_{x}.

(S.506)

Note that the above setup is the same as in Proposition
25
in the one-dimensional cases.

We prove the following proposition (see
Supplementary Note 8.4
for the proof):

Proposition 35
.

Let us adopt the interaction decay of high-dimensional Hamiltonians as

\displaystyle\left\|h_{Z}\Pi_{\Lambda,\leq N}\right\|\leq J_{Z}N^{k/2}

(S.507)

with

\displaystyle\max_{i,i^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{Z:Z\ni\{i,i^{\prime}\}}J_{Z}\right)\leq\frac{\bar{J}_{1}}{d_{i,i^{\prime}}^{\alpha}+1}.

(S.508)

Under the projection of
\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}
, the parameter
\epsilon_{H}
in Lemma
34
is upper-bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\epsilon_{H}\leq\frac{\gamma^{2}k\bar{J}_{1}\mathfrak{c}\epsilon_{\rm B}|S_{0}|^{2}2^{D+3}}{1-\left(4\gamma\mathfrak{c}\epsilon_{\rm B}|S_{0}|\right)^{2}}\left[\frac{3f_{0}\mathfrak{w}_{D}(\alpha-D)}{\alpha-D-1}+f_{0}\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)\right],

(S.509)

where we define
f_{0}=\left(2^{\mathfrak{a}+1}/\mathfrak{b}^{\mathfrak{a}}\right)^{k/2}
, and a constant
\mathfrak{w}_{D}
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}_{D}:=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{Z}}\left[\frac{\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(|x|^{3D}+1\right)}{|x|+1}\right].

(S.510)

Remark.

From the statement, we roughly obtain

\displaystyle\epsilon_{H}=\epsilon_{\rm B}\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)\mathcal{O}(|\partial L|^{2}),

(S.511)

where we use
S_{0}=\partial L
.

Supplementary Note 8.3
Proof of Proposition
25

Based on Proposition
35
, we here prove Proposition
25
.
For the convenience of readers, we show it again.

Proposition
25
.

Let us adopt
\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}
as the projection
\bar{\Pi}
, which is given by

\displaystyle\bar{\Pi}=\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}:=\bigotimes_{x\in\Lambda}\Pi_{x,\leq N_{x}},

(S.512)

with

\displaystyle N_{x}=\mathfrak{b}^{-\mathfrak{a}}\left[\log\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)+\log\left({|x|^{3}+1}\right)\right]^{\mathfrak{a}}\leq 2^{\mathfrak{a}}\mathfrak{b}^{-\mathfrak{a}}\left[\log^{\mathfrak{a}}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)+\log^{\mathfrak{a}}\left({|x|^{3}+1}\right)\right].

(S.513)

Then, the Hamiltonian
\bar{H}=\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}H\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}
preserves the ground state and the spectral gap as follows:

\displaystyle\left\||\Omega\rangle-|\bar{\Omega}\rangle\right\|\leq\delta_{\rm B},\quad\bar{\Delta}\geq\Delta\left(1-\frac{3}{2}\delta_{\rm B}^{2}\right),

(S.514)

with
\epsilon_{\rm B}
given by

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\rm B}=w_{0}\delta_{\rm B}^{2}\Delta\log^{-\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\delta_{\rm B}^{-1}\right),

(S.515)

where
w_{0}
is an
\mathcal{O}(1)
constant and we choose
\delta_{\rm B}
such that
\delta_{\rm B}\leq 1/\sqrt{6}
.
Note that
|\bar{\Omega}\rangle
and
\bar{\Delta}
are the ground state and the spectral gap of
\bar{H}
.

In 1D setup, we reduce the inequality (
S.509
) to

\displaystyle\epsilon_{H}\leq\frac{16\gamma^{2}k\bar{J}_{1}\mathfrak{c}\epsilon_{\rm B}}{1-\left(4\gamma\mathfrak{c}\epsilon_{\rm B}\right)^{2}}\left[\frac{3f_{0}\mathfrak{w}_{1}(\alpha-1)}{\alpha-2}+f_{0}\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)\right]=:\bar{\epsilon}_{H}.

(S.516)

For the proof, we set

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2\bar{\epsilon}_{H}}{\Delta}}\leq\delta_{\rm B}\longrightarrow\bar{\epsilon}_{H}\leq\frac{\Delta}{2}\delta_{\rm B}^{2}.

(S.517)

From the inequality (
S.490
), this choice yields the first main inequality in (
S.514
).
Because the parameters
\{\gamma,\mathfrak{c},\bar{J}_{1},f_{0},\mathfrak{w}_{1},\alpha,\mathfrak{a},k\}
are
\mathcal{O}(1)
constants, the above inequality is satisfied by choosing as in (
S.515
).

Under the same choice, we also obtain from the inequality (
S.491
)

\displaystyle\bar{\Delta}\geq\Delta-3\epsilon_{H}\geq\Delta\left(1-\frac{3}{2}\delta_{\rm B}^{2}\right),

(S.518)

which gives the desired lower bound for
\bar{\Delta}
in (
S.514
).
This completes the proof of Proposition
25
.
\square

Supplementary Note 8.4
Proof of Proposition
35

We start from the lower bound of
\left\|\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle\right\|^{2}=\langle\Omega|\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle
, which is also given by

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle=1-\langle\Omega|\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}^{\rm c}|\Omega\rangle=1-\left\|\Pi^{\rm c}_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle\right\|^{2},

(S.519)

where
\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}+\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}^{\rm c}=1
.

We adopt a set of subset
X_{1},X_{2},\ldots,X_{|\Lambda|}
such that
X_{s+1}\supset X_{s}
and
|X_{s+1}-X_{s}|=1
.
By denoting

\displaystyle\Pi_{s}=\bigotimes_{i\in X_{s}}\Pi_{i,\leq N_{i}},

(S.520)

we obtain

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\Pi_{|\Lambda|}|\Omega\rangle=|\Omega\rangle+\sum_{s=1}^{|\Lambda|}\left(\Pi_{s}-\Pi_{s-1}\right)|\Omega\rangle,

(S.521)

where we let
X_{0}=\emptyset
.
From the above equation, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi^{\rm c}_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle\right\|=\left\||\Omega\rangle-\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle\right\|\leq\sum_{s=1}^{|\Lambda|}\left\|\left(\Pi_{s}-\Pi_{s-1}\right)|\Omega\rangle\right\|\leq\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\left\|\left(\Pi_{i,\leq N_{i}}-1\right)|\Omega\rangle\right\|=\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\left\|\Pi_{i,>N_{i}}|\Omega\rangle\right\|.

(S.522)

Using the inequalities (
S.502
) and (
S.506
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\left\|\Pi_{i,>N_{i}}|\Omega\rangle\right\|\leq\mathfrak{c}\sum_{i\in\Lambda}e^{-\mathfrak{b}N_{i}^{1/\mathfrak{a}}}\leq\mathfrak{c}\epsilon_{\rm B}\sum_{x=-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{i\in S_{x}}\frac{1}{|x|^{3D}+1}\leq 4\gamma\mathfrak{c}\epsilon_{\rm B}|S_{0}|,

(S.523)

where we use
|S_{x}|+|S_{-x}|\leq\sum_{i\in S_{0}}|\partial i[x]|\leq\gamma(x^{D-1}+1)|S_{0}|
for
x\geq 0
[see (
S.4
) for the definition of
\gamma
] to derive

\displaystyle\sum_{x=-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{i\in S_{x}}\frac{1}{|x|^{3D}+1}\leq\gamma|S_{0}|\sum_{x=0}^{\infty}\frac{x^{D-1}+1}{x^{3D}+1}\leq 4\gamma|S_{0}|.

(S.524)

Note that
\sum_{x=0}^{\infty}\frac{x^{D-1}+1}{x^{3D}+1}
is maximized for
D=1
and
\sum_{x=0}^{\infty}\frac{2}{x^{3}+1}\approx 3.37301<4
.
We thus prove
\left\|\Pi^{\rm c}_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle\right\|\leq 4\gamma\mathfrak{c}\epsilon_{\rm B}|S_{0}|
, which lower-bound Eq. (
S.519
) by

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle\geq 1-\left(4\gamma\mathfrak{c}\epsilon_{\rm B}|S_{0}|\right)^{2}.

(S.525)

We then aim to calculate
\epsilon_{H}
, i.e.,
\epsilon_{H}:=\langle\Omega|\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}(H-E_{0})\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle/\langle\Omega|\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle
.
By letting

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbf{N}_{Z}}:=\bigotimes_{i\in Z}\Pi_{i,\leq N_{i}}\and\Pi_{\mathbf{N}_{Z}}^{\rm c}:=1-\Pi_{\mathbf{N}_{Z}},

(S.526)

we have from
\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}=\Pi_{\mathbf{N}_{Z}}\Pi_{\mathbf{N}_{Z^{\rm c}}}

\displaystyle\langle\Omega|\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}H\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{Z:Z\subset\Lambda}\langle\Omega|\Pi_{\mathbf{N}_{Z^{\rm c}}}\Pi_{\mathbf{N}_{Z}}h_{Z}\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{Z:Z\subset\Lambda}\left(\langle\Omega|\Pi_{\mathbf{N}_{Z^{\rm c}}}h_{Z}\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle-\langle\Omega|\Pi_{\mathbf{N}_{Z^{\rm c}}}\Pi^{\rm c}_{\mathbf{N}_{Z}}h_{Z}\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{Z:Z\subset\Lambda}\left(\langle\Omega|h_{Z}\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle-\langle\Omega|\Pi^{\rm c}_{\mathbf{N}_{Z}}h_{Z}\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=\langle\Omega|H\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle-\sum_{Z:Z\subset\Lambda}\langle\Omega|\Pi^{\rm c}_{\mathbf{N}_{Z}}h_{Z}\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\Omega|\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle E_{0}-\sum_{Z:Z\subset\Lambda}\langle\Omega|\Pi^{\rm c}_{\mathbf{N}_{Z}}h_{Z}\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle,

(S.527)

where we use
\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}\Pi_{\mathbf{N}_{Z^{\rm c}}}=\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}
and
[\Pi_{\mathbf{N}_{Z^{\rm c}}},h_{Z}]=[\Pi_{\mathbf{N}_{Z^{\rm c}}},\Pi^{\rm c}_{\mathbf{N}_{Z}}]=0
.
From the above equation, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\langle\Omega|\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}(H-E_{0})\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}|\Omega\rangle\right|\leq\sum_{Z:Z\subset\Lambda}\left\|\Pi^{\rm c}_{\mathbf{N}_{Z}}|\Omega\rangle\right\|\cdot\left\|h_{Z}\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}\right\|.

(S.528)

Using the inequality (
S.507
), i.e.,
\left\|h_{Z}\Pi_{\Lambda,\leq N}\right\|\leq J_{Z}N^{k/2}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|h_{Z}\Pi_{\mathbf{N}_{Z}}\right\|\leq J_{Z}\left[\max_{i\in Z}(N_{i})\right]^{k/2},

(S.529)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi^{\rm c}_{\mathbf{N}_{Z}}|\Omega\rangle\right\|\leq|Z|\max_{i\in Z}\left(\mathfrak{c}e^{-\mathfrak{b}N_{i}^{1/\mathfrak{a}}}\right)\leq k\max_{i\in Z}\left(\mathfrak{c}e^{-\mathfrak{b}N_{i}^{1/\mathfrak{a}}}\right),

(S.530)

where we use a similar inequality to (
S.522
).
By applying the above two inequalities to the RHS of the inequality (
S.528
). we upper-bound

\displaystyle\sum_{Z:Z\subset\Lambda}\left\|h_{Z}\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}\right\|\cdot\left\|\Pi^{\rm c}_{\mathbf{N}_{Z}}|\Omega\rangle\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{x_{1}=-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{x_{2}:|x_{2}|\geq|x_{1}|}\sum_{i_{1}\in S_{x_{1}}}\sum_{i_{2}\in S_{x_{2}}}\sum_{Z:Z\ni\{i_{1},i_{2}\}}kJ_{Z}N_{x}^{k/2}\cdot\frac{\mathfrak{c}\epsilon_{\rm B}}{|x_{1}|^{3D}+1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{x_{1}=-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{x_{2}:|x_{2}|\geq|x_{1}|}\frac{k\bar{J}_{1}|S_{x_{1}}|\cdot|S_{x_{2}}|}{(|x_{2}|-|x_{1}|)^{\alpha}+1}\left\{2^{\mathfrak{a}}\mathfrak{b}^{-\mathfrak{a}}\left[\log^{\mathfrak{a}}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)+\log^{\mathfrak{a}}\left({|x_{2}|^{3D}+1}\right)\right]\right\}^{k/2}\cdot\frac{\mathfrak{c}\epsilon_{\rm B}}{|x_{1}|^{3D}+1},

(S.531)

where we use the definition of
N_{x}
in Eq. (
S.505
), which also gives the inequality (
S.506
), and the upper bound (
S.508
) with
d_{i_{1},i_{2}}\leq|x_{2}|-|x_{1}|
.

We obtain the upper bound of

\displaystyle\left\{2^{\mathfrak{a}}\mathfrak{b}^{-\mathfrak{a}}\left[\log^{\mathfrak{a}}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)+\log^{\mathfrak{a}}\left({|x_{2}|^{3D}+1}\right)\right]\right\}^{k/2}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{k/2}2^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\mathfrak{b}^{-\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left[\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left({|x_{2}|^{3D}+1}\right)+\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(2^{\mathfrak{a}+1}/\mathfrak{b}^{\mathfrak{a}}\right)^{k/2}\left[\mathfrak{w}_{D}(|x_{2}|+1)+\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)\right]=f_{0}\mathfrak{w}_{D}(|x_{2}|+1)+f_{0}\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)

(S.532)

with
f_{0}=\left(2^{\mathfrak{a}+1}/\mathfrak{b}^{\mathfrak{a}}\right)^{k/2}
and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}_{D}:=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{Z}}\left[\frac{\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(|x|^{3D}+1\right)}{|x|+1}\right].

(S.533)

We therefore reduce the RHS of the inequality (
S.531
) to

\displaystyle\sum_{Z:Z\subset\Lambda}\left\|h_{Z}\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}\right\|\cdot\left\|\Pi^{\rm c}_{\mathbf{N}_{Z}}|\Omega\rangle\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{2}k\bar{J}_{1}\mathfrak{c}\epsilon_{\rm B}|S_{0}|^{2}\sum_{x_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\left[f_{0}\mathfrak{w}_{D}(x_{1}+r+1)+f_{0}\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)\right]\cdot\frac{(x_{1}^{D-1}+1)\left[\left(x_{1}+r\right)^{D-1}+1\right]}{\left(|x_{1}|^{3D}+1\right)\left(r^{\alpha}+1\right)},

(S.534)

where we let
|x_{2}|=|x_{1}|+r
and use
|S_{x}|+|S_{-x}|\leq\gamma(|x|^{D-1}+1)|S_{0}|
for
\forall x>0
from (
S.4
).
In the following, we separately estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{x_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}f_{0}\mathfrak{w}_{D}(x_{1}+r+1)\frac{(x_{1}^{D-1}+1)\left[\left(x_{1}+r\right)^{D-1}+1\right]}{\left(|x_{1}|^{3D}+1\right)\left(r^{\alpha}+1\right)},

(S.535)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{x_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\left[f_{0}\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)\right]\cdot\frac{(x_{1}^{D-1}+1)\left[\left(x_{1}+r\right)^{D-1}+1\right]}{\left(|x_{1}|^{3D}+1\right)\left(r^{\alpha}+1\right)}.

(S.536)

We begin with the quantity (
S.535
).
Using
(s^{a}+1)(s^{b}+1)\leq 2(s^{a+b}+1)
*11
*11

*11

The inequality derive from
2(s^{a+b}+1)-(s^{a}+1)(s^{b}+1)=s^{a+b}-s^{a}-s^{b}+1=(s^{a}-1)(s^{b}-1)
.
for
s\in\mathbb{N}
and
a,b\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{x_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}f_{0}\mathfrak{w}_{D}(x_{1}+r+1)\frac{(x_{1}^{D-1}+1)\left[\left(x_{1}+r\right)^{D-1}+1\right]}{\left(|x_{1}|^{3D}+1\right)\left(r^{\alpha}+1\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 2f_{0}\mathfrak{w}_{D}\sum_{x_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\frac{(x_{1}^{D-1}+1)\left[\left(x_{1}+r\right)^{D}+1\right]}{\left(|x_{1}|^{3D}+1\right)\left(r^{\alpha}+1\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 2f_{0}\mathfrak{w}_{D}2^{D}\sum_{x_{1}=0}^{\infty}\frac{(x_{1}^{D-1}+1)(x_{1}^{D}+1)}{x_{1}^{3D}+1}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\frac{r^{D}+1}{r^{\alpha}+1},

(S.537)

where we use
(x_{1}+r)^{D}+1\leq\max\left[(2x_{1})^{D}+1,(2r)^{D}+1\right]\leq 2^{D}\left(x_{1}^{D}+1\right)(r^{D}+1)
.
For the summations in the RHS of (
S.537
), we can derive

\displaystyle\sum_{x_{1}=0}^{\infty}\frac{(x_{1}^{D-1}+1)(x_{1}^{D}+1)}{x_{1}^{3D}+1}\leq 2\sum_{x_{1}=0}^{\infty}\frac{x_{1}^{2D-1}+1}{x_{1}^{3D}+1}\leq 2\sum_{x_{1}=0}^{\infty}\frac{x_{1}+1}{x_{1}^{3}+1}\approx 5.59629<6,

(S.538)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\frac{r^{D}+1}{r^{\alpha}+1}=2+\sum_{r=2}^{\infty}\frac{r^{D}+1}{r^{\alpha}+1}\leq 2+2\sum_{r=2}^{\infty}r^{D-\alpha}\leq 2+2\int_{1}^{\infty}z^{D-\alpha}dz=2+\frac{2}{\alpha-D-1}=\frac{2(\alpha-D)}{\alpha-D-1},

(S.539)

where we use
\alpha>D+1
and
D\geq 1
.
By applying them to the inequality (
S.537
), we obtain

\displaystyle\textrm{\eqref{eta_D_term_estimation0}}\leq\frac{24f_{0}\mathfrak{w}_{D}2^{D}(\alpha-D)}{\alpha-D-1}.

(S.540)

In the same way, for the summation (
S.536
), we can derive

(
S.536
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\left[f_{0}\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)\right]\frac{(x_{1}^{D-1}+1)\left[\left(x_{1}+r\right)^{D-1}+1\right]}{\left(|x_{1}|^{3D}+1\right)\left(r^{\alpha}+1\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{D-1}\left[f_{0}\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)\right]\sum_{x_{1}=0}^{\infty}\frac{(x_{1}^{D-1}+1)(x_{1}^{D-1}+1)}{x_{1}^{3D}+1}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\frac{r^{D-1}+1}{r^{\alpha}+1}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{D-1}\left[f_{0}\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)\right]\times(6.74601\cdots)\times(2.07666\cdots)\leq 2^{D+3}\left[f_{0}\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)\right],

(S.541)

where we use
\alpha>D+1
.

By applying the inequality (
S.540
) and (
S.541
) to the RHS of (
S.534
), we prove

\displaystyle\sum_{Z:Z\subset\Lambda}\left\|h_{Z}\Pi_{\mathbf{N}}\right\|\cdot\left\|\Pi^{\rm c}_{\mathbf{N}_{Z}}|\Omega\rangle\right\|\leq\gamma^{2}k\bar{J}_{1}\mathfrak{c}\epsilon_{\rm B}|S_{0}|^{2}\cdot 2^{D+3}\left[\frac{3f_{0}\mathfrak{w}_{D}(\alpha-D)}{\alpha-D-1}+f_{0}\log^{\mathfrak{a}k/2}\left(\epsilon_{\rm B}^{-1}\right)\right].

(S.542)

By applying the above inequality to (
S.528
) and combining it with (
S.525
),
we prove the main inequality (
S.509
).
This completes the proof.
\square

Supplementary Note 9
Proof of Proposition
26
: Error estimation of interaction truncation

The proofs of the inequalities (
S.383
), (
S.384
) and (
S.385
) immediately follows from the proof of Supplemental Lemma 4 of Ref.
[
29
]
.
We only have to prove the inequality (
S.382
).
For this purpose, we use the following lemma:

Lemma 36
.

Let us define the subsets
X
and
Y
as

\displaystyle X=(-\infty,x],\quad Y=[y,\infty),\quad y-x=r.

(S.543)

Then, for an arbitrary site
i\in\Lambda[r_{0}]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\overline{V}_{X,Y}:=\sum_{Z:Z\cap X\neq\emptyset,Z\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\left\|\bar{h}_{Z}\right\|\leq 2\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\bar{g}_{|x|+r}\left(r^{2}+1\right)\bar{J}(r),

(S.544)

where we define the parameter
\eta_{p}
(
\geq 1
) by

\displaystyle\sum_{y=y_{0}}^{\infty}\bar{g}_{r+y}(y^{p-1}+1)\bar{J}(y)\leq\eta_{p}\bar{g}_{r+y_{0}}(y_{0}^{p}+1)\bar{J}(y_{0})\quad\textrm{for}\quad p<\alpha.

(S.545)

For convenience, we here relabel

\displaystyle B_{0}\setminus\tilde{B}_{0}\to B_{-1},\quad\tilde{B}_{0}\to B_{0},\quad\tilde{B}_{q+1}\to B_{q+1},\quad B_{q+1}\setminus\tilde{B}_{q+1}\to B_{q+2}.

(S.546)

We then define

\displaystyle X_{s}:=\bigcup_{j\geq s+2}B_{j},\quad\Lambda_{s}=\bigcup_{j\geq s}B_{j},

(S.547)

where
s\in[-1,q]
.
From the definition of the truncated Hamiltonian (
S.378
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\bar{H}-H_{\rm t}\|\leq\sum_{s=-1}^{q}\overline{V}_{B_{s},X_{s}}.

(S.548)

Here,
B_{s}\subset[-\infty,-ql/2+sl]
for
s\in[-1,q]
from the definition, and hence by applying Lemma
36
with
x=ql/2+l
and
r=l
, we obtain the desired inequality of

\displaystyle\|\bar{H}-H_{\rm t}\|\leq 2\eta_{1}\eta_{2}(q+2)\bar{g}_{ql/2+2l}\left(l^{2}+1\right)\bar{J}(l)\leq 4\eta_{1}\eta_{2}q\bar{g}_{ql}\left(l^{2}+1\right)\bar{J}(l),

(S.549)

where we use the monotonic increase of
\bar{g}_{x}
and
q\geq 4
(which gives
q/2+2\leq q
).
This completes the proof.
\square

Supplementary Note 9.0.1
Proof of Lemma
36
.

Supplementary Figure 9:
Block-block interaction
\overline{V}_{X,Y}
. We denote the right-end site of
X
and the left-end site of
Y
as
x
and
y
, respectively.
The interaction
\overline{V}_{X,Y}
picks up all the interaction norms of
\left\|\bar{h}_{Z}\right\|
that act on both
X
and
Y
.

As long as
{\rm diam}(Z)=s
with
Z\ni x
, we have
Z\subset[-|x|-s,|x|+s]
, and hence

\displaystyle\sum_{Z:Z\ni x,{\rm diam}(Z)=s}\left\|\bar{h}_{Z}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i:d_{x,i}=s}\ \sum_{Z:Z\ni\{i,x\},Z\subset[-|x|-s,|x|+s]}\left\|\bar{h}_{Z}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\bar{g}_{|x|+s}\bar{J}(s),

(S.550)

where we use
|\partial x[s]|\leq 2
in one dimension.
In general, for
x^{\prime}\in\Lambda
and
Y\subseteq\Lambda
such that
d_{x^{\prime},Y}=r^{\prime}
,
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{Z:Z\cap\{x^{\prime}\}\neq\emptyset,Z\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\left\|\bar{h}_{Z}\right\|=\sum_{Z:Z\ni x^{\prime},{\rm diam}(Z)\geq r^{\prime}}\left\|\bar{h}_{Z}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{s=r^{\prime}}^{\infty}\bar{g}_{|x^{\prime}|+s}\bar{J}(s)

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta_{1}\bar{g}_{|x^{\prime}|+r^{\prime}}\left(r^{\prime}+1\right)\bar{J}(r^{\prime}),

(S.551)

where we use the inequality (
S.545
) with
p=1
.
Therefore, from
d_{X,Y}=r
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{Z:Z\cap X\neq\emptyset,Z\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\left\|\bar{h}_{Z}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{x^{\prime}=-\infty}^{x}\sum_{Z:Z\cap\{x^{\prime}\}\neq\emptyset,Z\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\left\|\bar{h}_{Z}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta_{1}\sum_{x^{\prime}=-\infty}^{x}\bar{g}_{|x^{\prime}|+x-x^{\prime}+r}\left[(x-x^{\prime}+r)+1\right]\bar{J}(x-x^{\prime}+r),

(S.552)

where we use
r^{\prime}=d_{x^{\prime},Y}=d_{x,Y}+(x-x^{\prime})=r+x-x^{\prime}
.
By replacing
x^{\prime}
with
x-s
, we reduce the above inequality to

\displaystyle 2\eta_{1}\sum_{x^{\prime}=-\infty}^{x}\bar{g}_{|x^{\prime}|+x-x^{\prime}+r}\left[(x-x^{\prime}+r)+1\right]\bar{J}(x-x^{\prime}+r)

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta_{1}\sum_{s=0}^{\infty}\bar{g}_{|x|+r+s}\left[(r+s)+1\right]\bar{J}(r+s)

\displaystyle=2\eta_{1}\sum_{s=r}^{\infty}\bar{g}_{|x|+s}\left(s+1\right)\bar{J}(s)

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\bar{g}_{|x|+r}\left(r^{2}+1\right)\bar{J}(r).

(S.553)

By combining the inequalities (
S.552
) and (
S.553
), we prove the main inequality (
S.544
).
This completes the proof.
\square

Supplementary Note 10
Proof of Theorem
4
: Effective Hamiltonian theory

In this section, we prove Theorem
4
, which ensures the preservation of the ground state and the spectral gap by the multi-energy cut-off.
The main proof will be shown in
Supplementary Note 10.4
.

Supplementary Note 10.1
Notations

We first remind several notations.
The projection operator onto the eigenspace of
h_{s}
is defined as

\displaystyle\Pi^{(s)}_{I}=\sum_{E_{s,j}\in I}|E_{s,j}\rangle\langle E_{s,j}|

(S.554)

for
I\subset\mathbb{R}
. Especially for
\Pi^{(s)}_{(-\infty,E)}
and
\Pi^{(s)}_{(-\infty,E]}
, we denote them by
\Pi^{(s)}_{<E}
and
\Pi^{(s)}_{\leq E}
, respectively.
In the same way, we define
\Pi^{(s)}_{>E}
and
\Pi^{(s)}_{\geq E}
.
By using the above notations, we define the projection
\tilde{\Pi}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{\Pi}=\bigotimes_{s=0}^{q+1}\Pi^{(s)}_{\leq\tau_{s}}.

(S.555)

The effective Hamiltonian
\tilde{H}_{\rm t}
is

\displaystyle\tilde{H}_{\rm t}=\tilde{\Pi}H_{\rm t}\tilde{\Pi}

(S.556)

for
s=0,1,2\ldots,q+1
, where we choose the cut-off energies
\{\tau_{s}\}_{s=0}^{q+1}
as

\displaystyle\tau_{s}=E_{s,0}+\tau\quad\textrm{for}\quad s=0,1,2\ldots,q+1.

(S.557)

We denote the ground state of
\tilde{H}_{\rm t}
by
|\tilde{\Omega}_{\rm t}\rangle
.

For the total Hamiltonian
H_{\rm t}
, we define
\{E_{{\rm t},j},|E_{{\rm t},j}\rangle\}_{j}
are the eigenvalues and the eigenstates of
H_{\rm t}
, respectively.
Using them, we define
\Pi_{{\rm t},I}
as

\displaystyle\Pi_{{\rm t},I}=\sum_{E_{{\rm t},j}\in I}|E_{{\rm t},j}\rangle\langle E_{{\rm t},j}|.

(S.558)

Supplementary Note 10.2
Multi-commutator bound

For the proof of the main theorem, we consider the norm of
\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},\geq E+E^{\prime}}O_{s}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\right\|
that plays a key role (see the proof below), where
O_{s}
commute with
h_{s}
:
[O_{s},h_{s}]=0
.
A standard approach in Ref.
[
51
]
utilizes the imaginary time evolution as

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{{\rm t},\geq E+E^{\prime}}O_{s}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},\geq E+E^{\prime}}e^{-\beta H_{\rm t}}e^{\beta H_{\rm t}}O_{s}e^{-\beta H_{\rm t}}e^{\beta H_{\rm t}}\Pi_{\leq E}\right\|\leq e^{-\beta E^{\prime}}\left\|e^{\beta H_{\rm t}}O_{s}e^{-\beta H_{\rm t}}\right\|.

(S.559)

Here, the Hamiltonian
H_{\rm t}
has an unbounded norm as a site becomes distant from the boundary [see the inequality (
S.375
)].
As shown in the following Lemma
37
, the multi-commutator
{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}^{m}(O_{s})
has a norm that scales as
m^{m(1+\chi)}
(see
Supplementary Note 10.2.1
for the proof):

Lemma 37
.

Let us define
O_{Z_{0}}
as an arbitrary operator with unit norm, where
Z_{0}
satisfies

\displaystyle Z_{0}\subseteq[-\ell,\ell],\quad|Z_{0}|\leq k.

(S.560)

Then, for an arbitrary
k
-local Hamiltonian
\bar{H}
satisfying the conditions (
S.375
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{\bar{H}}^{m}(O_{Z_{0}})\right\|\leq 2^{\bar{\alpha}}\left(\tilde{c}_{1}m\bar{g}_{m+\ell}\right)^{m}+2\left(\frac{2+\bar{\alpha}}{\bar{\alpha}}\right)\left(\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}m^{\chi+1}\right)^{m},

(S.561)

with
\tilde{c}_{1}
and
\tilde{c}_{2}
defined as

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{1}=\frac{2^{\chi+\bar{\alpha}+3}4k\eta_{1}}{1-2^{-\bar{\alpha}}},\quad\tilde{c}_{2}=\tilde{c}_{1}\left[\frac{2\chi(2+\bar{\alpha})}{\bar{\alpha}}\right]^{\chi}.

(S.562)

We remind that the parameter
\chi
has been defined as
\chi=k\mathfrak{a}/2
in the main inequality (
S.350
).

Remark.

In the lemma, we adopt
0^{0}=1
for
m=0
.
By taking the leading term with respect to
m
, we have
