\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{\bar{H}}^{m}\left(O_{Z_{0}}\right)\right\|\propto c^{m}m^{(\chi+1)m}

(S.563)

with
c
an
\mathcal{O}(1)
constant.
Even in the case where the short-range (or exponentially decaying) interactions are considered,
one can derive
*12
*12

*12

In the short-range interacting cases, the summation in (
S.577
) in the proof should be appropriately modified.

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{\bar{H}}^{m}\left(O_{Z_{0}}\right)\right\|\propto\left[c\log^{\chi}(m)\right]^{m}m^{m},

(S.564)

which still makes the imaginary time evolution
\left\|e^{\beta{\rm ad}_{\bar{H}}}\left(O_{Z_{0}}\right)\right\|
diverge to infinity.

By applying this lemma to
H_{\rm t}
, we immediately obtain the following corollary [The proof is immediately obtained by combining the inequality (
S.381
) with the inequality (
S.561
).]:

Corollary 38
.

Let us consider
h_{s,s+1}
in Eq. (
S.378
) for
s=[0,q]
, which is supported on
[-ql/2-l,ql/2+l]
from the definition.
Then, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}^{m}(h_{s,s+1})\right\|\leq c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}\left(2^{\bar{\alpha}}\left(\tilde{c}_{1}m\bar{g}_{m+ql}\right)^{m}+2\left(\frac{2+\bar{\alpha}}{\bar{\alpha}}\right)\left(\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}m^{\chi+1}\right)^{m}\right),

(S.565)

where we use the definition of
\bar{g}_{ql}
in (
S.381
).

In particular, when we consider a multi-commutator of
{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}^{m}\left(O_{s}\right)
for an operator that commutes with
h_{s}
, we prove the following statement that meets our purpose (see
Supplementary Note 10.2.2
for the proof):

Proposition 39
.

For an arbitrary operator
O_{s}
such that
[O_{s},h_{s}]=0
, we prove the inequality of

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}^{m}(O_{s})\right\|\leq(T_{m}m)^{m}\left\|O_{s}\right\|,\quad T_{m}=(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})\bar{g}_{m+ql}+\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}m^{\chi},

(S.566)

where
\tilde{c}_{3}
is defined as

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{3}=2^{\bar{\alpha}}+\frac{2(2+\bar{\alpha})}{\bar{\alpha}}.

(S.567)

Supplementary Note 10.2.1
Proof of Lemma
37

We define the length scale
\ell_{s}
(
s\geq-1
) as

\displaystyle\ell_{s}=2^{s},\quad\ell_{-1}=0.

(S.568)

Then, we consider a general region
[-R,R]
and define the Hamiltonian
\bar{H}_{R}
as follows:

\displaystyle\bar{H}_{R}:=\sum_{s=0}^{\infty}\bar{H}_{R,s},\quad\bar{H}_{R,s}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|Z|\leq k,Z\subset[-R,R]\\
{\rm diam}(Z)\in(\ell_{s-1},\ell_{s}]\end{subarray}}\bar{h}_{Z},

(S.569)

with

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z\subset[-R,R]:Z\ni i\\
{\rm diam}(Z)\in(\ell_{s-1},\ell_{s}]\end{subarray}}\left\|\bar{h}_{Z}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i^{\prime}:d_{i,i^{\prime}}\geq\ell_{s-1}}\sum_{Z\subset[-R,R]:Z\ni\{i,i^{\prime}\}}\left\|\bar{h}_{Z}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\bar{g}_{R}\sum_{r=\ell_{s-1}}^{\infty}\sum_{i^{\prime}:d_{i,i^{\prime}}=r}\bar{J}(r)\leq 2\bar{g}_{R}\eta_{1}(\ell_{s-1}+1)\bar{J}(\ell_{s-1}),

(S.570)

where we use the condition (
S.375
) and the inequality (
S.545
) with
p=1
.

By using

\displaystyle\bar{H}=\sum_{s=0}^{\infty}\bar{H}_{\infty,s},\quad\bar{H}_{\infty,s}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z\subset\Lambda\\
{\rm diam}(Z)\in(\ell_{s-1},\ell_{s}]\end{subarray}}\bar{h}_{Z},

(S.571)

we can write

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{\bar{H}}^{m}(O_{Z_{0}})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\bar{s}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\max(s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{m})=\bar{s}}\left\|{\rm ad}_{\bar{H}_{\infty,s_{m}}}\cdots{\rm ad}_{\bar{H}_{\infty,s_{2}}}{\rm ad}_{\bar{H}_{\infty,s_{1}}}(O_{Z_{0}})\right\|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bar{s}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\max(s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{m})=\bar{s}}\left\|{\rm ad}_{\bar{H}_{R_{\bar{s}},s_{m}}}\cdots{\rm ad}_{\bar{H}_{R_{\bar{s}},s_{2}}}{\rm ad}_{\bar{H}_{R_{\bar{s}},s_{1}}}(O_{Z_{0}})\right\|

(S.572)

with
R_{\bar{s}}=m\ell_{\bar{s}}+\ell=m2^{\bar{s}}+\ell
, where
{\rm ad}_{H_{\infty,s_{m}}}\cdots{\rm ad}_{H_{\infty,s_{2}}}{\rm ad}_{H_{\infty,s_{1}}}(O_{Z_{0}})
is supported on
[-R_{\bar{s}},R_{\bar{s}}]
from the definition (
S.569
) and
Z_{0}\subseteq[-\ell,\ell]
.

Using Lemma 3 in Ref.
[
67
]
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{\bar{H}_{R_{\bar{s}},s_{m}}}\cdots{\rm ad}_{\bar{H}_{R_{\bar{s}},s_{2}}}{\rm ad}_{\bar{H}_{R_{\bar{s}},s_{1}}}(O_{Z_{0}})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq(2k)^{m}m!\prod_{j=1}^{m}\left[2\bar{g}_{R_{\bar{s}}}\eta_{1}(\ell_{s_{j}-1}+1)\bar{J}(\ell_{s_{j}-1})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(2^{\chi+2}k\eta_{1}\right)^{m}m!\left(\bar{g}_{m+\ell}+g_{1}\bar{s}^{\chi}\right)^{m}\prod_{j=1}^{m}2^{-\bar{\alpha}(s_{j}-1)},

(S.573)

where, from the conditions (
S.346
) and (
S.375
), we use

\displaystyle(\ell_{s-1}+1)\bar{J}(\ell_{s-1})\leq\frac{1}{\ell_{s-1}^{\bar{\alpha}}+1}\leq 2^{-\bar{\alpha}(s-1)},

(S.574)

and

\displaystyle\bar{g}_{R_{\bar{s}}}=g_{0}+g_{1}\log^{\chi}\left(m2^{\bar{s}}+\ell+1\right)

\displaystyle\leq g_{0}+g_{1}\left[\log\left(m+\ell+1\right)+\bar{s}\right]^{\chi}

\displaystyle\leq g_{0}+g_{1}\left[2\log\left(m+\ell+1\right)\right]^{\chi}+g_{1}\left(2\bar{s}\right)^{\chi}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\chi}\left(\bar{g}_{m+\ell}+g_{1}\bar{s}^{\chi}\right).

(S.575)

The above inequality is derived from
m2^{\bar{s}}+\ell+1\leq(m+\ell+1)e^{\bar{s}}
and
(y_{1}+y_{2})^{a}\leq(2y_{1})^{a}+(2y_{2})^{a}
for
y_{1},y_{2},a>0
.

We then estimate the summation of

\displaystyle\sum_{\max(s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{m})=\bar{s}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}2^{-\bar{\alpha}(s_{j}-1)}

\displaystyle\leq m2^{-\bar{\alpha}(\bar{s}-1)}\left(\sum_{s=0}^{\infty}2^{-\bar{\alpha}(s-1)}\right)^{m-1}

\displaystyle=m\left(\frac{2^{\bar{\alpha}}}{1-2^{-\bar{\alpha}}}\right)^{m-1}2^{-\bar{\alpha}(\bar{s}-1)}.

(S.576)

By combining the inequalities (
S.573
) and (
S.576
) with the upper bound (
S.572
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{\bar{H}}^{m}(O_{Z_{0}})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left(2^{\chi+2}k\eta_{1}\right)^{m}m!\sum_{\bar{s}=0}^{\infty}\left(\bar{g}_{m+\ell}+g_{1}\bar{s}^{\chi}\right)^{m}m\left(\frac{2^{\bar{\alpha}}}{1-2^{-\bar{\alpha}}}\right)^{m-1}2^{-\bar{\alpha}(\bar{s}-1)}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{2^{\chi+\bar{\alpha}+3}4k\eta_{1}m}{1-2^{-\bar{\alpha}}}\right)^{m}\sum_{\bar{s}=0}^{\infty}\left[\bar{g}^{m}_{m+\ell}+\left(g_{1}\bar{s}^{\chi}\right)^{m}\right]2^{-\bar{\alpha}(\bar{s}-1)},

(S.577)

where we use
(y_{1}+y_{2})^{a}\leq(2y_{1})^{a}+(2y_{2})^{a}
and
m\cdot m!\leq m^{m}
for
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

Finally, to upper-bound the summation, we use Supplemental Lemma 1 of Ref.
[
37
]
to derive
*13
*13

*13

The inequality

\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}m^{D}e^{-cm}\leq e^{-c}+e^{-c}2^{D}D![\max(1,1/c)]^{D+1}
was derived.

\displaystyle\sum_{\bar{s}=1}^{\infty}2^{-\bar{\alpha}(\bar{s}-1)}\bar{s}^{\chi m}

\displaystyle\leq 1+2^{\chi m}(\chi m)!\left(\frac{1}{\bar{\alpha}\log(2)}+1\right)^{\chi m+1}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{1+\chi m}(\chi m)^{\chi m}\left(\frac{2+\bar{\alpha}}{\bar{\alpha}}\right)^{\chi m+1}=2\left(\frac{2+\bar{\alpha}}{\bar{\alpha}}\right)\left[\frac{2\chi m(2+\bar{\alpha})}{\bar{\alpha}}\right]^{\chi m}.

(S.578)

Also, we have
\sum_{\bar{s}=0}^{\infty}2^{-\bar{\alpha}(\bar{s}-1)}=2^{\bar{\alpha}}\left(1-2^{-\bar{\alpha}}\right)^{-1}
, and hence the main inequality is derived as follows:

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{\bar{H}}^{m}(O_{Z_{0}})\right\|\leq\left(\tilde{c}_{1}m\right)^{m}\left\{2^{\bar{\alpha}}\bar{g}^{m}_{m+\ell}+g_{1}^{m}\cdot 2\left(\frac{2+\bar{\alpha}}{\bar{\alpha}}\right)\left[\frac{2\chi m(2+\bar{\alpha})}{\bar{\alpha}}\right]^{\chi m}\right\}

\displaystyle=2^{\bar{\alpha}}\left(\tilde{c}_{1}m\bar{g}_{m+\ell}\right)^{m}+2\left(\frac{2+\bar{\alpha}}{\bar{\alpha}}\right)\left(\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}m^{\chi+1}\right)^{m},

(S.579)

where we have defined
\tilde{c}_{1}
and
\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}
as

\tilde{c}_{1}=\left(2^{\chi+\bar{\alpha}+3}4k\eta_{1}\right)/\left(1-2^{-\bar{\alpha}}\right)
and

\tilde{c}_{2}=\tilde{c}_{1}\left[2\chi(2+\bar{\alpha})/\bar{\alpha}\right]^{\chi}
, respectively.
This completes the proof of Lemma
37
.

\square

Supplementary Note 10.2.2
Proof of Proposition
39

Let us define
T_{0,m}
as

\displaystyle T_{0,m}=\tilde{c}_{1}\bar{g}_{m+ql}+\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}m^{\chi}.\quad

(S.580)

Then, using
T_{0,m}
and
\tilde{c}_{3}
in Eq. (
S.567
),
we simplify the inequality (
S.565
) in Corollary
38
as

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}^{m}(h_{s_{0},s_{0}+1})\right\|\leq c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}\bar{g}_{ql}\left(T_{0,m}m\right)^{m}\quad\textrm{for}\quad\forall s_{0}\in[0,q].

(S.581)

In the following, we aim to prove the inequality (
S.566
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}^{m}(O_{s})\right\|\leq(T_{m}m)^{m}\left\|O_{s}\right\|=\bigl[(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})m\bar{g}_{m+ql}+\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}m^{\chi+1}\bigr]^{m}\left\|O_{s}\right\|,

(S.582)

by using the induction method.

For
m=1
, we prove the inequality (
S.566
) from the inequality (
S.381
) as follows:

\displaystyle\|{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}(O_{s})\|=\|[h_{s-1,s}+h_{s,s+1},O_{s}]\|\leq 2\|h_{s-1,s}+h_{s,s+1}\|\cdot\left\|O_{s}\right\|\leq 4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}\left\|O_{s}\right\|\leq T_{1}\left\|O_{s}\right\|,

(S.583)

where we use
[h_{s},O_{s}]=0
and
\tilde{c}_{3}\geq 2
from the definition in (
S.567
).

We then assume the target inequality up to
m=m_{0}
and consider
m=m_{0}+1
:

\displaystyle\|{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}^{m_{0}+1}(O_{s})\|

\displaystyle=\|{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}^{m_{0}}\left([h_{s-1,s}+h_{s,s+1},O_{s}]\right)\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}=m_{0}}\binom{m_{0}}{m_{1}}\left\|{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}^{m_{1}}(O_{s})\right\|\cdot\left\|{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}^{m_{2}}\left(h_{s-1,s}+h_{s,s+1}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|O_{s}\right\|\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}=m_{0}}\binom{m_{0}}{m_{1}}(T_{m_{1}}m_{1})^{m_{1}}\left\|{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}^{m_{2}}\left(h_{s-1,s}+h_{s,s+1}\right)\right\|.

(S.584)

By using the inequality (
S.581
) for
{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}^{m_{2}}(h_{s-1,s}+h_{s,s+1})
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}^{m_{2}}(h_{s-1,s}+h_{s,s+1})\right\|\leq 2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}\bar{g}_{ql}\left(T_{0,m_{2}}m_{2}\right)^{m_{2}}.

(S.585)

By applying the inequality (
S.585
) to (
S.584
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}^{m_{0}+1}(O_{s})\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|O_{s}\|\cdot 2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}\bar{g}_{ql}\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}=m_{0}}\binom{m_{0}}{m_{1}}(T_{m_{1}}m_{1})^{m_{1}}\left(T_{0,m_{2}}m_{2}\right)^{m_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|O_{s}\|\cdot 4c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}\bar{g}_{ql}(T_{m_{0}}m_{0})^{m_{0}}\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}=m_{0}}\binom{m_{0}}{m_{1}}\frac{m_{1}^{m_{1}}m_{2}^{m_{2}}}{(m_{1}+m_{2})^{m_{1}+m_{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\|O_{s}\|\cdot 2T_{m_{0}}(T_{m_{0}}m_{0})^{m_{0}}(m_{0}+1)\leq\left[T_{m_{0}+1}(m_{0}+1)\right]^{m_{0}+1}\|O_{s}\|,

(S.586)

where, in the second inequality, we use
T_{0,m}\leq T_{m}
and the monotonic increase of
T_{m}
for
m
,
in the third inequality, we use Lemma
40
below and
2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}\bar{g}_{ql}\leq T_{m_{0}}
from the definition in (
S.566
), and in the last inequality, we use

m_{0}^{m_{0}}=(m_{0}+1)^{m_{0}}(1+1/m_{0})^{-m_{0}}\leq(m_{0}+1)^{m_{0}}/2
.
This completes the proof of the main inequality (
S.566
) in Proposition
39
.
\square

Supplementary Note 10.2.3
Lemma on the binomial coefficient

Lemma 40
.

Let us adopt
0^{0}=1
. Then, for an arbitrary pair of integers
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\binom{m_{1}+m_{2}}{m_{1}}\leq\frac{(m_{1}+m_{2})^{m_{1}+m_{2}}}{m_{1}^{m_{1}}m_{2}^{m_{2}}}.

(S.587)

Proof of Lemma
40
.

Let us set
m_{1}\leq m_{2}
without loss of generality.
We can trivially obtain the main inequality for the case of
m_{1}=0
, and hence, we only need to consider the case of
m_{1}\geq 1
.
For the proof, we utilize the following inequality in Ref.
[
68
]
:

\displaystyle\sqrt{2\pi n}\left(\frac{n}{e}\right)^{n}e^{1/(12n+1)}<n!<\sqrt{2\pi n}\left(\frac{n}{e}\right)^{n}e^{1/(12n)}.

(S.588)

Using it, we have

\displaystyle m_{1}^{m_{1}}<\frac{e^{-1/(12m_{1}+1)}}{\sqrt{2\pi m_{1}}}e^{m_{1}}m_{1}!,\quad m_{2}^{m_{2}}<\frac{e^{-1/(12m_{2}+1)}}{\sqrt{2\pi m_{2}}}e^{m_{2}}m_{2}!,

(S.589)

and

\displaystyle(m_{1}+m_{2})^{m_{1}+m_{2}}>\frac{e^{-1/(12m_{1}+12m_{2})}}{\sqrt{2\pi(m_{1}+m_{2})}}e^{m_{1}+m_{2}}(m_{1}+m_{2})!.

(S.590)

By using them, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{(m_{1}+m_{2})^{m_{1}+m_{2}}}{m_{1}^{m_{1}}m_{2}^{m_{2}}}>e^{1/(12m_{1}+1)+1/(12m_{2}+1)-1/(12m_{1}+12m_{2})}\frac{2\pi\sqrt{m_{1}m_{2}}}{\sqrt{2\pi(m_{1}+m_{2})}}\binom{m_{1}+m_{2}}{m_{1}}.

(S.591)

For
1\leq m_{1}\leq m_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{12m_{1}+1}+\frac{1}{12m_{2}+1}-\frac{1}{12m_{1}+12m_{2}}\geq\frac{2}{12m_{1}+1}-\frac{1}{12m_{1}+12}\geq 0,

(S.592)

and

\displaystyle\frac{m_{1}+m_{2}}{2\pi}=\frac{m_{1}}{2\pi}\left(1+\frac{m_{2}}{m_{1}}\right)\leq\frac{m_{1}}{2\pi}(1+m_{2})\leq\frac{2}{2\pi}m_{1}m_{2}\leq m_{1}m_{2}.

(S.593)

By applying the inequalities (
S.592
) and (
S.593
) to (
S.591
), we prove the main inequality (
S.587
).
This completes the proof.
\square

Supplementary Note 10.3
Subtheorem on the energy distribution

Based on Proposition
39
, we aim to upper bound

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I}O_{s}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\right\|,

(S.594)

where
I\subset\mathbb{R}
is arbitrary taken.
For our purpose, we consider

\displaystyle I=[E+\theta,E+\theta+T_{0}),

(S.595)

where
T_{0}
is defined from
T_{m}
in (
S.566
), i.e.,
T_{0}=T_{m=0}=(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})\bar{g}_{ql}
.

We prove the following subtheorem:

Subtheorem 2
.

For an arbitrary
E
and the region
I
as in Eq. (
S.595
), we prove the following upper bound:

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I}O_{s}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\right\|\leq e\left\|O_{s}\right\|\exp\left\{-\min\left[\frac{\theta}{4e\tilde{T}_{\theta/T_{0}}},\left(\frac{\theta}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right]\right\},

(S.596)

where
O_{s}
is arbitrarily chosen such that
[O_{s},h_{s}]=0
, and the quantity
\tilde{T}_{z}
(
z>0
) is defined as

\displaystyle\tilde{T}_{z}:=(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})\bar{g}_{z+ql}\longrightarrow T_{m}=\tilde{T}_{m}+\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}m^{\chi}.

(S.597)

Supplementary Note 10.3.1
Proof of Subtheorem
2

As has been shown, the multi-commutator
{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}^{m}(O_{s})
has a norm that scales as
m^{m(1+\chi)}
, and hence we cannot rely on the original argument
[
51
]
, which utilizes the imaginary time evolution as in (
S.559
).
To obtain a meaningful bound, we adopt analyses similar to those in Supplementary Lemma 39 of Ref.
[
60
]
.

We first derive

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I}O_{s}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\right\|\leq\frac{\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I}O_{s}(E+\theta-H_{\rm t})^{m}\right\|}{\theta^{m}},

(S.598)

where the inequality is derived from
\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}(E+\theta-H_{\rm t})^{m}\succeq\theta^{m}
as follows:

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I}O_{s}(E+\theta-H_{\rm t})^{m}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I}O_{s}(E+\theta-H_{\rm t})^{m}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I}O_{s}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\cdot\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}(E+\theta-H_{\rm t})^{m}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\theta^{m}\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I}O_{s}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\right\|.

(S.599)

Note that for arbitrary positive semi-definite operators
O
and
O^{\prime}
that satisfy
O\succeq O^{\prime}\succeq 0
and
[O,O^{\prime}]=0
, we have
\left\|O_{0}O\right\|\geq\left\|O_{0}O^{\prime}\right\|
, where the operator
O_{0}
can be arbitrarily chosen.

We next calculate

\displaystyle(E+\theta-H_{\rm t})^{m}O_{s}\Pi_{{\rm t},I}=\sum_{s=0}^{m}\binom{m}{s}{\rm ad}_{E+\theta-H_{\rm t}}^{s}(O_{s})\left(E+\theta-H_{\rm t}\right)^{m-s}\Pi_{{\rm t},I},

(S.600)

which yields

\displaystyle\left\|(E+\theta-H_{\rm t})^{m}O_{s}\Pi_{{\rm t},I}\right\|\leq\sum_{s=0}^{m}\binom{m}{s}\left\|{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}^{s}(O_{s})\right\|T_{0}^{m-s},

(S.601)

where we use
\left\|\left(E+\theta-H_{\rm t}\right)^{m-s}\Pi_{{\rm t},I}\right\|\leq T_{0}^{m-s}
and
{\rm ad}_{E+\theta-H_{\rm t}}=-{\rm ad}_{H_{\rm t}}
.

By applying the upper bound (
S.566
) to (
S.601
), we prove

\displaystyle\left\|(E+\theta-H_{\rm t})^{m}O_{s}\Pi_{{\rm t},I}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|O_{s}\right\|\sum_{s=0}^{m}\binom{m}{s}(T_{s}s)^{s}T_{0}^{m-s}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|O_{s}\right\|(T_{m}m)^{m}\sum_{s=0}^{m}\binom{m}{s}=\left\|O_{s}\right\|(2T_{m}m)^{m},

(S.602)

where we use
T_{s}\leq T_{m}
for
0\leq s\leq m
.
By combining the inequality (
S.602
) with the upper bound (
S.598
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I}O_{s}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\right\|\leq\left\|O_{s}\right\|\left(\frac{2T_{m}m}{\theta}\right)^{m}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|O_{s}\right\|\left[\frac{2m(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})\bar{g}_{\theta/T_{0}+ql}+2\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}m^{1+\chi}}{\theta}\right]^{m}

\displaystyle=\left\|O_{s}\right\|\left(\frac{2m\tilde{T}_{\theta/T_{0}}+2\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}m^{1+\chi}}{\theta}\right)^{m},

(S.603)

where the explicit form of
T_{m}
was given in Eq. (
S.566
), and we choose
m
such that
m\leq\theta/T_{0}
, which gives

\bar{g}_{m+ql}\leq\bar{g}_{\theta/T_{0}+ql}
since
\bar{g}_{z}
monotonically increases with
z
, and in the last inequality, we use the notation of
\tilde{T}_{z}
in Eq. (
S.597
).

We here set
m
so that it satisfies both of the conditions

\displaystyle\frac{2m\tilde{T}_{\theta/T_{0}}}{\theta}\leq\frac{1}{2e},\quad\frac{2\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}m^{1+\chi}}{\theta}\leq\frac{1}{2e},

\displaystyle\longrightarrow m=\min\left[\left\lfloor\frac{\theta}{4e\tilde{T}_{\theta/T_{0}}}\right\rfloor,\left\lfloor\left(\frac{\theta}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right\rfloor\right],

(S.604)

which reduces the upper bound (
S.603
) to

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I}O_{s}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\right\|\leq\left\|O_{s}\right\|\left(\frac{2T_{m}m}{\theta}\right)^{m}\leq e\left\|O_{s}\right\|\exp\left\{-\min\left[\frac{\theta}{4e\tilde{T}_{\theta/T_{0}}},\left(\frac{\theta}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right]\right\}.

(S.605)

We thus prove the main inequality (
S.596
).
This completes the proof of Subtheorem
2
.
\square

Supplementary Note 10.4
Proof of the Main Theorem

In this section, we will prove Theorem
4
.
For this purpose, we give a general theorem on the energy distribution of a subsystem
B_{s}\subset\Lambda
, given that the total energy lies within the interval
(-\infty,E]
. We will show the sub-exponential decay of the energy distribution of
h_{s}
(see
Supplementary Note 10.5
for the proof).

Theorem 5
.

Let us set
E_{s,0}
and
E_{{\rm t},0}
being the ground-state energies of
h_{s}
and
H_{\rm t}
.
Then, for
\tau_{s}=\tau+E_{s,0}
,
the overlap between the projections
\Pi^{(s)}_{>\tau_{s}}
and
\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}
is bounded from above as:

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi^{(s)}_{>\tau_{s}}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\right\|\leq{\mathcal{E}}_{\tau+E_{{\rm t},0}-4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}-E-T_{0}},

(S.606)

where
{\mathcal{E}}_{y}
for
\forall y>0
is defined as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{y}=\mu_{1}\exp\left(-\frac{y}{8e\tilde{T}_{y/T_{0}}}\right)+\mu_{2}\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{y}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right],

(S.607)

and
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
are
\mathcal{O}(1)
constants as follows:

\displaystyle\mu_{1}:=1+e\int_{0}^{\infty}(z+2)\exp\left[-\frac{1}{8e}\cdot\frac{z}{1+\log^{\chi}(z+3)}\right]dz,

(S.608)

\displaystyle\mu_{2}:=1+e\int_{0}^{\infty}(z+2)\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1}}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}}z\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right]dz.

(S.609)

The proof of the main Theorem
4

comes from the combination of the above theorem and Lemma
34
.
For the convenience of readers, we show the theorem again:

Theorem
4
.

Let us define
\varepsilon_{1}
and
\varepsilon_{2}
as

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{1}=2q{\mathcal{E}}_{\tau-4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}-T_{0}},\quad\varepsilon_{2}=\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{1-\varepsilon_{1}}2q\left(\tau+2c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}\right),

(S.610)

where
{\mathcal{E}}_{y}
(
y\geq 0
) is a sub-exponentially decaying function defined in Eq. (
S.607
), and
T_{0}
is defined by
T_{m=0}
using
T_{m}
in Eq. (
S.566
).
Then, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\||\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\tilde{\Omega}_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\varepsilon_{2}}{\Delta_{\rm t}}},

(S.611)

and the spectral gap
\tilde{\Delta}
is lower-bounded by

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}_{\rm t}\geq\Delta_{\rm t}-3\varepsilon_{2}.

(S.612)

Proof of Theorem
4
.

We here use Lemma
34
with

\displaystyle H\to H_{\rm t},\quad|\Omega\rangle\to|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle,\quad\Pi\to\bigotimes_{s=0}^{q+1}\Pi^{(s)}_{\leq\tau_{s}}:=\tilde{\Pi}.

(S.613)

In the following, we aim to treat the parameter
\epsilon_{H_{\rm t}}
in Lemma
34
:

\displaystyle\epsilon_{H_{\rm t}}=\frac{\left\langle\Omega_{\rm t}\left|\tilde{\Pi}(H_{\rm t}-E_{{\rm t},0})\tilde{\Pi}\right|\Omega_{\rm t}\right\rangle}{\left\|\tilde{\Pi}|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|^{2}}.

(S.614)

We here introduce an additional parameter
\epsilon_{\Omega_{\rm t}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\Omega_{\rm t}}=\left\|\tilde{\Pi}^{\rm c}|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|=\left\|\tilde{\Pi}|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|.

(S.615)

Our task is to derive the inequality of

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\Omega_{\rm t}}\leq 2q{\mathcal{E}}_{\tau-4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}-T_{0}}=:\varepsilon_{1},

(S.616)

and

\displaystyle\epsilon_{H_{\rm t}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{1-\varepsilon_{1}}2q\left(\tau+2c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}\right)=:\varepsilon_{2}.

(S.617)

After proving these inequalities, we prove the main inequalities of (
S.611
)
and (
S.612
) from (
S.490
) and (
S.491
), respectively.

From the same inequality as (
S.522
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\Pi}|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|\leq\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}\left\|\Pi^{(s)}_{\leq\tau_{s}}|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|=\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}\left\|\Pi^{(s)}_{>\tau_{s}}|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|=\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}\left\|\Pi^{(s)}_{>\tau_{s}}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E_{{\rm t},0}}\right\|.

(S.618)

By applying the inequality (
S.606
) with
E\to E_{{\rm t},0}
to the RHS, we have

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\Omega_{\rm t}}=\left\|\tilde{\Pi}|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle-|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|\leq\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}{\mathcal{E}}_{\tau-4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}-T_{0}}=(q+2){\mathcal{E}}_{\tau-4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}-T_{0}}\leq 2q{\mathcal{E}}_{\tau-4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}-T_{0}},

(S.619)

where we use the condition
q\geq 4
to get
q+2\leq 2q
.
We thus prove the first target inequality (
S.616
) for
\epsilon_{\Omega_{\rm t}}
.

We next consider
\epsilon_{H_{\rm t}}
as

\displaystyle\epsilon_{H_{\rm t}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\tilde{\Pi}|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|^{-2}\left\langle\Omega_{\rm t}\left|\tilde{\Pi}(H_{\rm t}-E_{{\rm t},0})\left(\tilde{\Pi}-1\right)\right|\Omega_{\rm t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\tilde{\Pi}|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|^{-1}\left\|(H_{\rm t}-E_{{\rm t},0})\tilde{\Pi}\right\|\cdot\left\|\left(1-\tilde{\Pi}\right)|\Omega_{\rm t}\rangle\right\|

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon_{\Omega_{\rm t}}}{1-\epsilon_{\Omega_{\rm t}}}\left\|(H_{\rm t}-E_{{\rm t},0})\tilde{\Pi}\right\|\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{1-\varepsilon_{1}}\left\|(H_{\rm t}-E_{{\rm t},0})\tilde{\Pi}\right\|,

(S.620)

where we use the monotonic increase of
x/(1-x)
for
0\leq x<1
.

We next estimate the norm of
\left\|(H_{\rm t}-E_{{\rm t},0})\tilde{\Pi}\right\|
. Because of
[h_{s},\tilde{\Pi}]=0
, we have

\displaystyle(H_{\rm t}-E_{{\rm t},0})\tilde{\Pi}=\tilde{\Pi}\left(\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}h_{s}-E_{{\rm t},0}\right)\tilde{\Pi}+\sum_{s=0}^{q}h_{s,s+1}\tilde{\Pi},

(S.621)

which yields an upper bound of

\displaystyle\left\|(H_{\rm t}-E_{{\rm t},0})\tilde{\Pi}\right\|\leq\left\|\tilde{\Pi}\left(\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}h_{s}-E_{{\rm t},0}\right)\tilde{\Pi}\right\|+\sum_{s=0}^{q}\left\|h_{s,s+1}\right\|.

(S.622)

We now aim to upper-bound

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\Pi}\left(\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}h_{s}-E_{{\rm t},0}\right)\tilde{\Pi}\right\|=\sup_{\psi}\left[\left|\langle\psi|\tilde{\Pi}\left(\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}h_{s}-E_{{\rm t},0}\right)\tilde{\Pi}|\psi\rangle\right|\right].

(S.623)

In the following, we separately consider the expectations for
\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}h_{s}-E_{{\rm t},0}
and
E_{{\rm t},0}-\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}h_{s}
.
For an arbitrary quantum state
|\psi\rangle
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\psi|H_{\rm t}|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}\langle\psi|h_{s}|\psi\rangle+\sum_{s=0}^{q}\langle\psi|h_{s,s+1}|\psi\rangle\geq\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}E_{s,0}-\sum_{s=0}^{q}\left\|h_{s,s+1}\right\|,

(S.624)

and hence

\displaystyle E_{{\rm t},0}=\inf_{|\psi\rangle}\left(\langle\psi|H_{\rm t}|\psi\rangle\right)\geq\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}E_{s,0}-\sum_{s=0}^{q}\left\|h_{s,s+1}\right\|,

(S.625)

From the inequality (
S.625
) and
\left\|\tilde{\Pi}h_{s}\tilde{\Pi}\right\|\leq\tau_{s}
for
\forall s
,
we can derive

\displaystyle\langle\psi|\tilde{\Pi}\left(\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}h_{s}-E_{{\rm t},0}\right)\tilde{\Pi}|\psi\rangle\leq\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}\left(\tau_{s}-E_{s,0}\right)+\sum_{s=0}^{q}\left\|h_{s,s+1}\right\|=(q+2)\tau+\sum_{s=0}^{q}\left\|h_{s,s+1}\right\|,

(S.626)

where we use
\tau_{s}=\tau+E_{s,0}
for
s\in[0,q+1]
.

On the other hand, the ground energy
E_{{\rm t},0}
is larger than the energy of the product state
|\psi_{0}\rangle=\bigotimes_{s=0}^{q+1}|E_{s,0}\rangle
:

\displaystyle E_{{\rm t},0}\leq\langle\psi_{0}|H_{\rm t}|\psi_{0}\rangle\leq\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}E_{s,0}+\sum_{s=0}^{q}\left\|h_{s,s+1}\right\|,

(S.627)

which yields

\displaystyle\langle\psi|\tilde{\Pi}\left(E_{{\rm t},0}-\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}h_{s}\right)\tilde{\Pi}|\psi\rangle\leq\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}\langle\psi|\tilde{\Pi}\left(E_{s,0}-h_{s}\right)\tilde{\Pi}|\psi\rangle+\sum_{s=0}^{q}\left\|h_{s,s+1}\right\|\leq\sum_{s=0}^{q}\left\|h_{s,s+1}\right\|.

(S.628)

From the inequalities (
S.626
) and (
S.628
), we prove

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\Pi}\left(\sum_{s=0}^{q+1}h_{s}-E_{{\rm t},0}\right)\tilde{\Pi}\right\|\leq(q+2)\tau+\sum_{s=0}^{q}\left\|h_{s,s+1}\right\|.

(S.629)

This reduces the inequality (
S.622
) to

\displaystyle\left\|(H_{\rm t}-E_{{\rm t},0})\tilde{\Pi}\right\|\leq(q+2)\tau+2\sum_{s=0}^{q}\left\|h_{s,s+1}\right\|\leq 2q\left(\tau+2c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}\right),

(S.630)

where, in the second inequality, we use the upper bound (
S.381
), i.e.,
\|h_{s,s+1}\|\leq c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}
.
Therefore, by combining the above inequality with (
S.620
), we derive the second target inequality (
S.617
).
This completes the proof of Theorem
4
.
\square

Supplementary Note 10.5
Proof of Theorem
5

We follow the approach from the proof of Proposition 8 of Ref.
[
29
]
. First, consider an arbitrary normalized quantum state
|\psi\rangle
, and define the quantum state
|\phi\rangle
as follows:

\displaystyle|\phi\rangle:=\Pi_{>\tau_{s}}^{(s)}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}|\psi\rangle.

(S.631)

Note that the state
|\phi\rangle
may not be normalized. The norm of
\Pi_{>\tau_{s}}^{(s)}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}
is then given by

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{>\tau_{s}}^{(s)}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\right\|=\sup_{|\psi\rangle}\left\|\phi\right\|,

(S.632)

where
\left\|\phi\right\|
denotes the norm of
|\phi\rangle
.

We aim to prove the following inequality (see
Supplementary Note 10.5.1
for the proof):

\displaystyle\left\|\phi\right\|\leq{\mathcal{E}}_{\langle H_{\rm t}\rangle_{\phi}-E-T_{0}},

(S.633)

where we use the definition (
S.607
) for
{\mathcal{E}}_{y}
and

\displaystyle\langle H_{\rm t}\rangle_{\phi}=\frac{\langle\phi|H_{\rm t}|\phi\rangle}{\|\phi\|^{2}}.

(S.634)

To obtain an explicit upper bound for
\left\|\phi\right\|^{2}
from Eq. (
S.633
), we must calculate a lower bound for
\langle H_{\rm t}\rangle_{\phi}
:

\displaystyle\langle H_{\rm t}\rangle_{\phi}=\langle h_{s}\rangle_{\phi}+\langle(h_{s,s+1}+h_{s-1,s})\rangle_{\phi}+\langle\delta H_{s}\rangle_{\phi},

(S.635)

where
\delta H_{s}:=H_{\rm t}-h_{s}-h_{s,s+1}-h_{s-1,s}
, acting on the sites
\Lambda_{s}:=\Lambda\setminus B_{s}
. Denote the ground state and the ground-state energy of
\delta H_{s}
by
|E_{\Lambda_{s},0}\rangle
and
E_{\Lambda_{s},0}
, respectively.

From the definition of
|\phi\rangle
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\langle h_{s}\rangle_{\phi}=\frac{1}{\left\|\phi\right\|^{2}}\langle\psi|\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\Pi_{>\tau_{s}}^{(s)}h_{s}\Pi_{>\tau_{s}}^{(s)}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}|\psi\rangle>\tau_{s},

\displaystyle\langle(h_{s,s+1}+h_{s-1,s})\rangle_{\phi}\geq-(\|h_{s,s+1}\|+\left\|h_{s-1,s}\right\|)\geq-2c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql},

\displaystyle\langle\delta H_{s}\rangle_{\phi}\geq E_{\Lambda_{s},0}\geq E_{{\rm t},0}-E_{s,0}-2c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql},

(S.636)

where, in the second inequality, we use (
S.381
), and third inequality follows from:

\displaystyle E_{{\rm t},0}\leq\langle E_{s,0}|\otimes\langle E_{\Lambda_{s},0}|H_{\rm t}|E_{s,0}\rangle\otimes|E_{\Lambda_{s},0}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq E_{s,0}+E_{\Lambda_{s},0}+\|h_{s,s+1}\|+\left\|h_{s-1,s}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq E_{s,0}+E_{\Lambda_{s},0}+2c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}.

(S.637)

Thus, the inequalities in Eq. (
S.636
) yield the following lower bound for
\langle H_{\rm t}\rangle_{\phi}
from Eq. (
S.635
):

\displaystyle\langle H_{\rm t}\rangle_{\phi}\geq E_{{\rm t},0}+\tau_{s}-E_{s,0}-4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}=E_{{\rm t},0}+\tau-4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql},

(S.638)

where we use
\tau_{s}=E_{s,0}+\tau
.

Therefore, by combining the upper bound (
S.633
) and the lower-bound (
S.638
) with Eq. (
S.632
), we prove

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{>\tau_{s}}^{(s)}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\right\|\leq\left\|\phi\right\|\leq{\mathcal{E}}_{\tau+E_{{\rm t},0}-4c_{0}\bar{g}_{ql}-E-T_{0}},

(S.639)

where we use the monotonic decreasing of
{\mathcal{E}}_{y}
from Eq. (
S.607
).
This completes the proof of Theorem
5
.
\square

Supplementary Note 10.5.1
Proof of the inequality (
S.633
)

We now prove inequality (
S.633
).
In the following, we adopt the decomposition of

\displaystyle[E+y,\infty)=\bigcup_{j=0}^{\infty}I_{j},\quad I_{j}=[E+y+T_{0}j,E+y+T_{0}j+T_{0}),

(S.640)

where the parameter
y
will be determined later.
Note that
T_{0}
is defined from
T_{m}
in (
S.566
), i.e.,
T_{0}=T_{m=0}=(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})\bar{g}_{ql}
.
Then, we begin with the following equality:

\displaystyle\langle\phi|H_{\rm t}|\phi\rangle=\langle\phi|\Pi_{{\rm t},<E+y}H_{\rm t}\Pi_{{\rm t},<E+y}|\phi\rangle+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\langle\phi|\Pi_{{\rm t},I_{j}}H_{\rm t}\Pi_{{\rm t},I_{j}}|\phi\rangle.

(S.641)

We further calculate the upper bound of
\langle\phi|H_{\rm t}|\phi\rangle
as

\displaystyle\langle\phi|H_{\rm t}|\phi\rangle

\displaystyle\leq(E+y)\|\Pi_{{\rm t},<E+y}|\phi\rangle\|^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(E+y+jT_{0}+T_{0})\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I_{j}}|\phi\rangle\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=(E+y)\left(\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},<E}|\phi\rangle\right\|^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I_{j}}|\phi\rangle\right\|^{2}\right)+T_{0}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(j+1)\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I_{j}}|\phi\rangle\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=(E+y)\left\|\phi\right\|^{2}+T_{0}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(j+1)\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I_{j}}|\phi\rangle\right\|^{2}.

(S.642)

By letting

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathcal{E}}}_{y}:=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(j+1)\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I_{j}}|\phi\rangle\right\|^{2},

(S.643)

we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\phi\right\|^{2}\cdot\langle H_{\rm t}\rangle_{\phi}=\langle\phi|H_{\rm t}|\phi\rangle

\displaystyle\leq(E+y)\left\|\phi\right\|^{2}+T_{0}\bar{{\mathcal{E}}}_{y},

(S.644)

where we use the definition in Eq. (
S.634
) to get
\langle\phi|H_{\rm t}|\phi\rangle=\left\|\phi\right\|^{2}\cdot\langle H_{\rm t}\rangle_{\phi}
.
Then, the above inequality reduces to:

\displaystyle\left\|\phi\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{T_{0}}{\langle H_{\rm t}\rangle_{\phi}-E-y}\bar{{\mathcal{E}}}_{y}.

(S.645)

By choosing
y
such that
\langle H_{\rm t}\rangle_{\phi}-E-y=T_{0}
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\left\|\phi\right\|\leq\sqrt{\bar{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\langle H_{\rm t}\rangle_{\phi}-E-T_{0}}}.

(S.646)

Therefore, the remaining task is to estimate
\bar{{\mathcal{E}}}_{y}
in Eq. (
S.643
).

From the definition of
|\phi\rangle
in Eq. (
S.631
), we first derive:

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I_{j}}|\phi\rangle\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I_{j}}\Pi_{>\tau_{s}}^{(s)}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}|\psi\rangle\right\|^{2}\leq\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I_{j}}\Pi_{>\tau_{s}}^{(s)}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\right\|^{2}.

(S.647)

To obtain an upper bound for
\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I_{j}}\Pi_{>\tau_{s}}^{(s)}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\right\|^{2}
, we use the upper bound (
S.596
) in Subtheorem
2
with
\theta\to y+T_{0}j
, which yields

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I_{j}}\Pi_{>\tau_{s}}^{(s)}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\right\|\leq e\exp\left\{-\min\left[\frac{y+T_{0}j}{4e\tilde{T}_{y/T_{0}+j}},\left(\frac{y+T_{0}j}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right]\right\}.

(S.648)

Here, the RHS of the above inequality monotonically decreases with
y
.

Then, using the definition (
S.607
), we estimate the summation in Eq. (
S.643
) to derive
\sqrt{\bar{{\mathcal{E}}}_{y}}\leq{\mathcal{E}}_{y}
as follows:

\displaystyle\sqrt{\bar{{\mathcal{E}}}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(j+1)^{1/2}\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I_{j}}|\phi\rangle\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(j+1)\left\|\Pi_{{\rm t},I_{j}}\Pi_{>\tau_{s}}^{(s)}\Pi_{{\rm t},\leq E}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq e\exp\left\{-\min\left[\frac{y}{4e\tilde{T}_{y/T_{0}}},\left(\frac{y}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+e\int_{0}^{\infty}(\theta+2)\left\{\exp\left[-\frac{y+T_{0}\theta}{4e\tilde{T}_{y/T_{0}+\theta}}\right]+\exp\left[-\left(\frac{y+T_{0}\theta}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right]\right\}d\theta

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{1}\exp\left(-\frac{y}{8e\tilde{T}_{y/T_{0}}}\right)+\mu_{2}\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{y}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right]={\mathcal{E}}_{y},

(S.649)

where the constants
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
were defined in Eqs. (
S.608
) and (
S.609
), and in the last inequality, we use
T_{0}=(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})\bar{g}_{ql}
from Eq. (
S.566
),
\tilde{T}_{z}=(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})\bar{g}_{z+ql}
from Eq. (
S.597
), and
\bar{g}_{ql}\geq\bar{g}_{2}\geq g_{0}+g_{1}\geq g_{1}
[see Eq. (
S.373
)] to derive

\displaystyle e\int_{0}^{\infty}(\theta+2)\exp\left(-\frac{y+T_{0}\theta}{4e\tilde{T}_{y/T_{0}+\theta}}\right)d\theta

\displaystyle\leq e\exp\left(-\frac{y}{8e\tilde{T}_{y/T_{0}}}\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}(\theta+2)\exp\left[-\frac{(y/T_{0}+\theta)T_{0}}{8e\tilde{T}_{y/T_{0}+\theta}}\right]d\theta

\displaystyle\leq e\exp\left(-\frac{y}{8e\tilde{T}_{y/T_{0}}}\right)\int_{y/T_{0}}^{\infty}(z+2-y/T_{0})\exp\left[-\frac{(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})\bar{g}_{ql}z}{8e\bar{g}_{z+ql}(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})}\right]dz

\displaystyle\leq e^{2}\exp\left(-\frac{y}{8e\tilde{T}_{y/T_{0}}}\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}(z+2)\exp\left[-\frac{\bar{g}_{ql}}{8e\bar{g}_{z+ql}}z\right]dz=(\mu_{1}-1)\exp\left(-\frac{y}{8e\tilde{T}_{y/T_{0}}}\right),

(S.650)

and

\displaystyle e\int_{0}^{\infty}(\theta+2)\exp\left[-\left(\frac{y+T_{0}\theta}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right]d\theta

\displaystyle\leq e\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{y}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right]\int_{0}^{\infty}(\theta+2)\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{(y/T_{0}+\theta)T_{0}}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right]d\theta

\displaystyle\leq e\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{y}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right]\int_{y/T_{0}}^{\infty}\left(z+2-y/T_{0}\right)\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{z}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\cdot(2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1})\bar{g}_{ql}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right]dz

\displaystyle\leq e\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{y}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right]\int_{0}^{\infty}(z+2)\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{2c_{0}\tilde{c}_{3}+\tilde{c}_{1}}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}}z\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right]dz

\displaystyle=(\mu_{2}-1)\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{y}{4e\tilde{c}_{2}g_{1}}\right)^{1/(1+\chi)}\right].

(S.651)

Note that to reach the definition (
S.608
) of
\mu_{1}
in (
S.650
), we use
ql\geq 2
to obtain the upper bound of

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{g}_{ql}}{\bar{g}_{z+ql}}=\frac{g_{0}+g_{1}\log^{\chi}(3)}{g_{0}+g_{1}\log^{\chi}(z+3)}\geq\frac{1}{1+\log^{\chi}(z+3)}.

(S.652)

Finally, by combining the upper bounds (
S.649
) with the inequality (
S.646
), we prove
the target inequality (
S.633
).
